The cooperative module runs a Philippine cooperative inside G-SMaRT Integrated Cooperative System: its members, share capital, savings and time deposits, loans, the tellers' cash, the year-end interest on share capital and patronage refund, and the reports to the Cooperative Development Authority (CDA). It is not a separate program. Every peso it receives, pays out or moves is posted as a voucher to the same general ledger as the rest of the books, so the Trial Balance, the General Ledger and the financial statements always include it.

In the sidebar the module has six menus:

  • Cooperative: the members, their statements and certificates, cooperative journal vouchers, the opening balances import, and the set-up pages (profile and policies, branches, lists, passbook types, numbering, document wording).
  • Savings & Deposits: savings and share capital accounts, deposits and withdrawals, transfers, interest posting, passbook printing, time deposits, payroll deductions and check clearing.
  • Loans: applications, credit investigation, approval, release, collection, the loan accounts, documents, adjustments, waivers, renewal, restructuring and write-off, and the penalty and accrual runs.
  • Teller: the teller's shift and cash count, official receipts and member disbursements.
  • Year-end Distribution: the allocation of the net surplus, interest on share capital (ISC) and patronage refund (PR).
  • CDA Compliance: officers and committees, compliance items, the regulatory calendar and the CAPR data.

Each menu also lists its reports.

How the cooperative books work

  • One ledger. An official receipt, a disbursement, a loan release or a cooperative journal voucher is a transaction of the general ledger. At the same moment the program writes the member's own ledger: the savings or share capital account, the time deposit, the loan. Each member ledger line carries the number of the voucher that posted it. The Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation proves that the member ledgers add up to the control accounts.
  • Members in the general ledger. Every member is also a name in Employees & Other Names. The ledger lines of a member carry that name, so the existing Subsidiary Ledger report shows a member's lines under each account.
  • Branches are classes. Each branch has a class, and every voucher of the branch carries it, so the Income Statement and Balance Sheet by Class give branch statements. A user tied to a branch sees and works on that branch's members only.
  • Nothing posted is edited or deleted. A wrong voucher is voided with a reason, which reverses the general ledger and the member ledgers together, or corrected with an adjusting entry. The program refuses a void that would leave the books wrong (for example the release of a loan that already has payments) and says why.
  • Approvals. What needs a second person (large receipts, withdrawals above a limit, share capital withdrawals, loan approvals, waivers, distribution batches, settlement of a member who has left) asks for a supervisor's username and password on the same form, unless the user has the Approve right on the page. The approver is recorded on the voucher and in the audit trail.
  • Posted once. Each form can be posted only once: a double click, a refresh or the browser's back button cannot post a receipt twice. Interest, accrual and penalty runs are posted once for each period or date.
  • Printed documents are controlled. The first print of a promissory note, a certificate or a voucher is the original; each later print is a numbered copy that asks for a reason. Every print is listed in the Document Print History.
  • Exact amounts. Money is computed as exact decimals and rounded to the centavo where a document shows it, so the totals of a report always equal the sum of its lines.

Setting up the cooperative

An administrator sets the cooperative up once, in this order. Each page is described in this chapter.

  1. Cooperative Profile & Policies: the registered name, CDA registration number, signatories, the membership, teller and loan policies, the alert windows and the year-end allocation percentages. Check the percentages against your bylaws.
  2. Module Default Accounts, Cooperative group: check that each cooperative account points at the right account of your chart.
  3. Branches: the head office and each branch, with its class and official receipt prefix.
  4. User Accounts: give each teller and branch user their branch and cash drawer, and give users their roles (see the next section).
  5. Document Numbering, Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists and Passbook Types.
  6. Savings & Deposit Products: at least one share capital product, the savings products and the time deposit products, with their rates. Then Savings Transaction Codes if you need codes of your own.
  7. Loan Charges, Loan Products and the Loan Approval Matrix.
  8. Document Templates, if your documents use wording of your own.
  9. Report Field Mappings, the Regulatory Calendar and the officers.
  10. When moving from another system: Opening Balances Import of the members, then the savings and share capital balances, the time deposits and the loans, all as of the cut-off date.
  11. Run the Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation. It must show no difference before you go live.

Roles and rights

The module adds eight roles. Like every role, they can be changed on User Role Configuration.

Role What it can do
General ManagerEvery cooperative, savings, loan, teller, distribution and compliance page with every right, including Approve, and the main accounting reports.
BookkeeperCooperative vouchers, interest and penalty runs, the distribution pages and every cooperative report, without the profile, branches, numbering, products and approval matrix set-up.
Loan OfficerLoan applications, credit investigation, release, collection, loan accounts, documents, waiver requests and renewals, and the loan reports.
Credit CommitteeReviews and approves loan applications and waivers, and reads the loan reports.
TellerThe teller shift, official receipts, member disbursements, deposits and withdrawals, loan collection, passbook printing, check clearing and time deposits.
Membership OfficerMember records and statements, savings account opening and the member reports.
CollectorLoan collection and the collection, due and aging reports.
Branch ManagerEverything of the branch with the Approve right, except the set-up pages.

The special permissions of a role (on User Role Configuration) set the limits: "Cooperative: max savings withdrawal without supervisor approval", "Cooperative: max loan release amount without supervisor approval", "Cooperative: can waive loan interest and penalties, and adjust loan balances", "Cooperative: can record back-dated member transactions", "Cooperative: can override the payment allocation (penalty, interest, principal) on a loan collection" and "Cooperative: sees every branch regardless of the branch on the user account".

Daily, monthly and year-end work

When What Where
Every business dayOpen the shift with the beginning cash; post the savings transactions, loan collections and receipts (all on the Teller menu); close the shift with the cash count and print the cash count sheet and the Daily Collection & Disbursement Summary.Teller Shift & Cash Count, Savings Transaction Center, Loan Collection, Official Receipt, Daily Collection & Disbursement Summary
Every business dayClear the checks the bank has cleared; record returned checks.Check Clearing
Month end (or the product's interest period)Post savings interest for each product; run dormancy.Post Savings Interest
Month endAccrue time deposit interest; settle the placements that matured.Time Deposit Center
Month endPost loan penalties, accrue loan interest and refresh the loan statuses.Post Loan Penalties & Interest Accrual
Month endCheck that the member ledgers agree with the general ledger.Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation
After the fiscal yearAllocate the net surplus as the General Assembly approved, then compute, approve and post the ISC and PR batches.Net Surplus Allocation, Interest on Share Capital and Patronage Refund
As scheduledPrepare the CAPR data, file the compliance requirements and watch the calendar.CAPR Data Preparation & Export, Compliance Tracking, Regulatory Calendar

The runs are made by a user on their pages; nothing runs by itself at night. Each run is safe to repeat for the same date or period: it adds nothing the second time. The Home page and the alert count in the top bar list what needs attention: amortizations due, past due loans, maturing time deposits, accounts about to turn dormant, documents expiring, approvals waiting, regulatory deadlines and teller shifts left open.

Member Center

The Member Center is the list of the cooperative's members and the place to start any work on a member. The counts at the top show how many members are in each membership status; click a count to list those members. Click a member's name to open Member Information.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Member Center.

The counts are Active members, Applicants, Suspended, Resigned, Terminated and Deceased. Under them are the filters:

Field What to enter / what it means
Find Part of the member's name or mobile number, the first digits of the member number, or the whole passbook number, TIN, savings account number or loan number.
Status One membership status, or Any status.
Member type One member type, or Any type.
Branch One branch, or All branches. Shown only to users who see every branch, when the cooperative has more than one branch.
Show only Everyone, On hold, With an alert, No share capital yet (the share capital balance is zero) or Subscription not fully paid (the subscribed capital is more than the paid-up capital).

Click Show to apply the filters, or Clear to remove them.

The member list

Column What it shows
Member The name (click it to open the member), with the member number and the city under it. A red circle with a slash means the member is on hold; a warning triangle means the member has an alert note. Point at the mark to read the reason or the note.
Type, Status, Branch The member type, the membership status and the branch of the member.
Member since The membership date.
Share capital The balance of the member's share capital accounts that are not closed.
Savings The balance of the member's ordinary and special savings accounts. Time deposits are not included.
Loans The principal balance of the member's open loans (current, past due or restructured). "past due" in red under the amount means one of the loans is past due.

The list is in name order, 50 members to a page; the page numbers and the number of members found are under the list.

The buttons at the top are New member, with an arrow beside it for Joint account and Institution (they need the Create right on Member Information), and Masterlist report, which opens the Member Master List report.

Import Member (beside them, with the Create right on Member Information) brings the members of the former system in from a CSV file: see Importing members.

A user whose account is tied to a branch sees only the members of that branch, in the list and in the counts.

To find a member:

  1. Open Members & Others > Member Center.
  2. Type part of the name, or a member, passbook, account or loan number, in Find.
  3. Choose a Status, Member type or Show only if you want fewer members.
  4. Click Show, then click the member's name.

To list the members on hold: choose On hold in Show only and click Show.

Importing members (Import Member)

For the move from the former system, the individual members are brought in from a CSV file instead of being typed one by one. Each member is created with the status Active. Import the members first, then their savings (Import Savings) and time deposits (Import Time Deposit).

Where to find it: Import Member on the title bar of the Member Center. Reading and checking a file needs the Create right on Member Information; uploading it needs the Approve right there.

The file. Download the CSV template gives the columns with two sample rows; open it in Excel, replace the samples with the members, and save it as CSV (Comma delimited). The first line holds the column names (the template's, or the headings in words, for example "Last Name" or "Date of Birth"); one member per line after it. The card The columns on the page lists every column with what goes in it; the required ones are in bold.

Column What to enter
Member no. The member number of the former system. Blank: the next member number of this system.
Last name, First name, Middle name Required. Write - in Middle name for a member who has none.
Suffix Jr., Sr., III and the like.
Date of birth Required. YYYY-MM-DD or MM/DD/YYYY.
Sex, Civil status M or F (or Male, Female); Single, Married, Widowed, Separated or Annulled.
Member type Required. The code or the name, as on Member Types, for example REG or Regular.
Membership date Required. YYYY-MM-DD or MM/DD/YYYY.
Street The house number and street of the residence address.
Barangay, Municipality / City, Province Required: the residence address.
Branch The branch code or name. Blank: the branch of the user.
Mobile, E-mail, TIN As on Member Information.

The full template. Full template (every detail), beside Download the CSV template, gives a template with a column for every other detail of Member Information as well: 53 more columns. Which of them to fill is up to you: keep only the columns you have and delete the others; a column left out, or a cell left blank, is simply not filled in. The page lists them under The full template: 53 more columns at the end of The columns (click it to open the list), by group:

Group Columns
Personal Salutation (Mr., Ms., Mrs., Dr., Engr., Atty. or Hon.), place of birth, nationality (blank: Filipino), passbook no.
Residence, provincial and office address The house / unit no. and ZIP code of the residence; the house no., street / barangay, municipality / city, province and ZIP code of the provincial and of the office address.
Education and work Highest education, year completed, course, occupation, employment (Employed, Self-employed, Business owner, OFW, Retired, Student, Unemployed, Other), employer or business name, its contact and address, other organizations.
Membership and grouping PMES attended (date), subscribed shares (a whole number), par value per share (blank: the par value of Cooperative Profile & Policies), cluster, segmentation, account officer (the username or the name of a user), account class, account group, address sub-group and savings group (each a name on its list of Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists).
Contacts and notes The alert shown at the counter, notes, landline (saved with the mobile number on the member's contact numbers).
Spouse Last, first and middle name, date of birth, contact no. A spouse needs the last and the first name.
Beneficiaries Two beneficiaries, each with the name, relationship, date of birth, contact no. and share % (0 to 100; the shares add up to 100 at most). A beneficiary's details need the name.

The review grid shows only the columns of the full template that the file fills. A row's line says which details it brings, for example "Becomes Pascual, Ramil S., member no. 200111, Active, with details on education and work; membership and grouping; spouse; beneficiaries".

The review. Choose the file and click Read the file for review. Nothing is saved yet: every row of the file is shown in a grid, one row per member, and each row is checked at once. A row with a problem is in red: a red ground and a red bar on its left, a red dot, and its messages in red under it, for example "Enter the barangay.", "The date of birth "1979-13-40" is not a date.", "Member type "XYZ" is not on Member Types." or "... is already a member: 200101." (the same member number, or the same name and date of birth, already on file or earlier in the file). The cells a message names are outlined in red. A row without a problem has a green dot and shows what it will become. The bar above the grid counts the rows and those with a problem; Find in the rows narrows the grid, and Only rows with a problem leaves those alone. The line under the grid says how many rows are still in red.

  • Correct a row by typing in its cells. The row is checked again by itself a moment after you stop typing (Enter in a cell checks it at once): while it is being checked it is marked in blue, then it turns red again with the problems left, or loses its red when there is none. The columns beyond the first ones are under … at the end of the row; they open by themselves when a message names one of them.
  • Remove a row with × at the end of it. Remove the rows with a problem, above the grid, removes every row in red at once. Put back the last brings back the last row, or the last rows, removed.
  • Check again checks every row as it now is. Start over leaves the file and opens the page again.
  • Upload (for example Upload 5 members) asks to confirm, checks every row once more and saves them all, or none if a row still has a problem.

The same review, with its rows in red and its automatic check, is on Import Savings, Import Time Deposit, the batch deposits and withdrawals, Import Loans and Import Items.

On a phone each row of the grid shows as a card with its cells under one another, and the buttons stay at the bottom of the screen.

Note

The upload is all or nothing: one row with a problem saves nothing, and the grid comes back with the rows as you left them. The import is kept on the audit trail with the file name and the number of rows. An upload is sent once: refreshing the page, going Back or a second tab does not upload the same rows again.

Member Information

Member Information holds everything about one member: the profile from the membership application, the accounts and loans, the documents on file, the membership status and its history, holds and notes. A new member is also entered here.

Where to find it: click a member's name on the Member Center, or click New member there. Member names on the cooperative reports also open this page. The page is not in the sidebar menu.

Note

Every member belongs to a party of the Party Master, which also holds the member's customer and supplier records. On a new member, Already on file as a customer or supplier chooses a customer, supplier or other name already in the program, so the person joins as a member on that same party instead of being recorded twice; a new member that looks like one on file is stopped with the possible matches. A saved member shows its Party ID beside Kind of member. The member's date of birth, TIN, contact numbers and addresses are masked for users without the permission to see confidential member details. See Member Information in chapter 14.

The top of the page

The buttons in the title bar of a saved member are:

  • Statement: the member's Member Statement of Account.
  • Receive payment: an official receipt for the member on Official Receipt (Member Collection).
  • Loan application: a new loan application for the member.
  • Open account: opens a new account for the member on the Savings Account Center.
  • ID card: prints the member's ID card (see The ID card).
  • Member Center: back to the list.

Each button appears only for users with the right it needs.

Under the title is the member panel: the photo (or the initials), the name, the member number, the member type, the status and the branch, an On hold line and the alert note when there are any, and the balances Share capital, Savings, Time deposits and Loans (with the number of open loans).

Note

The Loans figure on the member panel is the principal, interest and penalty still to be paid on the open loans. The Loans column of the Member Center shows the principal only, so the two can differ.

The page has four tabs: Profile, Accounts and loans, Documents and History and notes. A new member shows the profile form only.

The Profile tab

The profile of an individual is in parts: Personal, Family and beneficiaries, Financial, Membership and Loan and savings grouping, then Water Defaults, Customer Defaults and Vendor Defaults for the users who may change those (see below). A joint account or an institution has its own form, in the format of the former program: Joint/Institution Details and Membership Information, then the defaults tabs (see Joint accounts and institutions). All are saved together with Save member (or Add member for a new member). Users without the Edit right see the profile but cannot change it.

Personal

Field What to enter / what it means
Kind of member Individual, Joint account or Institutional. Choosing Joint account or Institutional changes the form to the joint account and institution form (see Joint accounts and institutions); choosing Individual brings back the individual's.
Member no. (blank = next number) Shown for a new member only. Leave it blank to get the next member number (the grey text shows it). A number you type must not be used by another member. The member number cannot be changed after the member is saved.
Salutation, Last name, First name, Middle name, Suffix Last name and first name are required for an individual. The program writes the member's name as last name, first name and middle initial, for example "Mendoza, Ramon" or "Lopez, Teresa R.".
Date of birth, Place of birth, Sex, Civil status, Nationality The date of birth is used by the duplicate check and for the age on the Member Master List. Nationality starts as Filipino.
TIN, Mobile, E-mail The e-mail address must be valid. When Mobile is left blank, the first mobile number under Contact numbers fills it.
Residence address House / unit no., Street, Barangay, City / municipality, Province and ZIP. The address prints on the statement and the ID card.
Provincial and office addresses Click the line to open the provincial and office address boxes.
Contact numbers One row per number: Type (Mobile, Landline, Office, Other), Country, Area, Number and Primary. Add a number adds a row; the x at the end of a row removes it.
Education and work Highest education, Year completed, Course, Occupation, Employment (Employed, Self-employed, Business owner, OFW, Retired, Student, Unemployed, Other), Employer / business name, Employer contact, Employer address and Other organizations.
Photo (JPG / PNG), Specimen signature A picture file (JPG, PNG, GIF or WEBP) of up to 8 MB. A new file replaces the one on file. The photo shows on the member panel and the ID card; the signature on file shows beside these boxes and prints on the ID card.

Family and beneficiaries

Field What to enter / what it means
Spouse, children and other dependents One row per person: Kind (Spouse, Child, Other dependent), Last name, First name, Middle name, Relationship, Birth date, Contact, and Dependent ticked for a person who depends on the member. Click Add a person for another row.
Beneficiaries Name, Relationship, Birth date, Contact, Address, Share % and Primary. The shares of all the beneficiaries must not add up to more than 100 %. Click Add a beneficiary for another row.
Relatives who are members The Member and the Relationship.

Financial

Field What to enter / what it means
Income, Expenses, Other cash outlay (amortizations elsewhere) One row per item: the Item, the Member (monthly) amount and the Spouse (monthly) amount.
Monthly income, Expenses and outlay, Net disposable income Shown after the member is saved: the income less the expenses and the other cash outlay. The net disposable income is checked against the NDI requirement of the loan products.
Assets and liabilities Kind (Asset, Liability), Description and Amount.

Membership

Field What to enter / what it means
Branch Required. The branch the member belongs to. A user tied to a branch can file members under that branch only.
Member type Required. The types are set up on Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists.
PMES attended The date the member attended the Pre-Membership Education Seminar.
Membership date The date the member was admitted. When it is blank, activating the member fills it with the activation date.
Status Shown for a saved member, with the date of the last change. The status is changed on the History and notes tab.
Subscribed shares, Par value per share The number of shares the member subscribed and the par value of one share. The par value starts at the Par value per share of Cooperative Profile & Policies. The subscribed capital is the shares times the par value.
Subscribed / paid-up capital Shown for a saved member: the subscribed capital, the paid-up capital (the balance of the share capital accounts) and the percentage paid.
Cluster, Segmentation, Account officer The cluster and segmentation (from Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists) and the user who looks after the member.
Attendance (general assembly, PMES, seminars) One row per meeting: Meeting (General assembly, PMES, Seminar / training, District meeting), Date, Attended and Remarks. Click Add a meeting for another row.

Loan and savings grouping

Field What to enter / what it means
Account class, Account group, Address sub-group, Savings group Groupings the cooperative keeps for its members. The account classes come from the Account classes list.
Passbook number The member's passbook number. Get passbook number puts the next number of Document Numbering (Passbook) that no member has in the box; it is taken from the series when the member is saved (if another member was given it in the meantime, the next free one is given and the message says so). A number typed by hand must not belong to another member. Find on the Member Center finds the member by it.
Alert shown at the counter A short warning for the staff, for example "Verify ID before withdrawals". It shows on the member panel of the teller, savings and loan pages, and as a mark on the Member Center.
Notes Any other remarks about the member.

One name: Water Defaults, Customer Defaults and Vendor Defaults

A member is entered once, on this form, and is the same name in every line of business: the member is the cooperative's customer (sales, the POS, statements) and its supplier (bills, checks, cash vouchers) on records kept with the member, and the member's water service is set here too. There is no separate customer, supplier or water consumer form for a member. The name, TIN, e-mail and contact numbers of those records follow the member when the member is saved; a member renamed is renamed everywhere.

Water Defaults (users who may change Water Billing's water-service accounts):

Field What it means
Zone, Address sub-group, Sequence The zone, the address sub-group and the meter reading sequence of the member's water service.
Rate code / classification, Pipe size The tariff of the service. Both are needed, and the two must have a complete tariff on Water Meter Rates.
Connection date The date the connection started.
Disconnection date A date makes the connection Inactive: the water-service account is marked disconnected in Water Billing, with the date.
Connection status Active or Inactive. To reconnect, clear the disconnection date and choose Active.
Water-service account these defaults are for Shown when the member has more than one water-service account: the one the defaults are applied to.

Saving the member applies the defaults to the member's water-service account; a member without one gets a new account when a rate code and a pipe size are given (the Create right on Water Billing's water-service accounts is needed). The table under the boxes lists the member's water-service accounts with their rate code, zone, connection and status. A disconnection made in Water Billing shows here too.

Customer Defaults (users with the Edit right on the Customer Center): Payment terms, Credit limit (0 = none), Tax code, Preferred payment method, Customer type, Sales representative, Billing group (batch invoicing), Taxes this customer withholds and Price level. A credit limit change by a user who may not approve on the Customer Center needs the Reason for a credit limit change.

Price level decides what the member pays for the items, as the price level of a customer does: on invoices, sales receipts, sales orders, estimates, statement charges and batch invoices (with each customer's price level) the item's price comes from it when the member is chosen, and at the POS when the member is the sale's customer (the POS shows the level under the customer). A level with a price of its own for an item gives that price; otherwise its percentage is applied to the item's sales price. Left blank, the member pays the members' price level of the line of business (Business Units), or of the cooperative, when one is set (the blank choice then reads Members' price level), else the standard prices (Standard). Only an active level can be chosen; a level the member already has keeps working until it is changed, shown with "(inactive)" if it was made inactive since (an inactive level gives the standard prices). Water bills are not affected: a material on a water bill is priced from its bill code.

Vendor Defaults (users with the Edit right on the Vendor Center): Payment terms, Credit limit with vendor, Our account no. with this vendor, Default expense account, Tax code and Withholding taxes.

On the name lists of every form the member appears once, by member type ("Regular Member: Santos, Maria"). An entry with the member is recorded on the record of the role the member plays: on Accounts Receivable (an invoice, a receivable line of a journal entry) the member's customer record, on Accounts Payable or as the payee of a check or cash voucher its supplier record, elsewhere its member name. The Subsidiary Ledger, the schedules and Find show the member once, with all of them. On the Customer Center and the Vendor Center the member's record carries a line that leads to these tabs.

Joint accounts and institutions

A joint account (two or more people saving together, for example a husband, a wife and a son) and an institution (a hospital, a school, an association) are filed on the form of the former program. Choosing Joint account or Institutional as the Kind of member shows it. It has no Family and beneficiaries, Financial or Loan and savings grouping: those are for an individual. On the right of the form the status and the type are shown in large letters, for example NEW and ASSOCIATE - JOINT for a joint account not saved yet, with the Member ID once it is saved.

Joint/Institution Details

Field What to enter / what it means
Member ID (blank = next number) Shown for a new member only, as for an individual.
Membership name Required. The name the account goes by, which becomes the member's name: for a joint account the holders, for example "Santos, Ana or Ramon or Miguel"; for an institution its registered name. When a save is refused (an e-mail that is not valid, a passbook number already given), the form comes back with everything as typed.
Authorized representatives One row per person who may act for the account: Authorized representative name, Position / relation (Wife, Husband, Son; President, Vice President, Treasurer, Secretary) and Member (if a member). Click Add a representative for another row.
Residence address House / bldg no., Postal code, Street, Barangay, City / municipality and Province. Water Billing uses this address for the member's water-service account.
Office / business address House / bldg no., Postal code, Street, City / municipality and Province.
Contact details The Contact list, one row per number: Type, Country, Area code, Number and Primary; then the E-mail address and the TIN (optional). The first mobile of the list is the member's mobile (Find on the Member Center finds the member by it).
Specimen signatures of the representatives A picture of the representatives' signatures (JPG, PNG, GIF or WEBP, up to 8 MB). The signatures on file show beside the box and print on the ID card, as an individual's specimen signature does.

Membership Information

Field What to enter / what it means
Passbook number As for an individual: type it, or click Get passbook number for the next number of the series that no member has.
Membership date, PMES date The date the member was admitted and the date of the pre-membership seminar.
Type Required. The member types of the kind (Associate - Joint and Regular - Joint for a joint account, Associate - Institutional for an institution on a new installation; see Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists). A new joint account or institution starts with the first one. A member type of another kind is refused.
Status New for a member not saved yet (it is saved as an Applicant); for a saved member the status and the date of the last change. The status is changed on the History and notes tab.
Cluster, Segmentation From Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists.
Branch, Subscribed shares, Par value per share, Account officer As for an individual: the branch is required.
Alert shown at the counter, Notes As for an individual, for example "Two of the representatives sign every withdrawal".

To add a joint account or an institution:

  1. Open Members & Others > Member Center, click the arrow beside New member and choose Joint account or Institution.
  2. On Joint/Institution Details, type the Membership name and the authorized representatives with their position or relation, then the addresses and the contact details.
  3. On Membership Information, click Get passbook number (or type the number), check the Type, type the Membership date and the PMES date, and choose the Branch.
  4. Click Add member. It is saved as an Applicant, as an individual is.

Adding a member

To add a member:

  1. Open Members & Others > Member Center and click New member. For a joint account or an institution, click the arrow beside it and choose Joint account or Institution.
  2. On Personal, leave Member no. (blank = next number) blank, or type the number from the membership application.
  3. Type the name, date of birth and the other personal details, the residence address and the contact numbers. Choose the photo and the specimen signature if you have them.
  4. On Family and beneficiaries, enter the dependents and the beneficiaries with their shares.
  5. On Membership, choose the Branch and the Member type, and type the PMES attended date and the Subscribed shares.
  6. Fill the other parts as needed.
  7. Click Add member, or Add member & New to start another one.

The member is saved as an Applicant, and the program says: "Member added as an applicant. Activate the membership when the Board has approved it and the share capital is paid." The member's name is also added to Employees & Other Names as a member, so that ledger lines can carry it. To make the member active, see Membership status.

The duplicate check. When another member already has the same name and the same date of birth, the program does not save and says, for example: "A member with the same name and date of birth already exists (member no. 100006). Tick "This is a different person" to save anyway." Open that member first to make sure it is not the same person. If it is a different person, tick This is a different person beside the save button and save again.

To change a member's profile: open the member, change the fields on any part of the Profile tab and click Save member. The changes are kept in the audit trail of the member record.

The Accounts and loans tab

The tab lists, with links to each account and loan:

  • Savings, share capital and time deposits: Account, Product, Opened, Balance, Available (what can be withdrawn now) and Status, with "restricted" beside a restricted account. Closed accounts are greyed at the end.
  • Loans: Loan, Product, Released, Maturity, Principal, Balance (principal), Penalty and Status.
  • Loan applications in process, when there are any.
  • Loans guaranteed as co-maker: the open loans of other members that this member signed for, with the borrower and the principal balance.

The Documents tab

The documents on file for the member, such as IDs, the membership application form or the PMES certificate, with a scan or photo of each. The list shows Document (the kind and the notes), No., Issued, Expires and File. An expiry date that has passed is shown in red with "(expired)". Click the file name to open the file in a new tab.

To add a document:

  1. Open the member and click the Documents tab.
  2. In Add a document, choose the Kind. The kinds are the Member document types list of Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists.
  3. Type the Document / ID no. and the Issued and Expires dates.
  4. Choose the File (picture or PDF, up to 8 MB) and type any Notes.
  5. Click Save document.

To remove a document from the list: click Remove on its row, type the reason and confirm. Removing needs the Delete/Void right on Member Information; the removal and the document's details are kept in the audit trail.

Documents that expire within the number of days set in Documents, collateral and insurance expiring within (days) on Cooperative Profile & Policies, or have already expired, are counted in the cooperative alerts on the Home page (for active members and applicants).

The photo, the signature and the document files can be opened by users who may open Member Information, the Member Center, the Official Receipt page, the Savings Account Center or the Loan Application Center, for the members of their own branch. Each time a document file is opened, it is recorded in the audit trail.

Membership status

The History and notes tab shows the Membership status history: each status, the status it came from, the reason and reference, the date and the user. The Change status button needs the Approve right on the Member Center.

A status can only move as follows:

From May change to
Applicant Active, Terminated
Active Suspended, Resigned, Terminated, Deceased
Suspended Active, Resigned, Terminated, Deceased
Resigned Active
Terminated Active
Deceased No further change

The Change the membership status form:

Field What to enter / what it means
New status Only the statuses the member may move to are listed.
Effective date The date the change takes effect. It starts as today.
Reason (kept in the member's history and the audit trail) Required. For example "Approved by the Board (membership committee)".
Board resolution / reference The resolution or other reference, for example BR-2024-01.
Activate even if the paid-up share capital is below the minimum Tick to activate a member whose paid-up share capital is below the minimum. The waiver is noted in the audit trail.
Allow resignation / termination with open loans (to be offset) Tick to let a member with open loans resign or be terminated, so the loans can be offset against the member's deposits and share capital.

What the program checks and does:

  • Active. The paid-up share capital must reach the minimum: the higher of Minimum paid-up share capital to activate a member on Cooperative Profile & Policies and the Minimum share capital to activate of the member type. A blank membership date is filled with the effective date, and a hold on the member is released.
  • Resigned and Terminated. A member with open loans is refused unless the open-loans box is ticked.
  • Deceased. The date of death is recorded and the member is put on hold with the reason "Deceased: claims by the beneficiaries only".
  • For a resigned, terminated or deceased member, the member's name in Employees & Other Names is made inactive; it is active again when the member is reactivated.
  • Deposits, loan releases and time deposit placements are refused for a suspended, resigned, terminated or deceased member. An applicant can already pay at the teller (for example the membership fee and the first share capital); a loan release or a time deposit needs an active member.
  • A member who has resigned, been terminated or died is settled: the teller still receives loan payments, the loans can be paid from the member's own deposits on Loan Collection (paid by savings, with a supervisor's approval), and the share capital and deposits are paid out in full with Settle the accounts on the member's page (Member Disbursement), always with a supervisor's approval. A deceased member's claim is paid to the beneficiary or the estate named in Received by.

To activate an applicant:

  1. Receive the membership fee and the share capital on Official Receipt (Member Collection).
  2. Open the member and click the History and notes tab.
  3. Click Change status.
  4. Choose Active in New status and check the Effective date.
  5. Type the Reason and the Board resolution / reference.
  6. Click Change status. The program says "Status changed to Active."

Hold and alert note

A hold stops the member's transactions until it is released: "No deposit, withdrawal, payment or loan can be recorded." The Hold card on the History and notes tab shows "Not on hold." or the reason of the hold.

To put a member on hold: click Put on hold on the Hold card, type the Reason (required) and click Save. To release the hold: click Release the hold, type the reason if you want, and click Save. Both need the Edit right on Member Information and are recorded in the audit trail.

An alert note does not stop anything: it is a warning shown to the staff. Type it in Alert shown at the counter on Loan and savings grouping and save the member.

Notes and the audit trail

To add a note: on the History and notes tab, choose the Kind (Note, Phone call, Visit, Complaint), type the Note and click Add. The Notes list shows each note with its kind, date and time, and the user. Notes cannot be changed or removed.

Audit trail of the member record shows the last 40 entries for the member: when, by whom, and what was done (created, changed, status changes, holds, notes, photo and signature uploads, prints) with the reason.

Good to know

  • Adding a member needs the Create right on Member Information; changing the profile, holds, notes and adding documents need the Edit right; removing a document needs Delete/Void; changing the status needs Approve on the Member Center.
  • "Choose the member type."
  • "Enter the last name and first name." or, for a joint account or an institution, "Enter the name of the joint account or institution."
  • "The e-mail address is not valid."
  • "Member number 100013 is already used." Leave the number blank to get the next one.
  • "The beneficiaries' shares add up to more than 100 %" with the total. Correct the Share % of the beneficiaries.
  • "You may only file members under your own branch."
  • "The file is larger than 8 MB." and "Upload a JPG, PNG, GIF or WEBP picture." for the photo and the signature.
  • "Paid-up share capital is 500.00, below the minimum of 1,000.00 for activation. Collect the share capital first, or tick the waiver (recorded in the audit trail)."
  • "The member still has 1 open loan(s) with a balance of 7,334.40. Settle them, or tick "allow with open loans" (for offsetting against deposits and share capital)."
  • "Enter the reason for the hold."
  • A user tied to a branch cannot open a member of another branch.

Member Statement of Account

The statement of account shows a member's savings, share capital and time deposit accounts with their movements in a period, the loans with their payments, and the interest on share capital and patronage refund received. From the same page you print the statement, the member's ID card, the share capital certificate and a certificate of balances.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Member Statement of Account, or the Statement button on Member Information.

Showing a statement

Field What to enter / what it means
Member Type part of the name, the member number, or an account or loan number, and pick the member from the list. The statement shows at once.
From, To The period. It starts as January 1 of this year to today. Change the dates and click Show.

The statement shows the member panel, then:

  • One table per savings, share capital and time deposit account, headed by the product and the account number: Date, Code (the transaction code), Particulars, OR / ref., Withdrawals, Deposits and the running Balance. The first line is the Balance forwarded on the From date and the last line the balance on the To date. A deposit still clearing is marked "clearing". A time deposit account also lists its certificates (amount, rate, term, maturity value and status). A closed account with no balance and no movement is left out.
  • One table per loan, headed by the product, the loan number, the amount released, the maturity date and the status: Date, Entry (with the OR number of a payment), Principal, Interest, Penalty and Principal balance. The release shows in brackets. The last line gives what is outstanding on the loan now: principal, interest and penalties. A paid, renewed, cancelled or written-off loan with no movement in the period is left out.
  • Interest on share capital and patronage refund, when the member received any from a posted distribution whose period ends within the statement period: Batch, Kind, Period ending, Basis and Amount.

The buttons Print, Share certificate, ID card and Certificate of balances appear when a member is shown and you have the Print/Export right on this page.

Printing the statement

Click Print. The statement opens as a printed page and the print dialog opens. It has the cooperative's heading, the title STATEMENT OF ACCOUNT, today's date and the period; the member's name, number and address; the accounts, loans and distributions as on the screen; the note "Please examine this statement and report any discrepancy to the cooperative within fifteen (15) days; otherwise it will be considered correct."; and the signature lines Prepared by (your name) and Certified correct (the bookkeeper named on Cooperative Profile & Policies). Each print of a statement is recorded in the audit trail.

The ID card

Click ID card (here or on Member Information). The card prints in two parts. The front has the cooperative's name, "Member identification", the photo (or the initials), the member's name, member number, member type and membership date, and the branch. The back has the date of birth, the address, the mobile number, "In case of emergency, contact the cooperative office.", the line for the member's signature with the specimen signature on file, and the name of the General Manager.

The ID card is a controlled document: the first print is the original, and a reprint needs a reason and prints "Reissued (copy 1)" and so on (see Reprints of controlled documents).

The share capital certificate

Click Share certificate. The certificate prints with the heading SHARE CAPITAL CERTIFICATE, its number and today's date; the wording of the Share capital certificate template (see Document Templates), which states the member, the number of fully paid-up shares, the par value and the amount in words and figures as of today; the amount in large figures; and the signature lines Chairperson, Secretary / Treasurer and General Manager with the names from Cooperative Profile & Policies.

  • The number of shares is the paid-up share capital divided by the par value per share, in whole shares. The par value is the member's own; if the member has none, the Par value per share of the share capital product; if that is blank too, the one on Cooperative Profile & Policies.
  • The first print of a member's certificate takes the next Share capital certificate number of Document Numbering (SC-000001 in the sample data). Every later print for the same member keeps that number.
  • A reprint needs a reason and is marked "COPY 1 (reprint)" and so on. It shows the paid-up share capital on the day it is printed.

Certificate of balances

A certificate of balances certifies a member's share capital and deposit balances, and the loans, on a date the member asks for, for example for a visa or a loan with another lender. Each certificate is a new document with its own number.

Field What to enter / what it means
Balances as of The date of the balances. It starts as today and cannot be a future date.
Purpose Required. What the member needs the certificate for, for example "loan application with another lender". It prints on the certificate.

To issue a certificate of balances:

  1. Show the member's statement.
  2. Click Certificate of balances.
  3. Check the Balances as of date and type the Purpose.
  4. Click Issue and print. The certificate opens with the print dialog. Back to the statement above it returns to the statement.

The certificate has:

  • The heading CERTIFICATE OF BALANCES, the next number of the Certificate of balances series (BC-000003 is next in the sample data) and the date of issue.
  • The wording of the Certificate of balances template: the member, member number, member type, membership date and status, and the as-of date.
  • A table of the share capital and deposit accounts (savings and time deposits) open on that date, with Account, Account no. and Balance on that date, and the lines Total paid-up share capital and Total deposits.
  • A table of the loans released by that date that still had a principal balance: Loan, Released, Maturity and Principal balance, with Total loan principal outstanding, or "No outstanding loan.".
  • The Past due today column, only when the date is today: what is due and unpaid on each open loan today. The note then says "Past due amounts include the interest and penalties due today." For an earlier date the column is left out and the note says "Loan balances are principal; interest is charged by the schedule and is not included."
  • "This certification is issued on (date) upon the request of the member for (purpose).", "Not valid without the seal of the Cooperative or with erasures or alterations.", and the signature lines Prepared by (your name), Certified correct (the bookkeeper) and Noted (the General Manager).

Each certificate is recorded in the Document Print History report with its number, the as-of date and the purpose, and in the audit trail. To give the member another copy later, issue a new certificate: it takes a new number.

Reprints of controlled documents

The ID card, the share capital certificate and the vouchers and receipts printed from the cooperative pages are controlled documents. Every print is recorded in the Document Print History report:

  • The first print is the Original. It prints without asking anything.
  • Any later print is a reprint. The program first shows Reprint ...: "This share capital certificate was already printed 1 time. A reprint is marked as a copy and recorded in the audit trail." Type the Reason for the reprint and click Reprint, or Cancel.
  • The reprint is marked as a numbered copy ("COPY 1 (reprint)" on certificates and vouchers, "Reissued (copy 1)" on the ID card), and the reason is kept with it.

Printing a controlled document needs the Print/Export right on the page it is printed from.

Good to know

  • "Enter an as-of date that is not in the future."
  • "Enter the purpose the member asked the certificate for."
  • "Issue a new certificate of balances from the member statement." appears when a certificate page is opened again outside the session that issued it.
  • "That member belongs to another branch." A user tied to a branch sees the statements of that branch's members only.

Cooperative Vouchers

A cooperative journal voucher records an adjustment between the members' accounts and the general ledger, with the member on each line: for example moving an amount posted to the wrong member's savings, or a charge to a member's share capital. A line on a member's savings or share capital account changes that account's ledger as well as the general ledger. The page also lists the cooperative journal vouchers and loan release vouchers already posted, so they can be opened, printed and voided.

Where to find it: no longer in the menu: the cooperative asked for it to be taken out in version 39, as not needed. The page stays for the vouchers already on file: the number of a cooperative journal voucher (CJV) opens it from Find, the General Ledger, a member's ledger or the reports, to be viewed, printed or voided. A correction between members' accounts is made with a debit or credit memo on Savings Account Transaction, and any other entry on Make Journal Entries.

The voucher list

The list shows the cooperative journal vouchers (CJV numbers) and the loan release vouchers (LR numbers) of the period. Besides the vouchers made on this page, it has the cooperative journal vouchers the other cooperative pages post, for example loan adjustments, waivers and write-offs, interest postings and accruals, batch deductions, year-end distributions, the net surplus allocation, the cash over or short of a closed teller shift, and opening balance imports.

Type the From and To dates (this month to today at first), and in Find part of a voucher number, a member's name or number, or the memo, then click Show. The columns are Date, No., Member, Memo, Mode, Amount and Status (Posted or Void). The footer gives the number of vouchers and their total, voids excluded. Up to 500 vouchers are listed.

Click the number to open a voucher. A loan release voucher opens on Loan Release. From version 33 a loan is released through Banking (a Cash Voucher or a Check Voucher): those vouchers are on Loan Release Vouchers.

A saved voucher

The voucher shows Voucher (type and number), Date, Member, Branch / class, Reference, Memo, Approved by, Recorded (when and by whom), Status and the Total; the General ledger entry (Account, Name, Memo, Debit, Credit); and Member ledgers, the rows it wrote to the members' savings accounts and loans with the balance after each. A voided voucher shows when, by whom and why it was voided. The buttons are Print, Void and Back.

Print prints the JOURNAL VOUCHER with the cooperative's heading, the number and date, the member and reference, the account lines, the memo, and the signature lines Prepared by, Checked by (the bookkeeper), Approved by (the approver, or the General Manager) and Received by. A voided voucher prints with VOID across it. The voucher is a controlled document: a reprint needs a reason (see Reprints of controlled documents).

Making a journal voucher

Click New journal voucher (it needs the Create right). The form New cooperative journal voucher has:

Field What to enter / what it means
Date The date of the voucher. A past date is allowed only within the back-dating days of Cooperative Profile & Policies, or with the permission to back-date; a future date is refused.
Reference An outside reference, for example a memo number.
Branch The branch of the voucher. Its class goes on every ledger line.
Explanation Required. What the voucher is for. It is the memo of the voucher and of every line that has no memo of its own.

The lines (at least two):

Column What to enter / what it means
Member savings / share capital account A member's savings or share capital account (number, member and product). The line posts to the general ledger account of the account's product, and to the member's account as a credit memo (CM) for a credit or a debit memo (DM) for a debit.
or GL account Any other account of the chart of accounts, when the line is not on a member's savings or share capital account.
Member (for a member ledger account) The member, for a GL account that keeps a member ledger (an account marked With SL). It is required on those accounts.
Memo The description of the line.
Debit, Credit The amount, on one side only.

Add a line adds a row. Under the lines, Debits and Credits add up the lines; they must be equal before you post.

To make a cooperative journal voucher:

  1. On the voucher list (open a cooperative journal voucher and click Vouchers), click New journal voucher.
  2. Check the Date, type the Reference, choose the Branch and type the Explanation.
  3. On each line, choose the member's account in Member savings / share capital account, or a GL account (with the Member where needed), and type the Debit or the Credit.
  4. Check that Debits equals Credits.
  5. If you do not have the Approve right on this page, open Supervisor approval and let the supervisor type their username and password, or type an approval code (see Supervisor approval).
  6. Click Post voucher and confirm Post this journal voucher?. The saved voucher opens.
Example

A deposit of ₱1,000.00 was posted to the savings account of the wrong member. The voucher has two lines: the wrong member's savings account, debit ₱1,000.00 (a DM on that account), and the right member's savings account, credit ₱1,000.00 (a CM on that account). The general ledger lines cancel out on the savings deposits account, each with the member's name.

What it records

Account Debit Credit
Each debit line: the product account of the member's savings or share capital account (for example 2300 Savings Deposits), or the GL account of the line Line amount
Each credit line: the product account of the member's savings or share capital account, or the GL account of the line Line amount

The voucher is a Cooperative Journal Voucher numbered from the Cooperative journal voucher series of Document Numbering (CJV-000009 is next in the sample data). Each line carries the member's name and the class of the branch. A line on a member's account also writes a CM or DM entry to that account's ledger, with the line's memo.

Voiding a voucher

A void keeps the voucher on file marked VOID and reverses the general ledger and the member ledgers together. Open the voucher, click Void, type the Reason (required, recorded in the audit trail) and click Void with the voucher number. A supervisor approves in the same form unless you have the Approve right on Cooperative Vouchers. The Void button needs the Delete/Void right.

When a voucher cannot be voided, the Void button is greyed and the voucher shows "This voucher cannot be voided now:" with the reason. A void is refused when:

  • the voucher brought opening balances over from the previous system: "Correct an opening balance with an adjusting entry (Loan Adjustments, a debit / credit memo), not by voiding the import.";
  • it posted a year-end distribution batch, or the net surplus allocation of a fiscal year: these are reversed with an adjusting entry;
  • it released a loan that already has payments or adjustments ("Void those first, or restructure the loan."), or a loan that is paid, written off or renewed;
  • voiding it would overdraw a member's savings account: "Void the later withdrawals first.";
  • it placed a time deposit that has since matured or been paid, or that has interest accruals posted;
  • it is a receipt of a teller shift that is already closed, and you do not have the Approve right on Teller Shift & Cash Count;
  • a check deposited or received on it was cleared or returned since: "Its check was cleared or returned on voucher ... Void that voucher first.";
  • it is dated on or before the closing date, unless you may change closed periods (see The closing date).

Good to know

  • "Enter the explanation of the voucher."
  • "A voucher has at least two lines."
  • "The voucher does not balance: debits ..., credits ..., difference ...." Correct the amounts so the debits equal the credits.
  • "A line is either a debit or a credit, not both."
  • "Choose the account of each line."
  • "Account ... keeps a member ledger: choose the member on its line." The line is on an account marked With SL and has no member. Choose the member, or use the member's account in the first column.
  • "Loan balances are changed on Loans > Loan Adjustments, so the loan ledger and the schedule follow." A loans receivable account cannot be used on this voucher; use Loan Adjustments.
  • "A member on the voucher belongs to another branch."
  • A debit on a member's savings account cannot take more than the available balance: "Insufficient available balance on ...".
  • "Back-dated entries beyond 0 day(s) need the "can record back-dated member transactions" permission." and "The transaction date cannot be in the future."
  • "Supervisor approval is required: cooperative journal voucher. Ask a supervisor to enter their username and password below, or type a one-time approval code."

Opening Balances Import

When the cooperative starts using the program, this page brings over the members and their balances from the previous system as of a cut-off date: the members, the savings and share capital balances, the time deposit placements and the loan balances. Each file is checked first, row by row, without saving anything. The import then saves every row in one go, with one journal voucher that puts the same totals on the general ledger control accounts, so the member ledgers and the general ledger agree from the first day.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Opening Balances Import.

What can be imported

Import in this order: members, then savings and share capital, time deposits and loans. The Columns card on the page lists the columns of each kind, the required ones in bold, and has a sample link that downloads a CSV file with the header and one example row.

Import Required columns What it creates
Members member_no, last_name, first_name One individual member per row. member_type and branch are codes; status is active (the default) or applicant; membership_date is the cut-off date when blank. No voucher is posted.
Savings and share capital balances member_no, product, balance One account per row of the savings or share capital product (its code), with an opening balance entry (code OB) dated on the cut-off date. An empty account of the same product is used if the member has one; otherwise a new account is opened. account_no, opened_on and passbook_no are optional.
Time deposit placements member_no, product, principal, rate, placement_date, term_days One placement per row. rate is in % a year; maturity_action is payout (the default), renew_principal, renew_all or credit_savings; accrued_interest is the interest already booked as payable at the cut-off.
Loan balances member_no, product, principal_balance, first_due, remaining_term One loan per row with a new schedule: the principal balance over the remaining term (months, or days when term_unit is days) from the first_due date. A first_due before the cut-off makes those amortizations past due. mode and rate default to the loan product's. interest_balance replaces the scheduled interest of add-on loans; accrued_interest is interest receivable already in the general ledger.

The file is a CSV file (comma, semicolon or tab separated) whose first line is the header. Dates are written as YYYY-MM-DD.

Step by step

Field What to enter / what it means
Import What the file holds: Members, Savings and share capital balances, Time deposit placements or Loan balances.
Cut-off date The date of the balances. It starts as the last day of last month. The opening entries and the voucher are dated on it.
Offset account Shown for balances, placements and loans: the account that takes the other side of the voucher. "Normally Opening Balance Equity, cleared when the opening trial balance is entered."
CSV file The file to check (CSV, TXT or TSV, up to 10 MB).
or paste the rows (first line is the header) Instead of a file, paste the rows copied from a spreadsheet.

To import opening balances:

  1. Open Members & Others > Opening Balances Import.
  2. Choose what to Import, type the Cut-off date, and for balances check the Offset account.
  3. Choose the CSV file, or paste the rows.
  4. Click Check. Nothing is saved by checking.
  5. Read the result: the heading gives the number of rows and how many have errors. Each row shows its line number, its first columns and, under Check, "ok" or what is wrong. Rows with errors are red. For balances, General ledger effect shows the debit or credit each control account will receive.
  6. If any row has an error, correct the file and check it again: "Correct the rows marked in red in the file and check it again. Nothing has been saved."
  7. When every row is ok, click Import with the number of rows and confirm. All the rows are saved together, or none.
  8. Run the Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation (the SL vs GL reconciliation button) to check that the ledgers agree.

The Imports done list shows each import: When (and by whom), What (and the file name), Cut-off, Rows, Amount and the Voucher with the offset account. Click the voucher number to open it.

What it records

Each import of balances, placements or loans posts one Cooperative Journal Voucher dated on the cut-off date, with the memo "Opening balances:" and the kind. Each member row is a line with the member's name:

Account Debit Credit
The account of the savings, share capital or time deposit product Balance or principal
The accrued interest account of the time deposit product (or the default interest payable on deposits) Accrued interest
The receivable account of the loan product (or the default loans receivable) Principal balance
The default interest receivable on loans Accrued interest
The offset account (for example 3900 Opening Balance Equity) The difference, on the side that balances the voucher

A loan's interest_balance and penalty_balance go to the loan's own ledger; the general ledger receives only the principal balance and the accrued interest.

Important

An import cannot be voided. Correct an opening balance with an adjusting entry, for example on Loan Adjustments or with a debit or credit memo on the member's account.

Good to know

  • Checking a file needs the Create right on this page. Importing needs the Approve right on this page (or the Administrator role): "The file is ready. Importing needs the Approve right on this page."
  • Every row is checked again when you click Import. If the checked file is no longer in your session: "The checked file is no longer in your session. Check it again."
  • "Missing column(s): ..." The header lacks a required column.
  • Typical row errors: "member no. 100101 already exists", "member no. 100101 is not on file (import the members first)", "product 01 is not a savings / deposit product", "time deposits are imported as placements (choose Time deposit placements)", "opened after the cut-off date", "the member already has a Regular Savings account with transactions".
  • Balances can only be imported for active, applicant or suspended members.
  • The voucher is refused when the books are closed through the cut-off date, unless you may change closed periods.

Cooperative Profile & Policies

The cooperative's registration details, the names of the signatories that print on documents, and the policies the cooperative pages follow: the minimum share capital, back-dating, approval thresholds, teller rules, account number formats, loan payment rules, alerts and the year-end distribution percentages.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Cooperative Profile & Policies.

The settings

Registration

Setting What it means and where it is used
Registered name (as in the CDA certificate) Printed at the top of every cooperative document and on the ID card. Blank uses the company name.
CDA registration number Printed under the name on the cooperative documents as "CDA Registration No.".
Type of cooperative Credit, Multi-Purpose, Consumers, Producers, Marketing, Service, Agrarian Reform, Cooperative Bank, Electric, Transport, Workers, Housing or Other.
Category Primary, Secondary or Tertiary, as registered.
Fiscal year ends (MM-DD) Usually 12-31. Used for the fiscal year on Net Surplus Allocation, CAPR Data Preparation & Export and Compliance Tracking.
Division name printed under the cooperative name on documents Printed under the cooperative's name at the top of every cooperative document, for example Savings and Credit Division. Leave it blank to print nothing.

Signatories

Setting What it means and where it is used
General Manager Prints on the ID card, the share capital and time deposit certificates, as Noted on the certificate of balances, and as Approved by on vouchers that have no approver.
Chairperson of the Board Prints on the share capital certificate and the CDA reports.
Treasurer Prints on the share capital and time deposit certificates and on the teller's cash count.
Credit Committee chair / Loans officer Prints as Noted by (Credit Committee) on the credit investigation.
Bookkeeper / Accountant Prints as Checked by on vouchers and as Certified correct on the statement of account and the certificate of balances.

Membership and share capital

Setting What it means and where it is used
Minimum paid-up share capital to activate a member Checked when a member is made active on Member Information. The member type can set a higher minimum; the higher one applies. Zero means no minimum.
Par value per share The par value a new member starts with (subscribed shares times par value is the subscribed capital). On the share capital certificate it is used for a member with no par value of their own when the share capital product has none either.
Membership fee When an applicant has not paid a membership fee yet, the official receipt for the applicant starts with a Membership fee line of this amount.

Teller and transactions

Setting What it means and where it is used
Cash transactions need an open teller shift Yes: official receipts and member disbursements (savings deposits and withdrawals and loan collections included) and loan releases in cash are refused until the user opens a teller shift on Teller Shift & Cash Count: "Open your teller shift first (Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count)."
Days a transaction may be back-dated without the special permission Checked on every member transaction: official receipts and disbursements, savings transfers, time deposits, loan releases, adjustments, waivers, restructures and write-offs, interest and dormancy postings, batch deductions, distributions and cooperative journal vouchers. A date further back needs the special permission Cooperative: can record back-dated member transactions. Zero: only users with that permission may back-date.
A receipt above this needs supervisor approval An official receipt or a deposit (on Official Receipt, Savings Account Transaction or Loan Collection) above this total needs a supervisor's approval, unless the user has the Approve right on that page. Zero = no limit.
A withdrawal above this needs supervisor approval A member disbursement or savings withdrawal above this total needs a supervisor's approval, unless the user has the Approve right on that page. This is on top of each role's own limit, the special permission Cooperative: max savings withdrawal without supervisor approval. Zero = only the role limits apply.
Cash over/short a teller may post without approval When a teller shift is closed on Teller Shift & Cash Count, a difference between the cash counted and the cash expected that is larger than this needs a supervisor's approval, unless the user has the Approve right on that page. Zero: every difference needs a supervisor.
Savings account number format How new savings account numbers are built: {BRANCH} the 2-digit branch, {BRANCHC} the branch code as written, {MEMBER} the member number, {PRODUCT} the 2-digit product code, {PRODUCTC} the product code as written, {SEQ} the next Savings account number of Document Numbering. Reference layout: {BRANCH}{MEMBER}{PRODUCT}1. When the number is already used (a second account of the same product), -2, -3 and so on is added.
Loan account number format How new loan numbers are built: {BRANCH}, {BRANCHC}, {PRODUCT} (the product code), {SEQ} (the next Loan account number) and {YEAR}. Reference layout: {BRANCH}-{PRODUCT}-{SEQ}, for example 02-6-0000005.

Loans

Setting What it means and where it is used
Payment allocation order How a loan payment is split when no one splits it by hand: Penalty, interest, then principal, Interest, penalty, then principal, Interest, principal, then penalty or Principal, interest, then penalty. Users with the special permission Cooperative: can override the payment allocation (penalty, interest, principal) on a loan collection may split a payment themselves.
Compute penalties when a payment is received Yes: the penalty up to the payment date is worked out when a payment is received. No: only the penalties posted by the batch run (Post Loan Penalties & Interest Accrual) are collected.
Remind of amortizations due within (days) The cooperative alerts on the Home page count the loans with an amortization due within this many days.

Alerts

Setting What it means and where it is used
Time deposits maturing within (days) The Home page alert counts the time deposits maturing within this many days, or already matured.
Accounts turning dormant within (days) The Home page alert counts the savings accounts that become dormant within this many days.
Documents, collateral and insurance expiring within (days) The Home page alerts count the member documents, and the loan collateral documents and insurance, expiring within this many days or already expired.

Year-end distribution (defaults; confirm against your bylaws and the General Assembly resolution)

Setting What it means and where it is used
Reserve fund (% of net surplus), Education and training fund (%), Part of the education fund due to the union / federation (%), Community development fund (%), Optional fund (%) The percentages a new year starts with on Net Surplus Allocation, where they can be changed for that year. The Statement of Operations also shows the reserve, education and training, community development and optional funds at these percentages, for information. Each is from 0 to 100, and the reserve, education, community development and optional funds together may not be more than 100 %.
Interest on share capital basis Average daily balance or Ending balance: the basis a new interest on share capital batch starts with.

To change the settings: change the boxes and click Save settings. The program says how many settings were saved, and each change is recorded in the audit trail with the old and new value. Changing the settings needs the Edit right on this page; other users see them greyed.

Good to know

  • "Enter the fiscal year end as MM-DD, for example 12-31."
  • "Reserve fund (% of net surplus): enter a percentage from 0 to 100." and "The statutory percentages add up to more than 100 %."
  • The company address, TIN and logo are set on Company, Security & Closing Date; the wording of the promissory notes, certificates and letters on Document Templates.

Branches

The cooperative's head office and branches. Every member belongs to a branch, every branch posts under its own class (so the reports by class are the branch reports), and each branch can have its own series of official receipt numbers.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Branches.

The list shows Code, Branch (with "head office" beside the head office, and the address), Class (reports), OR prefix ("(none)" when there is none), Members, Users and Edit. An inactive branch is greyed.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Code Required, up to 10 letters, numbers or dashes, for example 01. It must not be used by another branch. It is part of the savings and loan account numbers ({BRANCH} uses its digits).
Name Required, for example Poblacion Branch.
Address, Phone, Email The branch's contact details.
Class used on its postings The class every posting of the branch carries. Leave it at Make a class with the branch name and the program uses the class with the branch's name, or creates it.
Official receipt prefix Starts every official receipt number of the branch, for example P for P000123, so each branch keeps its own series. No two branches may have the same prefix.
Head office Tick for the head office. Only one branch is the head office: ticking it on one branch takes it off the others.
Active Untick for a branch that is no longer used. An inactive branch is not offered for new members and vouchers.

To add a branch: fill the New branch form and click Save branch, or Save branch & New to add another. To change a branch: click Edit on its row, change the fields and click Save branch. Branches cannot be deleted. Each change is recorded in the audit trail.

Good to know

  • Users are given a branch in Cooperative branch on User Accounts, with their teller cash account. A user with a branch works only with the members of that branch, their accounts and vouchers, and sees that branch only on the member reports. Administrators, and roles with the special permission Cooperative: sees every branch regardless of the branch on the user account, see every branch.
  • "Enter the branch code and name."
  • "The branch code uses letters, numbers and dashes only (it is part of the account numbers)."
  • "Branch code 01 is already used." and "Another branch already uses the official receipt prefix P."

Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists

The pick lists of the cooperative pages: the member types with their minimum share capital, the clusters and segmentation of members, the member groupings (account classes, account groups, address sub-groups and savings groups), and the lists of loan purposes, collateral types, member document types and ID types. Every entry can be added, renamed, switched off and deleted; an entry still used by a member (or a water-service account) is not deleted: the page says how many use it, and switching it off stops offering it.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists.

The tabs

Tab What it holds and where it is used
Member types The Member type of each member, for example Regular or Associate - Individual. The list shows Code, Member type, Kind, Regular, Min. share capital and Members.
Clusters The Cluster on a member's Membership tab, also a filter of the Member Master List and of the distribution batches. A cluster can belong to one branch or to Any branch.
Segmentation The Segmentation on a member's Membership tab. A loan product can be limited to the members of one segmentation, and distribution batches can be limited to one.
Loan purposes The Purpose of a loan application.
Collateral types The Type of a collateral on a loan application.
Account classes, Account groups, Address sub-groups, Savings groups The member groupings on the Grouping tab of Member Information, and the Grouping filter of the Member Master List. Renaming an entry renames it on the members that have it. The Address sub-group is also shown, filtered and set (for selected accounts at once) on Water Billing > Water Service Accounts, and its list is kept on Water Billing > Service & Materials > Address Sub-groups too, or from the link Add, edit or delete sub-groups under every Address sub-group box (Member Information and Water Service Accounts): a renamed sub-group is changed on the members and the water-service accounts that have it; one in use cannot be deleted, but it can be switched off.
Member document types The Kind of a document on a member's Documents tab.
ID types The kinds of identification documents the cooperative accepts.

Fields

A member type has:

Field What to enter / what it means
Code Required and not used by another type, for example REG. The code is what the Members import uses in its member_type column.
Name Required, for example Regular.
Kind Individual, Joint account or Institutional: the kind of member the type is for.
Minimum share capital to activate The paid-up share capital a member of this type needs before being made active. When the minimum on Cooperative Profile & Policies is higher, that one applies.
