Regular member (votes, may be elected) Ticked for regular members, unticked for associate members. The CDA report figures count regular and associate members by this tick.
Order The position of the type in the lists.
Active Untick to stop using the type.

A cluster has a Name and a Branch; a segmentation a Name; an entry of the other lists a Name (not used twice on the same list), a Note and an Order. All have Active.

To add an entry: click the tab, fill the form on the right and click Save. To change one: click Edit on its row, change it and click Save. Adding needs the Create right, changing the Edit right. Nothing is deleted on this page: an entry in use is switched off (not active) rather than deleted, so old records keep their names. Each change is recorded in the audit trail.

Messages: "Enter the name.", "Enter the code.", "That code is already used." and "That name is already on the list."

Passbook Types

This is the Passbook Type Setup of the former program, with the design of each passbook. A passbook type is either a savings passbook or a loan passbook: each kind has its own design, its own header and its own columns. The savings and share capital products choose a savings passbook type, the loan products a loan passbook type, and Passbook Printing prints with it.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Passbook Types (Setup). Passbook Types on Passbook Printing opens the type of the passbook shown.

The list of Passbook types shows the Type ID, the Name and description, the Kind (Savings or Loan) and Used by (the products that use it). Click a type to open it; New savings passbook and New loan passbook start a type of that kind with the set-up and the design of the former program.

Passbook Type Setup

Every measurement is in tups (twips), as in the former program: 1 inch = 1,440 tups (1 cm = 567). Each is a whole number from 0 to 30,000.

Field What to enter / what it means
Passbook Information: Type ID, Name, Description, Kind, Active The name is required and must not be used by another type. Kind: Savings passbook or Loan passbook; it cannot change once a product or an account uses the type. Untick Active for a type no longer used.
Cover: Top, Left Where the inside cover starts printing (Print Passbook Cover). The fields of the cover are placed from here.
Header: Top, Left The top margin and the left margin of the passbook header: where the header starts printing on a page.
Footer: Top, Left Where the footer prints (Print Passbook Footer, and the foot of a full page when the footer is set to print there).
Passbook Body: Top, Left The top margin and the left margin of the passbook body: where line 1 prints.
Interval The distance from one entry to the next (275 tups is about 1/5 inch). Line 5, for example, prints at Body Top plus 4 intervals.
Start Line Skip The first line never printed on (the centre fold of the passbook), or 0 when no line is skipped. Printing always starts on line 1, so it is 2 or more.
No. of Line Skip How many lines are skipped from Start Line Skip. With 13 and 2, lines 13 and 14 are left blank: after line 12 the next entry prints on line 15. With 12 and 1, line 12 is left blank and printing resumes on line 13.
Max Lines The last line of a page. The entry after it goes to line 1 of the next page, after the header.
Passbook size, Page width, Page height The size of an open passbook page: choose one of the sizes (4.5 x 7, 4.75 x 6.75, 5 x 7, 6 x 8 inches, 8.5 x 6 inches, or letter paper for a test on plain paper) or type the width and height. One design fits any size: every field is placed in tups.
Font, Characters per inch, Font size (pt) Dot matrix (Courier New) or Arial; the characters per inch of the dot-matrix printer (10, 12, 15, 17 or 20; 17 and 20 are the condensed print of the passbooks of the former program), or As the font size; the size from 6 to 16 points.
Header prints On a new page, before line 1 (as the former program), With every printing, or Never (preprinted).
Footer at the foot of a full page Ticked, when a page is full and entries continue on the next page, the footer prints at the foot of the full page.

Under the Passbook Body, a line in words says which lines of a page print, for example "Printing starts on line 1. A page prints lines 1 to 12 and 15 to 29; lines 13 and 14 are skipped (after line 12 the next entry prints on line 15). After line 29, the next entry goes to line 1 of the next page, after the header." It follows the numbers as you type them.

The design: cover, header, body and footer

Design of the savings passbook (or of the loan passbook) replaces the cover, header, body and footer templates of the former program. Its tabs Cover, Header, Body and Footer each show a drawing of the page with sample data, and under it the fields of that part:

Part Savings passbook Loan passbook
Cover Account Name, Account Number, Address, Contact Number, Branch; also Passbook No., Member ID, Savings product, Date opened, a text Member Name, Loan No., Address, Contact Number, Branch; also Member ID, Loan Type, Loan amount, Date released, Due Date, Term, P.N. No., a text
Header Account Name, Account Number; also Savings product, Passbook No., Member ID, Branch, Page no., a text Loan Type, Term (for example 36mos), Due Date; also Member Name, Loan No., P.N. No., Loan amount, Date released, Interest rate, Page no., a text
Body (one line per entry) Line no., Date, Transaction code, Deposit / interest, Withdrawal, Balance, Teller ID; also Reference no. Line no., Date, Reference No., Release, Loan payment (principal), Loan balance, Interest payment, Fines / penalties / surcharges, Teller ID; also Total payment, Code
Footer Balance, Print date, Page no., Next page no., the account (loan) no. and name, a text such as "Balance as of {date}: {balance}"

Each field has:

  • Left and Top: its place in tups from the Left / Top of its part (for the body, from Body Left; Top moves the column up or down within its line);
  • Width and Align: an amount is lined up on the right of its width (Right), a text starts at Left (Left), or is centred;
  • Format: a date as 6/12/25, 06/12/25, 06/12/2025, Jun 12, 2025 or 12/06/2025; an amount as 1,234.56, 1234.56 or 1,235, and what a zero amount prints (blank, a dash or 0.00); the term as 36mos, 36 months or 36; the rate as 18.00% or 18%;
  • Size (pt): blank for the font size of the type.

To place the fields: drag a field on the drawing (the arrow keys move the field selected by 10 tups, with Shift by 100), or type its numbers in the table. Drag the blue cross of a part to move the whole part: it changes the Top and Left of the part in the set-up. Add a field adds the next field not placed yet; the red cross removes one; Use the default ... puts back the design of the former program for that part. On the Body tab the drawing shows every line of the page with sample entries, the skipped lines shaded and the line numbers beside the page; Zoom enlarges the drawing.

Click Save. Print a test page prints the saved type with sample data: A page with sample entries (the header, every printable line and the footer), The line numbers of a page (to check the interval and the skipped lines against a real passbook) or The cover. Put a passbook (or plain paper) in the printer, print, and adjust the margins until every field falls on its place.

To set up the passbooks of the former program:

  1. Open Regular Passbook (a savings passbook): Cover Top 900, Left 2400; Header Top 700, Left 300; Footer Top 8000, Left 100; Body Top 1230, Left 200, Interval 275; Start Line Skip 12, No. of Line Skip 1, Max Lines 26. Check the design and click Save.
  2. Open Loan Passbook (a loan passbook): Body Left 1; Start Line Skip 13, No. of Line Skip 2, Max Lines 29, the other margins as above. Its header prints the Loan Type, the Term and the Due Date. Click Save.
  3. Print a test page of each and adjust.
  4. On Savings & Deposit Products and Loan Products, choose the passbook type of each product: a savings product lists only the savings passbooks, a loan product only the loan passbooks.

Slip validation: margins (at the bottom of the page) sets where the machine validation prints on the deposit and withdrawal slips of Savings Account Transaction and on the loan payslips of Loan Collection: the Top margin, Right margin, Bottom margin and Left margin in inches (1 inch = 25.4 mm), measured from the edges of the slip, the Distance between the lines (inches) and the Font size (pt). The picture under the boxes shows a slip with the two lines where they will print, and follows the margins as you type them. Click Save the margins. Only the two lines are printed: no title, page number or address of the page.

Note

The validation, the passbook pages, the header, the footer and the cover print with no margin of their own, so the positions you set are the positions on the paper. In the browser's print window use Scale 100 %, Margins None (or Default) and untick Headers and footers, so the browser adds no title, address or page number. The printing window reminds you of this above the page; the reminder is not printed.

Adding needs the Create right, changing the Edit right and deleting (a type no product or account uses) the Delete right. Each change is recorded in the audit trail. A type saved before version 34 keeps printing as it did until it is saved here: its column positions, header and cover positions and footer text are shown in the design.

Messages: "Enter the name of the passbook type.", "Passbook Body, Interval: the distance from one line to the next, 100 to 2,000 tups (275 is about 1/5 inch).", "Start Line Skip: the first line never printed on (the centre fold), from 2 to 29, or 0 when no line is skipped. Printing always starts on line 1.", "No. of Line Skip: the lines skipped from line 13, at least 1, and a line must be left after them before Max Lines (29).", "The body prints no field: add the columns of the passbook lines to the Body.", "The passbook type is used by products or accounts: it stays a loan passbook." and "A passbook type with that name exists."

Document Numbering

The numbers of the cooperative's documents: member numbers, loan applications, vouchers, official receipts, certificates, batches and passbooks. For each one you set the prefix, the next number and the number of digits.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Document Numbering.

The documents

Each row has Document, Prefix (up to 10 characters), Next number (1 or more), Digits (0 to 12; the number is padded with zeros to this length) and Next one will be, which shows the result, for example CJV-000009.

Document When a number is taken
Member number A new member saved with Member no. (blank = next number) left blank.
Loan application A new loan application.
Promissory note A loan release, when no promissory note number is typed.
Loan account ({SEQ} of the loan number format) The {SEQ} part of a new loan number (the format is on Cooperative Profile & Policies).
Official receipt (each branch adds its own prefix) A new official receipt. The branch's Official receipt prefix goes in front of the number.
Member disbursement voucher, Cooperative journal voucher A new voucher of that kind.
Loan release voucher ID The voucher ID of a loan release (LR-), printed on its Cash Voucher or Check Voucher; the cash voucher and the check keep their own numbers.
Time deposit certificate A new time deposit placement, when no certificate number is typed.
Share capital certificate The first print of a member's share capital certificate.
Certificate of balances Every certificate of balances issued.
Savings account ({SEQ} of the account number format) The {SEQ} part of a new savings account number, when the format uses {SEQ}.
Batch deduction, Distribution batch A new batch deduction or distribution batch.

Numbers are drawn while the document is saved, with a lock, so two users never get the same number. A number typed by hand, such as a pre-printed receipt number, is checked against the numbers already used.

To change the numbering: change the Prefix, Next number or Digits of the rows and click Save numbering. A next number lower than the present one is refused, because numbers already issued could repeat: "Cooperative journal voucher: the next number would go back from 9 to 5 and numbers already issued could repeat. Tick "allow going back" if this is intended." To lower it on purpose, tick Allow a next number lower than now (risk of repeats) and save again. Changing the numbering needs the Edit right; each change is recorded in the audit trail.

Document Templates

The wording of the cooperative's printed documents: the promissory note, the disclosure statement notes, the co-maker statement, the time deposit certificate, the share capital certificate, the certificate of balances, the loan payment reminder and the demand letter. Until the cooperative saves its own version, each document prints the standard wording. A version is never overwritten: each one takes effect on a date, so a document printed again later keeps the wording in effect on its own date.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Document Templates.

There is one tab per document: Promissory note, Disclosure statement notes, Co-maker statement, Time deposit certificate, Share capital certificate, Certificate of balances, Loan payment reminder and Demand letter.

A new version

Field What to enter / what it means
Name The name of the version. It starts as the document's name.
Effective from Required. Documents dated on or after this date use the version. It starts as today.
Paper A4, Letter or Legal: the page size the browser prints the document on while this version is in effect.
Text (paragraphs, bold, italic, lists and tables are kept) Required. The wording, with the formatting tags. It starts with the version in use today, or the standard wording. Type the placeholders listed under the box, such as {{member}} or {{amount_words}}, where the program should fill in the member's details.

To change the wording of a document:

  1. Open Members & Others > Document Templates and click the document's tab.
  2. Change the Text. Keep the placeholders you need; the list under the box gives the ones this document can fill.
  3. Type the Effective from date.
  4. Click Save as a new version. The program says: "Version 1 saved. Documents dated from 10/11/2026 use it; older documents keep the version of their date."

Only these formatting tags are kept: p, br, b, strong, i, em, u, ul, ol, li, table, thead, tbody, tfoot, tr, th, td, h3, h4, h5, div, span, hr, small and blockquote. Other tags are taken out but their text stays; scripts, styles and form fields are taken out with their content; and every attribute except colspan and rowspan is removed. The values put in the placeholders are printed as plain text. A placeholder the document does not know prints as typed.

Versions

The Versions card lists the versions of the document: Version (number and name, with "in use today" on the one printing today), Effective and By. View shows the text of a version as saved. Switch off takes a version out of use, after a confirmation: documents then use the previous version, or the standard wording. A switched-off version stays on the list, greyed.

The version a document prints is the active version with the latest effective date on or before the document's date:

Document The date that picks the version
Promissory note, Disclosure statement notes, Co-maker statement The release date of the loan (printed on Loan Documents).
Time deposit certificate The placement date (printed on Time Deposit Center).
Share capital certificate The day it is printed (on Member Statement of Account).
Certificate of balances The day it is issued (on Member Statement of Account).
Loan payment reminder, Demand letter The date of the notice.

Saving or switching off a version needs the Create right on this page. Each is recorded in the audit trail, and every print and reprint of the documents is logged in the Document Print History.

Messages: "Enter the text of the document." and "Enter the date the version takes effect."

Cooperative reports

The Cooperative reports are listed under Members & Others > Reports in the sidebar and in the Cooperative card of the Report Center. The common report tools (Refresh, Print / PDF, Export to spreadsheet, Email link, Modify Report, Memorize and clicking a figure to open what is behind it) are explained in Working with reports.

The member reports (Member Master List, Membership Movement, Share Capital Balances and Capital Build-up) show only the members of the user's branch when the user is tied to a branch. The financial statements are for the whole cooperative, from the general ledger.

Member Master List

The list of members with their details and their present balances: the masterlist the cooperative keeps of its membership.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Member Master List, or the Masterlist report button on the Member Center.

Filters: Status, Member type, Branch and Cluster (each All at first), and Search, which finds part of a name or mobile number, or a whole member number.

The columns: Member no., Name (click it to open the member), Type, Status, Member since, Mobile, Branch, Subscribed capital, Paid-up capital (the share capital accounts), Savings and deposits (savings and time deposit accounts) and Loans outstanding (the principal of the open loans). Sex, Age and Address are extra columns, left out until they are ticked on the Columns tab of Modify Report. The total line gives the number of members and the totals of the amounts.

Membership Movement

How the membership changed in a period, by member type: the members at the start, those admitted, those who resigned, were terminated or died, and the members at the end.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Membership Movement.

Filters: Dates (This fiscal year at first), From, To and Branch.

How to read it: one line per member type with Beginning, Admitted, Resigned, Terminated, Deceased and Ending, and a TOTAL line. A member counts from the membership date until they resign, are terminated or die; a member who is reinstated counts again. A member without a membership date is not counted. Under the totals, Movements in the period lists each admission, suspension, resignation, termination and death with the date, the member and the reason; click a line to open the member.

Share Capital Balances

Each member's paid-up share capital on a date, against the capital they subscribed.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Share Capital Balances.

Filters: As of, Product (the share capital products), Branch and Member type.

The columns: Member, Member no., Account (click it to open the account), Paid-up shares (the paid-up capital divided by the member's par value), Subscribed (the member's subscribed capital), Paid-up (the balance on the as-of date), Unpaid subscription and Paid (the percentage paid). Product is an extra column. An account with nothing paid and no subscription is left out. The total line adds the subscribed, paid-up and unpaid amounts.

Capital Build-up

The share capital each member added in a period: from deposits, loan deductions, payroll batches and distributions credited to capital.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Capital Build-up.

Filters: Dates (This fiscal year at first), From, To, Product and Branch. Product lists the share capital and special savings products; All takes every share capital product.

The columns: Member, Member no., Account, Beginning, Build-up (what was credited in the period), Withdrawn / applied (what was debited) and Ending, with totals. Accounts with no balance and no movement are left out.

Statement of Financial Condition

The balance sheet in the cooperative format: assets, liabilities and members' equity under the headings cooperatives use, from the general ledger.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Statement of Financial Condition.

Filter: As of.

How to read it: every account with a balance is listed under a heading, with a total for each heading and each section. Click an account to open its General Ledger.

  • ASSETS: Cash and cash equivalents (the Bank-type accounts), Loans and interest receivable, net (the receivable accounts of the loan products, the past due and restructured loans, the allowance for losses and the interest receivable), Other receivables and current assets, Property and equipment, net and Other non-current assets.
  • LIABILITIES: Deposit liabilities (the accounts of the savings and time deposit products and the interest payable on deposits), Interest on share capital, patronage refund and other dues to members, Accounts and other payables and Non-current liabilities.
  • MEMBERS' EQUITY: Paid-up share capital (the accounts of the share capital products), Undivided net surplus and other equity (with the net surplus of prior years not yet allocated), Statutory funds (reserve, education and training, community development and optional funds) and Net surplus for the year to date.
  • TOTAL LIABILITIES AND MEMBERS' EQUITY at the end.

The note above the statement says "Total assets equal total liabilities and members' equity." When they do not, it says so in red.

Tip

The net surplus of the year stays in equity until the General Assembly allocates it on Net Surplus Allocation. After the allocation, the statutory funds and the amounts due to members appear under their headings.

Statement of Operations

The income statement in the cooperative format: revenues, costs and expenses of a period, and the net surplus or net loss.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Statement of Operations.

Filters: Dates (Fiscal year to date at first), From and To.

How to read it: the accounts with activity in the period, in six groups with a total each: Revenues from credit operations (the income accounts the loan products and the financial loan charges post interest, penalties and fees to), Other revenues, Financing cost (the interest expense on savings and time deposits), Cost of sales and services, Operating expenses and Other expenses. Then TOTAL REVENUES, TOTAL EXPENSES and NET SURPLUS (or NET LOSS). Click an account to open its General Ledger for the period.

When there is a net surplus, the report adds Allocation by the policies (for information; the General Assembly decides): the reserve fund, education and training fund, community development fund and optional fund at the percentages of Cooperative Profile & Policies.

Statement of Changes in Equity

How members' equity moved in a period, from the general ledger: share capital paid in and withdrawn, the net surplus, its allocation, and the use of the statutory funds.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Statement of Changes in Equity.

Filters: Dates (Fiscal year to date at first), From and To.

How to read it: the columns are Paid-up share capital, Statutory funds, Undivided net surplus and other equity and Total members' equity. The lines are:

  • Balance on the From date.
  • Share capital paid in and Share capital withdrawn or transferred out.
  • Net surplus for the period (always shown).
  • Allocation of net surplus (to statutory funds; ISC and patronage refund declared): the funds receive their part and the undivided surplus goes down by the whole allocation, because the interest on share capital and the patronage refund leave equity for the payables.
  • Statutory funds used and Other changes in equity.
  • Balance on the To date.

A line with nothing on it is left out. The surplus of the years not yet allocated stays in the undivided column until the General Assembly allocates it. The total agrees with members' equity on the Statement of Financial Condition; if the ending balance does not agree with the ledger, the note says so in red.

Statutory Funds

The statutory funds and the amounts due to members set up by the net surplus allocation, and what was added to them or spent and paid from them in a period.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Statutory Funds.

Filters: Dates (This fiscal year at first), From and To.

How to read it: one line each for the Reserve fund, the Education and training fund, the Education fund due to the union / federation (CETF), the Community development fund, the Optional fund, the Interest on share capital payable and the Patronage refund payable, with the Account (click it to open its General Ledger), Beginning, Allocated / added (the credits), Used / paid (the debits) and Ending. A fund whose account is not set in the cooperative's module default accounts (Module Default Accounts) is left out.

Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation

Compares the total of each member ledger (savings, share capital, time deposits, loans) with the balance of the general ledger control account it feeds. They should always agree.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation, or the SL vs GL reconciliation button on Opening Balances Import.

Filter: As of.

The columns: Member ledger, GL control account (click it to open its General Ledger), Accounts / loans (the number of open accounts or loans), Subsidiary ledger, General ledger and Difference. There is one line for each account the savings, share capital and time deposit products post to (Savings deposits, Share capital, Time deposits), and one for each loans receivable account (Loans receivable). Interest receivable on loans and Accrued interest on time deposits are compared only when the date is today.

When a line does not agree, the members whose balances differ are listed under it with their own figures (click a member to open them), and the note says how many ledgers do not agree.

The line Accrued interest on time deposits (one for each accrued-interest account of the time deposit products) compares the interest accrued on the active and matured deposits, less what the members withdrew of it before maturity (CAIW), with the account in the general ledger.

Tip

A difference usually comes from an entry posted to a control account without going through the member pages, for example a journal entry on Financial > Make Journal Entries. Click the control account to find the entry in its General Ledger.

Document Print History

Every print of a controlled document: ID cards, share capital certificates, certificates of balances, vouchers and receipts, and the loan documents. The first print is the original; each later print is a numbered copy with its reason.

Where to find it: Members & Others > Reports > Document Print History.

Filters: Dates, From, To, User, Show (All prints or Reprints only) and Search (a document number).

The columns: Printed (date and time), Document, Number, Copy (Original, or Copy 1, Copy 2 and so on), Reason or purpose and User, newest first. A certificate of balances is numbered on each issue; its line shows the as-of date and the purpose.

Tip

Choose Reprints only in Show to review the reprints of the period and their reasons.

Savings Account Center

The Savings Account Center lists the members' savings and share capital accounts with their balances, as in the cooperative's former program: a toolbar above the list, and a right-click menu on each account for its approval and its passbook. From it you open a new account. Open one account to see its ledger, its holds and the checks still clearing.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Savings Account Center. Account numbers shown on other pages, such as Savings Account Transaction, Check Clearing and the savings reports, open the account here.

The toolbar and the account list

Click an account to choose it, then use the toolbar. Double-click an account to open it.

Button What it does
New Opens a new savings or share capital account (see below).
Edit Opens the account chosen: its details, actions and ledger.
Close Closes the account chosen. A balance left is paid out first (see "To close an account"). Approve right.
Restrict Restricts the withdrawals of the account chosen with a reason, or lifts the restriction. Edit right.
Hold Holds an amount of the account chosen: that amount cannot be withdrawn, the rest can (see Holding an amount). Approve right.
Unhold Releases an amount on hold, or every hold of the account. Open only for an account with an amount on hold. Approve right.
Notes The notes of the account, shown to the tellers on Savings Account Transaction. Edit right.
Filter Shows the filters: Savings product, Status (For approval, Active, Dormant, Closed, Every status; left at Open accounts, every account not closed) and Branch.
Refresh Reads the list again.
Import Savings Opens the accounts of the former system, Active and with their balances, from a CSV file (see Importing savings accounts). Create right; uploading needs the Approve right.

The search box at the right finds a part of the member's name, the account number (or its first digits), the member number or the passbook number. The list follows as you type; no button is needed.

Column What it shows
Account no The account number.
Passbook no The passbook issued for the account.
Member name The member.
Savings Code, Savings Name The product of the account.
Current Balance The ledger balance.
Status ACTIVE, FOR APPROVAL, DORMANT or CLOSED; "restricted" when the withdrawals are restricted.

The footer gives the number of accounts and the total of their balances. When accounts are waiting for approval, a line above the list says so; click it to list them.

The right-click menu

Right-click an account (on a phone or tablet: the ⋮ button at the end of the row, or a long press) for these. Only what applies to the account is open; the rest is grey.

Menu item What it does
Approve Account Approves an account waiting for approval. Approve right.
Re-Open Account Reopens a closed account, with the reason. Approve right.
Reset Passbook Print Line After a printing error: sets the page and line where the next line prints, and from which transaction the lines print again (see Passbook Printing). Edit right on Passbook Printing.
Print Passbook Cover Prints the inside cover: Account Name, Account Number, Address, Contact Number and Branch, at the positions of the passbook type (Passbook Types).
Reprint Passbook Prints again the lines of a period, at the lines they were printed on, after a passbook was damaged or lost. The line tracking does not change.
Print Passbook Footer Prints the footer of the passbook type's design on the current page, for example the balance carried to the next passbook.
Update Passbook Opens Passbook Printing with the lines not yet printed.
Hold, Unhold As on the toolbar. Approve right.
Close Account The same as Close on the toolbar.
Delete Account Deletes an account opened by mistake. Only an account with no transaction at all can be deleted; any other is closed instead. Delete right.

Holding an amount (Hold and Unhold)

A hold earmarks an amount of the balance: that amount cannot be withdrawn until it is released, while the rest of the balance stays withdrawable. Restrict stops every withdrawal of the account; Hold stops only the amount held. An account can carry several holds, for example the collateral of a loan and a court order. The holds the loans place on the deposits (collateral, the payoff) are the same holds and are released the same way.

In the list an account with an amount on hold shows it under the balance, for example "2,000.00 on hold". Available on the account and on Savings Account Transaction is the balance less the checks clearing and the amounts on hold.

To hold an amount:

  1. Click the account in the Savings Account Center and click Hold (or right-click it and choose Hold).
  2. The window shows the Balance, what is already On hold and what is Withdrawable. Type the Amount to hold and the Reason (required), for example "Collateral of loan application LA-000015".
  3. Click Hold. The message gives what can be withdrawn now: "2,000.00 of 01100004011 is on hold: it cannot be withdrawn until it is released (Unhold). Withdrawable now 4,512.75."

The amount held can be no more than what is withdrawable: "Only 6,512.75 of 01100004011 can be held: the balance is 8,512.75, 2,000.00 already on hold." A closed account cannot be held.

To release a hold: click the account and click Unhold (grey for an account with nothing on hold). The window lists the active holds with their amount, reason, date and the user who placed them; choose one, or Every hold of the account when there are several, type the reason if you wish and click Unhold: "Unhold: 2,000.00 of 01100004011 released. Withdrawable now 6,012.75." On the account's own page, Unhold beside a hold in the Holds card releases that one.

A withdrawal, transfer or batch withdrawal that would take the balance below the amount on hold is refused. Every hold and release is on the audit trail with its amount and reason.

Importing savings accounts (Import Savings)

For the move from the former system, the members' savings and share capital accounts are brought in from a CSV file, one row per account. Each account is opened with the status Active and its balance is entered on the cut-off date with the savings transaction code you choose, whose GL account takes the other side of the journal entry. Import the members first (Import Member).

Where to find it: Import Savings on the toolbar of the Savings Account Center, after Refresh. Reading and checking a file needs the Create right; uploading it needs the Approve right.

Field or column What to enter
Savings transaction code (for the journal entry) On the page: the code that enters the balances. Only deposit codes with a GL account of their own are offered, such as INI Initial Savings Import (against 3900 Opening Balance Equity); add one on Savings Transaction Codes for another account. It fills the rows that leave the code blank.
Savings cut-off date On the page: the date of the balances, for the rows that leave it blank.
Member code, Member name Required. The member number, and the name, checked against it: every word of the last name and the first name must be there, in any order ("Dela Cruz, Juan S." or "Juan S. Dela Cruz"; "Cruz" alone is not enough).
Savings code Required. The code of the savings or share capital product (Savings & Deposit Products). A time deposit product is imported on the Time Deposit Center.
Amount Required. The balance at the cut-off date, as 12500.00 or 12,500.00. 0 opens the account without an entry.
Savings transaction code, Savings cut-off date Required, but a blank cell takes the code and the date of the page.
Account no., Passbook no., Opened on The account number and passbook number of the former system (blank: new numbers), and the opening date (blank: the cut-off date).

The file is read for review as on Import Member: every row is checked, corrected in its cells or removed, then uploaded. The bar adds up the amounts of the rows. A row is refused when the member is not on file, is not an active member or applicant, or is on hold, the name does not match the member code, the product is a time deposit or not active, the code has no GL account of its own, the member already has an account of the product with transactions, the same member and product are on an earlier row, the amount is above the product's maximum balance, or the cut-off date is on or before the closing date of the books (without the permission to change closed periods).

What it records: one cooperative journal voucher for each cut-off date and code, with a credit to each product's savings or share capital account for each member and a debit to the code's GL account for the total; each account gets one entry with the code (for example INI) on the cut-off date, its opening balance on the passbook and the statements.

Example

With INI and the cut-off date 09/30/2026, a row "200101, Dela Cruz, Juan S., 01, 12,500.00" opens Regular Savings for member 200101, Active, with 12,500.00: Savings Deposits credited 12,500.00, 3900 Opening Balance Equity debited 12,500.00.

Opening an account

Click New on the toolbar. The form Open a savings / share capital account opens.

Field What to enter / what it means
Member The member. Type part of the name, the member number, or an account or loan number, and choose from the list.
Product The product of the new account. Only active ordinary savings, special savings and share capital products are listed. A product open only to some member types refuses the others.
Branch The branch the account belongs to. It starts with the member's branch.
Opening date The date the account is opened. Today by default.
Passbook no. The number of the passbook issued for the account. It starts with the passbook number on the member's record.
Passbook type The passbook layout used to print this account's passbook. Leave it at As the product to use the passbook type of the product.

The account number is made from the Savings account number format on Cooperative Profile & Policies. The reference format {BRANCH}{MEMBER}{PRODUCT}1 gives 01100001031 for branch 01, member 100001 and product 03. When the number is already taken (a member's second account of the same product), the program adds -2, -3 and so on.

Time deposit accounts are not opened here: placing a time deposit on Time Deposit Center opens its account.

To open an account:

  1. Click New.
  2. Choose the Member and the Product.
  3. Check the Branch, the Opening date, the Passbook no. and the Passbook type.
  4. Click Open account. The new account opens. Or click Open and make the opening deposit to go to Savings Account Transaction with the new account filled in, and receive the opening deposit there. That button shows only when you may create savings transactions.

When you have the Approve right on the Savings Account Center, the new account is active at once. Otherwise it is Waiting for approval: it accepts deposits, but withdrawals and transfers out of it are refused until a supervisor approves it. Opening an account posts nothing; the opening deposit is a transaction of Savings Account Transaction.

One account

Click an account number to open the account.

The buttons at the top are Savings transaction (opens Savings Account Transaction with the account; not shown on a closed account or a time deposit), Passbook (opens Passbook Printing; shown when the account has a passbook type), Print ledger and Account Center. Under the member panel, which shows the member's share capital, savings, time deposits and loans, five figures describe the account:

Column What it means
Ledger balance The balance of the account, with its status (and "restricted" when withdrawals are restricted).
Available The balance less the checks clearing and the amounts on hold. The product's minimum balance must still stay in the account after a withdrawal.
Checks clearing Check deposits that are not cleared yet.
On hold The total of the active holds.
Interest this year The interest credited (code INT) since January 1, with the interest since opening under it.

The details give the account number, product, branch, opening date, when it was approved (or "not yet") and the last transaction. When they apply, they also give the date the account became dormant, the closing date and reason, the reason of a restriction, and the passbook number with the page and line last printed.

The Actions card holds the buttons you may use:

Button What it does
Approve the account Shown while the account is waiting for approval. Makes it active. Needs the Approve right on the page.
Restrict withdrawals / Lift the restriction Stops withdrawals and transfers out of the account, or allows them again. Deposits are still accepted. A reason is required to restrict. Needs the Edit right.
Hold Holds an amount (Amount to hold and Reason). The amount on hold cannot be withdrawn; the rest of the balance can. The Holds (Hold / Unhold) card lists the holds, each with Unhold while it is active. Needs the Approve right.
Close Closes the account with a reason. Greyed out until the balance is zero. Needs the Approve right.
Reopen Shown on a closed account. Makes it active again; a reason is required. Needs the Approve right.
Passbook details Changes the Passbook no. and the Passbook type (None for no passbook). Needs the Edit right.

When the account has holds, the Holds card lists each one: when it was placed, the reason (with the loan number when the hold belongs to a loan) and the amount. Release releases an active hold; released holds stay on the list in grey with the release date. On a time deposit account, a line gives the certificate, principal, rate, dates and status, with Open the placement.

The ledger shows the entries up to today. Type From and To and click Show to see one period; with From blank the ledger starts at the first entry.

Column What it shows
Date The date of the entry.
Code The savings transaction code, for example CSD for a cash deposit or INT for interest (see Savings Transaction Codes).
Particulars The description. A check deposit not yet cleared shows "clears" and its clearing date. A voided entry is struck through and marked (void).
Voucher / OR The official receipt or voucher number. Click it to open the voucher.
Withdrawal, Deposit The amount taken out of or put into the account.
Balance The running balance after the entry (blank on a voided entry).
By The user who recorded the entry.

Print ledger prints the ACCOUNT LEDGER with the cooperative heading, the member, member number and product, and the entries of the dates chosen.

Step by step

To approve an account that is waiting for approval:

  1. Click the line "... account(s) waiting for approval." above the list, or choose the Status For approval under Filter.
  2. Right-click the account and choose Approve Account, or open it and click Approve the account.
  3. Check the member and the product, and confirm.

To place a hold on part of the balance:

  1. Open the account and click Place a hold.
  2. Type the Amount held and the Reason.
  3. Click Place a hold. The Available figure goes down by the amount.

To release a hold: click Release on the hold in the Holds card and confirm.

To restrict withdrawals:

  1. Open the account and click Restrict withdrawals.
  2. Type the Reason, for example a court order or a dispute, and click Restrict withdrawals.
  3. To allow withdrawals again, click Lift the restriction, and Lift the restriction in the window. The reason is optional there.

To close an account:

  1. Clear or return the checks still clearing (Check Clearing) and release the holds.
  2. Click the account and Close on the toolbar (or Close Account on the right-click menu).
  3. Type a Closing fee if one is charged, choose the Class and type the Reason.
  4. Click Close the account. The fee is charged first with the closing fee code (CF). The balance left is then paid out with the closing code (CSW), a transaction of the Savings Transaction Center paid from your teller drawer, and the account is closed. It keeps its ledger and can be reopened later.

To reopen a closed account: right-click it and choose Re-Open Account (or open it and click Reopen), type the Reason and click Reopen the account.

What it records

Opening, approving, restricting, holding and reopening an account post nothing to the books. They are recorded in the audit trail, with the reason where one is asked. Closing an account with a balance posts its closing fee and closing withdrawal as transactions of the Savings Transaction Center. The money moves on other pages: deposits and withdrawals on Savings Account Transaction, and transfers, interest and batch deductions on cooperative journal vouchers. Each of those writes an entry to the account ledger shown here.

Good to know

  • "Only active members (or applicants opening their share capital) can open accounts."
  • "The member is on hold: ..." A member on hold cannot open an account.
  • "Product Special Savings is not open to this member type." The product is limited to other member types on Savings & Deposit Products.
  • "Choose an active product."
  • "Checks of ... are still clearing on the account. Clear or return them first." and "The closing fee is more than the balance."
  • "Account ... has transactions (voided ones included), so it is kept. Close it instead." Delete Account is for an account opened by mistake, before anything was posted to it.
  • "Release the holds on the account first."
  • "The time deposit is still active." A time deposit account is closed by closing its placement on the Time Deposit Center.
  • "Enter the reason for closing.", "Enter the reason for reopening." and "Enter the reason for the restriction."
  • A dormant account becomes active again with the member's next deposit or withdrawal.
  • A user limited to one branch sees only the accounts of that branch.

Savings Transaction Center

The Savings Transaction Center lists every savings and time deposit transaction the tellers make, in one transaction number series: deposits, withdrawals, check deposits, memos, error corrections, returned checks, time deposit placements and withdrawals, and accrued interest withdrawals. Each transaction gets the next number of the series (0000001, 0000002 ...) whatever its kind, so the numbers of a day run without a gap. Its toolbar opens each kind of transaction, as in the cooperative's former program.

Where to find it: Teller > Savings Transaction Center (under Savings & Deposits before version 33); every transaction of it is on the teller's Daily Collection & Disbursement Summary. The page opens with the transactions of today. A user who may open Savings Account Transaction may open this page too.

The toolbar

Button What it does
Savings Opens Savings Account Transaction: deposits and withdrawals by transaction code, check deposits and error corrections.
Time Deposit Opens the Time Deposit Center: placements, rollovers, closing and accrued interest withdrawals (CAIW).
Memo Opens Savings Account Transaction for a debit or credit memo: an adjustment against a GL account you choose (codes CM and DM).
Refund Opens Patronage Refund, computed and credited to the members' savings.
Interest Opens Post Savings Interest.
Dividend Opens Interest on Share Capital, credited to share capital.
R Check Opens Returned Checks: a deposited check dishonored by the bank (see below).
View Opens the transaction chosen with its journal entry; from there it is printed or voided. Double-clicking a transaction does the same.
Ledger Opens the account of the transaction chosen on the Savings Account Center.
Filter Shows the filters: Trans Code, Savings product, Teller and Show deleted transactions.
Print Prints the list as shown, with its totals.

Show Transactions is Today or Custom; with Custom, type the two dates. The search box finds a part of the member's name, an account number, a transaction number or a word of the memo, and the list follows as you type. The buttons a transaction needs (View, Ledger) are grey until you click a transaction.

The list

Column What it shows
Transact Date The date of the transaction, with the time it was entered.
Transaction no The number of the series. Newest first.
Trans Code The transaction code (CSD, CSW, CK3, ECSD, CTD, CAIW ...), set up on Savings Transaction Codes.
Account no The savings, share capital or time deposit account.
Savings Name The product of the account; a time deposit shows TD and its term in days (TD 180).
Member Name The member.
Amount The amount. An error correction shows negative (for example -700.00), as the passbook prints it.
Memo The memo, or the description of the code. "(deleted)" marks a deleted transaction (shown only with Show deleted transactions), "(back entry)" one entered on an earlier date.

The footer gives the number of transactions and the totals of the deposits, the withdrawals and the net. At most 1,500 transactions are listed; narrow the dates or the filter for more.

Returned checks (R Check)

R Check lists the check deposits of the last 30, 60, 90 or 180 days or a year (Deposited within), still clearing or cleared, that were not returned yet, with their cheque numbers. Search by member, account, cheque number or transaction number.

To record a returned check:

  1. Click R Check and find the deposit.
  2. Click Return. Check the Date returned and the Transaction code (RTC, Returned Check, or another debit code).
  3. When the check was already cleared, choose the Bank account that charged it back. While it is still clearing nothing has to be chosen: the amount comes out of the checks clearing account.
  4. Type the Reason from the bank's advice, choose the Class and click Record the returned check.

The member's account is debited with the next transaction number. The entry is: debit the member's savings product account, credit the checks clearing account of the deposit's code (still clearing) or the bank account (cleared). A check still clearing stops floating. When the debit would overdraw the account, a supervisor approves.

What it records

Every transaction of the center is one voucher of the type Savings Transaction, numbered with the transaction number, and one entry in the member's account ledger with the same number. Cash deposits and withdrawals are counted in the teller's shift on Teller Shift & Cash Count, in the cash of the drawer the teller works with. The voucher is listed under the transaction number on Find and on the teller reports (deposit slips and withdrawal slips).

Good to know

  • The number series is set on Document Numbering (Savings transaction). The same series numbers the time deposit placements, withdrawals and accrued interest withdrawals; the tellers of every branch share it.
  • A transaction is deleted (voided) from its view or from Savings Account Transaction, with the reason. A transaction already printed in the passbook is corrected with an error-correct code instead.
  • A user limited to one branch sees the transactions of that branch only.

Savings Account Transaction

The teller's form for one savings, special savings or share capital account, laid out as in the cooperative's former program: the account number at the top, the member and savings information on the left, the transaction on the right. The transaction code decides the entry: which side of the account it goes on, and what the other side of the journal entry is (the teller's cash, a GL account, the checks clearing account, or the GL account chosen on a memo). Every transaction saved here gets the next number of the Savings Transaction Center series.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Savings Account Transaction, Savings on the Savings Transaction Center, and Savings transaction on an account of the Savings Account Center. Memo on the center opens the same form for debit and credit memos (titled Savings Memo (Debit / Credit Memo)).

The account

Type the Account no, the passbook number or part of the member's name, and choose the account from the list (the binoculars button opens the Savings Account Center). The state of the account shows on the right in large letters: ACTIVE, FOR APPROVAL, DORMANT or CLOSED. A time deposit account is sent to the Time Deposit Center.

Box What it shows
Member ID, Member Type, Member Name The member of the account. The card button opens Member Information. A member on hold or with an alert note shows the hold or the note under the name.
Savings Code, Savings Name The product of the account.
Current Balance The ledger balance.
Withdrawable Balance What can be withdrawn now: the balance less the checks still clearing, the holds and the minimum balance of the product.
Floating Amount Check deposits still clearing. Clear Cheque opens Check Clearing with the account.
Restricted Amount The amounts on hold, or the whole available balance when the withdrawals of the account are restricted (the reason shows under the notes).
Savings Notes A note on the account, shown to every teller. Save notes saves it (Edit right on the Savings Account Center).

The transaction

Field What to enter / what it means
Transaction no The next number of the series, in grey. It is given when you click Save.
Error Correct Ticked, the Transaction type lists the error-correct codes only, and the transaction entered by mistake is chosen (see below).
Transaction type The transaction code, for example CSD - Cash Savings Deposit, CSW - Cash Savings Withdrawal or CK3 - Local Check Deposit 3 Days Clearing. The line under it tells what the code does, for example "Deposit: credited to the account, cash: your drawer 1007 - Cash on Hand - Cooperative Teller". The codes are set up on Savings Transaction Codes.
Transaction entered by mistake With Error Correct: the transaction to negate, from the account's transactions of the code the error-correct code corrects. The amount is filled in: an error correction negates the whole transaction.
Amount The amount of the transaction.
Date Today. With Back Entry ticked, an earlier date.
GL account Memos only: the GL account on the other side of the entry.
Memo A remark kept with the transaction and printed on the voucher. It replaces the code's description in the ledger.
Class The class of the voucher (required).
Cheque Deposit Details For a check deposit code: one line for each cheque, with Cheque No, Bank Name, Date and Amount. Add a cheque adds a line. The cheques must add up to the amount. The boxes are open only for a check deposit code.
Print to Passbook Ticked, the passbook printing window opens after Save. The tick is remembered on the computer.
Show Preview Ticked, the machine validation of the deposit / withdrawal slip is shown after Save, to print on the slip. The tick is remembered on the computer.
Back Entry Ticked, the transaction is recorded on an earlier date (the Date box opens to earlier dates). How far back is the policy "Days a transaction may be back-dated" on Cooperative Profile & Policies, or any date with the permission to record back-dated member transactions.
Supervisor approval For what needs a supervisor (see Good to know).

Under the form: Teller: with your name and ID, the buttons Delete, Clear and Save, and a status line with the account state, the cash account you transact with, the workstation (your shift and drawer), the date and the time. Latest transactions lists the last 12 transactions of the account with their numbers, codes, amounts, balance, teller ID and whether they are printed in the passbook.

Step by step

To receive a cash deposit:

  1. Open your teller shift on Teller Shift & Cash Count if it is not open.
  2. Type the Account no or the member's name and choose the account.
  3. Choose CSD - Cash Savings Deposit in Transaction type and type the Amount.
  4. Choose the Class. Leave Print to Passbook and Show Preview ticked.
  5. Click Save and confirm. The message "Transaction 0000010 saved." appears.
  6. The Machine Validation window shows the validation. Insert the deposit slip in the printer and click Print. The validation opens in its own window and prints; once it is printed, that window closes by itself and you are back on Savings Account Transaction.
  7. The Print to Passbook window tells the page and the line to put the passbook at. Click Print; when every line printed in the right place, click Yes, mark them printed. When the lines not yet printed go on to the next page of the passbook, they all print in the same run: see Lines on two or more pages.
  8. Once the passbook is marked printed, the form is cleared for the next transaction: the account box is empty and a note says which account was last used, with Open ... again to bring it back.

With Print to Passbook unticked, the form is cleared as soon as the validation is printed; the note says "Transaction 0000010 (CSD 500.00) is printed. The form is clear for the next transaction."

To pay out a withdrawal:

  1. Choose the account and CSW - Cash Savings Withdrawal, and type the Amount. It must not be more than the Withdrawable Balance.
  2. Click Save. A withdrawal above your limit or the policy's threshold, from a dormant account, from share capital or from a product that requires it, asks for a supervisor in Supervisor approval.
  3. Print the validation on the withdrawal slip and update the passbook.

To receive a check deposit:

  1. Choose the check deposit code, for example CK3 - Local Check Deposit 3 Days Clearing, and type the Amount.
  2. Fill in a line of Cheque Deposit Details for each cheque. The cheques must add up to the amount.
  3. Click Save. The deposit is in the Floating Amount until it is cleared on Check Clearing after its clearing days.

To correct a transaction entered by mistake (Error Correct):

  1. Open the account and tick Error Correct.
  2. Choose the error-correct code of the transaction, for example ECSD - Error Correct Cash Savings Deposit for a CSD deposit.
  3. Choose the transaction in Transaction entered by mistake. The amount is filled in.
  4. Type the reason in Memo and click Save.

The correction is a new transaction with its own number. It negates the erroneous one in full: the amount shows negative in the same column (a deposit corrected shows -700.00 under Deposit), and the journal entry of the erroneous transaction is reversed. The passbook prints both lines. A transaction is corrected once; a correction is not corrected again (enter the transaction again instead). Correcting another teller's transaction, or one of an earlier day, needs a supervisor.

To enter a debit or credit memo:

  1. Click Memo on the Savings Transaction Center.
  2. Choose the account, CM - Credit Memo (Adjustment) or DM - Debit Memo (Adjustment), and the GL account on the other side.
  3. Type the Amount and the Memo, and click Save. A memo needs a supervisor unless you may approve on the page.

To delete a transaction entered by mistake:

  1. Click Delete. The window lists the account's transactions not yet printed in the passbook.
  2. Choose the transaction, type the Reason and click Delete.

The transaction is voided: it stays on file marked void, and the account and the books are reversed. Your own transaction of the day is deleted at once; another teller's or an earlier day's needs a supervisor. A transaction already printed in the passbook is not deleted: correct it with Error Correct, so the passbook keeps both lines.

Update Passbook opens the passbook printing window for the lines not yet printed (the number in brackets: every line still to print, on every page). Reprint Validation lists the latest transactions; click one to print its validation again (a reprint asks for the reason and is counted). Clear empties the transaction boxes.

Batch Deposit and Batch Withdrawal

Batch Deposit and Batch Withdrawal on the title bar post deposits or withdrawals to many accounts at once from a CSV file with two columns, Account no. and Amount (and a Memo of the row if you wish). Each row becomes one transaction of the Savings Transaction Center, with its own transaction number, exactly as if it were entered on this form.

Field What to enter
Transaction code A deposit code for a batch deposit, a withdrawal code for a batch withdrawal. A code on the teller's cash (User Account) needs your teller shift open; a code with a GL account (for example a payroll deduction or release account) does not.
Date Today, or an earlier date for back entries (the policy on back-dated member transactions applies).
Class Required: the class of every entry.
Memo of the batch The description of every row that has no memo of its own.

The file is read for review as on Import Member. Each row shows the member, the product, the balance and, for a withdrawal, what can still be withdrawn. A row is refused when the account is not on file, is a time deposit or closed, or belongs to another branch, or when the amount is not more than zero. For a withdrawal the amount must be within what is withdrawable, counting the rows above it on the same account and the amounts on hold; a restricted account is refused. Click Post (for example Post 6 withdrawals): every row is posted, or none if one still has a problem. A batch is posted once: a refresh, Back or a second tab does not post it again ("This form was already submitted ..."). A withdrawal that needs a supervisor (a dormant account, share capital, or above your limit) is noted on its row and approved under Supervisor approval below the grid with the supervisor's username and password, once for the batch; a one-time approval code covers one transaction only.

Lines on two or more pages

When the lines not yet printed do not fit on the page the passbook is at, Print to Passbook (and Update Passbook) prints them all in one run, page after page. You do not press Update Passbook again for the rest.

  1. The window lists the pages: for example Page 1, lines 21 to 26, and Page 2, lines 1 to 6, header first. Put the passbook in the printer at the first page and click Print the 2 pages.
  2. The first page prints. The print window then asks Turn the passbook to page 2: take the passbook out, turn it to page 2, put it back in open at that page and click Print page 2. Its header prints first on a new page, then line 1. With more pages, the window asks again for each page.
  3. If a page came out wrong (a paper jam), click Print page 1 again before you go on.
  4. After the last page the print window closes by itself. Back on the form, the question Did every line print in the right place? is waiting. Choose Yes, every page and click Yes, mark them printed.

When only the first pages printed right, choose for example Only page 1; page 2 prints again later: page 1 is marked printed and page 2 prints with the next Update Passbook. A page that came out wrong can also be printed again alone from the question (Print page 2 again). Nothing is marked printed until you answer the question.

The machine validation

The validation prints two lines on the deposit or withdrawal slip: the transaction number, the account number, the date and time; then the member's name, the transaction code, the amount (negative for an error correction) and the teller ID. Where it prints on the slip is set under Deposit / withdrawal slip validation on Passbook Types. The first print is the original; each later print is a counted copy. The validation window closes by itself once it is printed, and you are back on the form (opened on its own, it goes to an empty Savings Account Transaction).

What it records

One Savings Transaction voucher numbered with the transaction number, and one entry in the account ledger with the code and the same number:

Code Debit Credit
Deposit (credit side), for example CSD The other side of the code: your cash account (User Account), the GL account of the code, or the checks clearing account of a check deposit code The GL account of the savings product, under the member's name
Withdrawal (debit side), for example CSW The GL account of the savings product, under the member's name The other side of the code
Credit memo (CM) / debit memo (DM) The GL account chosen (CM) or the savings product (DM) The savings product (CM) or the GL account chosen (DM)
Error correction The reverse of the transaction corrected The reverse of the transaction corrected

User Account is the cash account of the teller: the drawer of the open shift, else the cash account of the user on User Accounts, else the cash account of the user's profile on User Role Configuration. Cash deposits and withdrawals count in the shift's cash and are listed as deposit and withdrawal slips on the teller reports.

Good to know

  • "The transaction is dated today. Tick Back Entry to record it on an earlier date." and "A back entry is dated before today."
  • "Choose the transaction to correct (the erroneous one) from the list of the account.", "That transaction was already corrected." and "An error correction negates the whole transaction: the amount is ...".
  • "Code ECSD corrects CSD transactions, and the transaction chosen is CSW." Choose the error-correct code of the transaction's own code.
  • "Enter the cheque details (cheque no., bank, date and amount) of the check deposit." and "The cheques (...) must add up to the amount (...)."
  • "Choose the GL account of the memo (the other side of the entry)."
  • "No cash account is assigned to you or your profile (System Administration > User Accounts or User Role Configuration > Cash account)." and "Open your teller shift first (Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count)." A User Account code takes cash from your drawer.
  • "Transaction 0000012 is printed in the passbook. Correct it with Error Correct instead (the passbook keeps both lines)."
  • A deposit above the large transaction threshold, a withdrawal above your limit or the threshold, from a dormant account, from share capital or from a product that requires approval, a memo, and a correction of another teller's or an earlier day's transaction need a supervisor unless you have the Approve right on the page.
  • Savings deposits, share capital and withdrawals are no longer received on the official receipt: they are made here, so each has its transaction number. Loan payments are received on Loan Collection.

Transfer Between Accounts

A transfer moves money from one savings or share capital account to another without cash: from a member's savings to their share capital, between two of their accounts, or to another member's account. It is posted as one cooperative journal voucher, so it needs no teller shift.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Transfer Between Accounts.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
From account no. The account the money comes out of. When you leave the box, the member, member number, product and available balance show under it.
To account no. The account the money goes into. The same line shows under it.
Date The date of the transfer. Today by default. It cannot be in the future.
Amount The amount transferred.
Reference An outside reference, for example the member's written request.
Memo Added to the memo of the voucher and to the description of both ledger entries.
Supervisor approval For the transfers that need a supervisor (see below). See Supervisor approval.

The account the money comes out of is checked like a withdrawal: its available balance, the product's minimum balance and minimum withdrawal, a restriction or lock-in, and whether the product allows withdrawals. The receiving account may be waiting for approval, but not closed, and must stay within its product's maximum balance.

When a supervisor is asked

A supervisor with the Approve right on Transfer Between Accounts must approve a transfer:

  • to another member's account;
  • out of share capital;
  • out of a product whose withdrawals need approval (Every withdrawal needs a supervisor on the product);
  • above your withdrawal limit (the special permission "Cooperative: max savings withdrawal without supervisor approval").

A user who has the Approve right on the page approves their own transfers. The approver's name is recorded on the voucher.

To transfer between accounts:

  1. Type the From account no. and the To account no., and check the names and products that show under them.
  2. Check the Date and type the Amount, the Reference and the Memo.
  3. If a supervisor is needed, open Supervisor approval for their username and password or an approval code.
  4. Click Transfer and confirm. The voucher opens, with the message "Transferred ... from ... to ...".
Example

Pedro Bautista asks to move ₱1,000.00 from his Regular Savings 01100004011 to his share capital 01100004031. Both accounts are his and the money does not leave share capital, so no supervisor is needed unless ₱1,000.00 is above the teller's withdrawal limit.

The voucher view shows the voucher number and date, the member, the reference and memo, who approved and recorded it, the General ledger entry and the Member ledgers lines, with Print, Void and Back. Recent transfers on the right lists the last 15 transfers; click a number to open one. A voided transfer is struck through.

What it records

A Cooperative Journal Voucher with the memo "Transfer (from account) to (to account)":

Account Debit Credit
The GL account of the product of the From account (for example 2300 Savings Deposits), under that member's name Amount
The GL account of the product of the To account (for example 3010 Paid-up Share Capital - Common), under that member's name Amount

The From account gets a TRO (Transfer Out) entry and the To account a TRI (Transfer In) entry. When both products use the same GL account, the debit and the credit fall on the same account under the two names. To undo a transfer, void its voucher; it cannot be voided while the void would overdraw the receiving account.

Good to know

  • "Enter both account numbers." One of the numbers was not found.
  • "Choose two different accounts."
  • "Supervisor approval is required: transfer to another member's account. ..." The text after the colon can also be "transfer out of share capital", "transfer out of ... (withdrawals need approval)" or "transfer above your withdrawal limit".
  • "Insufficient available balance on ...", "Account ... is restricted: ..." and the other withdrawal messages of Savings Account Transaction apply to the From account.

Post Savings Interest

This page credits interest to the savings and share capital accounts of one product for a period, as one voucher for the product. The same page runs the dormancy process: it marks accounts dormant after the product's months without a member transaction, and can charge the dormancy fee. Time deposit interest is not posted here; it is accrued and paid on Time Deposit Center.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Post Savings Interest.

Interest for a period

Field What to enter / what it means
Product The product to post. Active ordinary savings, special savings and share capital products are listed.
From, To The period. They start as the last full calendar quarter, for example 07/01/2026 to 09/30/2026.
Posting date The date of the voucher and of the interest entries in the account ledgers. It starts as the To date.

For each active or dormant account of the product, the program works out:

  • the balance the interest is computed on, set by Computed on on the product: the average daily balance, the lowest balance of the period or the ending balance. An account opened during the period counts from its opening day;
  • the rate: the rate tier of the product whose balance range holds that balance, with the latest effective date on or before the end of the period;
  • the interest: balance × rate ÷ 100 × days in the period ÷ days in a year (365 or 360, from the product);
  • the withholding tax: the interest × the product's withholding tax rate, and the net credited.

An account earns nothing when the balance is zero, when no rate tier applies, or when its lowest balance during the period fell below the product's maintaining balance (this last rule does not apply when the product computes on the ending balance). Interest below 0.01 is not posted. The run covers the accounts of the branches you can see.

Example

An account of Regular Savings has an average daily balance of ₱12,000.00 from 07/01/2026 to 09/30/2026 (92 days). The tier for 10,000.00 and over is 1.50 % a year, and the product counts 365 days. The interest is ₱12,000.00 × 1.50 % × 92 ÷ 365 = ₱45.37.

To post the interest of a period:

  1. Choose the Product, type the From and To dates and check the Posting date.
  2. Click Preview. The table lists each account that earns interest: Account, Member, Basis (the balance used), Days, Rate, Interest, Tax and Net, with the totals.
  3. Check the figures. Change the dates and preview again if needed.
  4. Click Post the interest and confirm. The message gives the number of accounts, the interest and the tax withheld.

Interest runs on the right lists the runs posted: Product, Period, Accounts, Interest and the Voucher. A voided run is struck through. To undo a run, open its voucher and void it: the interest leaves the member ledgers and the general ledger together.

What it records

One Cooperative Journal Voucher per run, dated the posting date, with the memo "Interest posting: (product) (period)":

Account Debit Credit
The interest expense account of the product (or the module default Interest Expense on Savings Deposits) Total interest
The GL account of the product, one line per account under the member's name Net interest of the account
The withholding tax account of the product (or the module default Withholding Tax Payable (Expanded)), when tax is withheld Total tax

Each account ledger gets an INT entry for the interest and, when tax is withheld, a WTX entry for the tax.

Dormant accounts and dormancy fees

The lower card marks accounts dormant. It works on the active ordinary and special savings products that have Dormant after (months without a transaction) set on Savings & Deposit Products.

Field What to enter / what it means
As of The date of the run. Today by default.
Also charge the dormancy fees (once per fee period) Ticked, the dormancy fee of the product is also charged to the dormant accounts.
  • An active account with a balance becomes dormant when it has had no transaction for the product's number of months before the As of date. Interest (INT), withholding tax (WTX) and dormancy fees (DF) do not count as transactions. The account's "Dormant since" date is the As of date.
  • With the fees ticked, each dormant account with a balance is charged the product's Dormancy fee, but never more than its balance, once per month, quarter or year as set in Fee charged on the product. Running the process again in the same period does not charge the fee twice.
  • A dormant account becomes active again with the member's next transaction. A withdrawal from a dormant account needs a supervisor.

Click Run and confirm. The message gives the number of accounts marked dormant and the fees charged. The fees are one Cooperative Journal Voucher dated the As of date:

Account Debit Credit
The GL account of the product, under the member's name Fee
The dormancy fee income account of the product (or the module default Dormancy Fees) Fee

Each account charged gets a DF entry in its ledger.

Good to know

  • Anyone who can open the page can preview. Posting the interest and running the dormancy process need the Create right.
  • "Interest for Regular Savings through 09/30/2026 has already been posted." A period is posted once per product.
  • "A posted interest run for Regular Savings already covers part of this period. Void that run first, or start this period after its end."
  • "No account earns interest for this period."
  • The posting date follows the date rules of member transactions: not in the future, not on or before the closing date, and back-dated only as allowed. The same rules apply to the As of date when fees are charged.
  • The Interest Posting Register lists the runs and, if you choose, the interest of each account.

Passbook Printing

This page prints the member's savings passbook or loan passbook: the ledger entries not yet printed go on the next lines of the passbook, starting on line 1 of a new passbook. When they go on to the next page, every page prints in the same run: the print window asks you to turn the passbook to the next page. Printing is done in two steps: you print the pages, then you confirm that they came out right. Only then are the lines marked as printed, so a paper jam or a misfeed is simply printed again.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Passbook Printing. Passbook and Update Passbook on the Savings Account Center, Print to Passbook and Update Passbook on Savings Account Transaction and Loan Collection, Update Passbook on the Loan Accounts Center and Print To Passbook on the Loan Release Form print the account's lines too.

Choose Savings / share capital or Loan, type the Account no. or the Loan no. and click Open. The account must have a passbook type: on the account or its savings product, or on the loan product. The head of the page shows the account, the member, the product, the Passbook type, the Next line (page and line), the entries Waiting to print and the Lines of a page (for example "1 to 29, 13-14 skipped").

To print lists the entries waiting, page by page (for example "Page 1: 9 lines, continued on page 2" and "Page 2: 8 lines, the header prints first (a new page)"), each with its Line and Date; a savings passbook with the Code, Deposit, Withdrawal, Balance and Teller; a loan passbook with the Reference No., Release, Principal, Balance, Interest, Fines and Teller. "The header prints first (a new page)" tells you when the header prints before line 1. Under the list, the box of the run says where to put the passbook and has the Print button; under it, a preview shows each page as it prints. When nothing is waiting, the page says "Everything is printed." with the next line.

What the passbook prints

  • A savings passbook line: the line number beside the date, the transaction code, the deposit (or the interest) or the withdrawal, the balance and the teller ID.
  • A loan passbook line: the line number beside the date, the reference no. (the P.N. no. of the release, the OR no. of a payment), the release, the loan payment (principal), the loan balance, the interest payment, the fines / penalties / surcharges payment and the teller ID. The release is on line 1. The charges of the loan (penalties, adjustments, waivers, interest accrual) are not passbook lines.
  • An error correction prints negative in the columns of the transaction it corrects, for example -700.00 under the deposits, or EC and the negative payment on a loan passbook.
  • The header prints on a new page before line 1: the account name and number of a savings passbook; the Loan Type, the Term and the Due Date of a loan passbook (as the passbook type says: on a new page, with every printing, or never).
  • The lines from Start Line Skip (the centre fold) are left blank: with 13 and 2 skipped lines, after line 12 the next entry prints on line 15. After Max Lines, the next entry goes to line 1 of the next page, after the header.
  • Each field prints where the design of the passbook type puts it (Passbook Types).

Step by step

To print the passbook:

  1. Open the account or the loan.
  2. Put the passbook in the printer, open at the page shown in Next line.
  3. Click Print (Print the 2 pages when the lines go on to the next page). The print window opens with the note "Put the loan passbook of 01-6-0000002 in the passbook printer, open at page 1, then print. Printing starts on line 21." and prints the first page. Only the passbook header and lines are printed, on one sheet: no title, page heading, address or page number.
  4. With more pages, the print window then asks Turn the passbook to page 2: take the passbook out, turn it to page 2, put it back in and click Print page 2. The next page starts on line 1, after the header. Print page 1 again prints the page before once more (a paper jam).
  5. After the last page the print window closes by itself.
  6. Check the passbook. If every line is in its place, answer Yes, every page and click Yes, mark them printed. The lines are marked as printed on their pages, and Next line moves on.

If a page did not print right, print it again with Print page ... again under the question, or choose Only page 1; page 2 prints again later to mark only the pages that came out right. No, print again later marks nothing.
