Cover, header and footer: Print the cover prints the inside cover, Print the header of page ... prints the header alone (a page whose header did not print), Print the footer prints the footer of the current page. Each prints at its place on the passbook type and does not move the print line; each print is logged.

To print lines again (after a misprint found later):

  1. In Print lines again, choose From the line: the list shows the last printed lines as page, line, date and code (or the kind and OR no. of a loan entry).
  2. Type the Reason, for example "Paper jam".
  3. Click Print again from there. That line and every line printed after it are waiting to print again, starting on the same page and line.

Reset the print line: after a paper jam or a misprint, type the Page and Line where the next line prints and, if lines must print again, Print again from (the transaction from which they print again), with the Reason. A skipped line cannot be the next line. The same is on the right-click menu of the Savings Account Center (Reset Passbook Print Line), with Print Passbook Cover, Reprint Passbook (a period printed again at its own lines, with the header of a page reprinted from its first line) and Print Passbook Footer.

To start a new passbook (full, lost or damaged):

  1. In New passbook, type the New passbook no. (savings accounts only; left blank, the number stays) and the Reason.
  2. Click Issue a new passbook and confirm. Printing starts again on page 1, line 1, with the header; the entries already printed stay marked as printed.

What it records

Passbook printing posts nothing to the books. Each confirmed page, header, cover, footer, reprint and new passbook is kept in the passbook print log and the audit trail, with the reason for reprints and new passbooks.

Good to know

  • Confirming a printed page and printing lines again need the Print/Export right on the page; resetting the print line and issuing a new passbook need the Edit right.
  • "The product of 01100001011 has no passbook type. Set one on the product or on the account." Choose the passbook type on Savings & Deposit Products, Loan Products or with Passbook details on the account.
  • "Line 13 is never printed on (Start Line Skip and No. of Line Skip of the passbook type). Choose another line."
  • "The passbook was printed by someone else in the meantime. Reload the page."
  • "The passbook lines changed after they were printed (another teller printed them, or an earlier-dated entry came in). Print the passbook again." Nothing is marked; print the run again.
  • "Enter the reason for reprinting." and "Enter the reason for the new passbook."
  • Voided entries are not printed.

Time Deposit Center

A time deposit is money a member places for a fixed term at a fixed rate. The Time Deposit Center lists the placements with a toolbar, as in the cooperative's former program: new placements with their certificate, rollovers at maturity, closing (paid out, credited to savings or pre-terminated), withdrawals of the accrued interest during the term (code CAIW), and the monthly accrual of the interest expense. Each placement, withdrawal and closing paid in cash gets the next number of the Savings Transaction Center series and is listed there.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Time Deposit Center, and Time Deposit on the Savings Transaction Center. The certificate numbers on the time deposit reports and the placement line on a time deposit account open a placement here.

Note

The interest earned so far can be withdrawn during the term with Withdraw Accrued (code CAIW); the rest is paid at settlement. The monthly accrual only books the interest expense as it is earned.

The toolbar and the list

Button What it does
Time Deposit Opens the form of a new placement (see below).
Rollover For a matured time deposit: renews the principal (the interest paid out) or the principal plus the interest, for a new duration.
Close Closes the time deposit chosen: paid out, or credited to a savings account of the member. Before maturity it is pre-terminated, which needs a supervisor.
Withdraw Accrued Pays out the interest earned so far, the principal staying (see below).
Ledger Opens the time deposit: its certificate, accruals, interest withdrawals and ledger. Double-clicking a row does the same.
Filter Status (Active, Paid, Renewed, Pre-terminated, Cancelled) and Maturing (within 7, 15 or 30 days).
Print Prints the list as shown.
Accrual Shows the monthly interest accrual (see below).
Import Time Deposit Places the time deposits of the former system, Active, from a CSV file (see Importing time deposits). Create right; uploading needs the Approve right.

Click a time deposit to choose it; the buttons that do not apply to it stay grey. The search box finds a member, a certificate or an account number as you type.

Column What it shows
Date Opened The placement date.
Certificate No. The certificate number.
Member Name The member.
Duration The term in days.
Maturity Date The maturity date. A time deposit past its maturity shows it in red.
Principal Amount The amount placed.
Accrued Interest The interest earned to date, less what was already withdrawn.
Status Active, Paid, Renewed, Pre-terminated or Cancelled.

The footer gives the number of time deposits and the totals of the principal and the accrued interest.

Importing time deposits (Import Time Deposit)

Like Import Savings, for the time deposits of the former system, one row per placement: each is placed Active with its certificate, term, rate, date opened and maturity date, and its principal is entered with the savings transaction code chosen. Import the members first.

Where to find it: Import Time Deposit on the toolbar of the Time Deposit Center, after Accrual.

Field or column What to enter
Savings transaction code, Time deposit product, Posting date (cut-off) On the page. The code and the product fill the rows that leave them blank; the principal is entered on the posting date.
Member code, Member name Required, as for savings.
Time deposit term Required. In days (180), months (6 months, 6M) or years (1 year, 1Y). It is written on the placement in words, for example "6 months".
Amount Required. The principal placed, within the product's minimum and maximum.
Date opened, Maturity date Required. The maturity date must be after the date opened. When the term ends on another day, the maturity date given is kept, with a note.
Rate % a year Blank: the rate of the product for the amount and term.
Certificate no. The certificate of the former system (blank: the next number). A certificate already on file, or twice in the file, is refused.
At maturity, Accrued interest What happens at maturity: payout (also when blank), renew_principal, renew_all or credit_savings (in the grid: Pay out, Renew the principal, Renew with the interest, Credit to savings); and the interest already booked as payable at the posting date.

The rows are read for review and uploaded as on Import Member. Besides the checks of Import Savings, a row is refused when the product is not open to the member's type, the date opened is after the posting date, the amount is below the product's minimum placement or above its maximum, or no rate is set up for the amount and term and the file gives none. A placement already matured at the posting date is imported with a note and settled on the Time Deposit Center afterwards. The posting date follows the policy on back-dated member transactions and the closing date. A placement opened before the posting date with no Accrued interest is noted: the interest it earned before the posting date is booked by the next accrual run. A time deposit is placed for an active member (not an applicant).

What it records: one cooperative journal voucher for each code: the time deposits account credited with the principal, the interest payable account with the accrued interest given, and the code's GL account debited with both. Each placement can then be accrued, matured, rolled over or pre-terminated as one placed here.

Withdrawing the accrued interest (CAIW)

To pay out the accrued interest of a time deposit:

  1. Open your teller shift on Teller Shift & Cash Count: the interest is paid from your drawer.
  2. Click the time deposit and Withdraw Accrued. The window TD Accrued Withdrawal shows the Certificate No., the Time Deposit (TD and the duration), the Member Name and the Accrued Interest.
  3. Type the Withdraw Amount, at most the accrued interest, choose the Class, and click OK.

The withdrawal gets the next number of the Savings Transaction Center series with the code CAIW and is listed there. The time deposit account shows the interest credited (TDI) and withdrawn (CAIW); the principal stays. The entry is: debit the accrued interest payable for the part already accrued, debit the interest expense for the rest, credit your cash account. At settlement only the interest not yet withdrawn is paid. Voiding the withdrawal puts the interest back.

The withdrawal is deducted from the accrued interest: the time deposit's Interest accruals lists each accrual and each withdrawal with a Balance, and ends with the Accrued interest payable (for example 997.26 accrued, 500.00 withdrawn: 497.26). The tile Interest earned to date says how much is accrued in the books, accrued and withdrawn. The Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation, the Time Deposit Register and the Accrued Interest report use the same accrued interest payable.

Placing a time deposit

Click Time Deposit on the toolbar (Create right). The New placement form opens. Each placement opens its own account of the time deposit product for the member, so the account ledger shows the principal.

Field What to enter / what it means
Member The member placing the deposit. Only active members are found.
Product The time deposit product.
Principal The amount placed. It must be at least the product's opening / minimum placement and not above its maximum balance, when these are set.
Duration (days) The term in days. The box starts with the standard term of the first time deposit product on the list. The maturity date is the date opened plus these days.
Date opened The date the money is placed. Today by default.
Special rate % (blank = product tier) Leave blank to use the product's rate tier for the principal and the term in effect on the placement date. A rate typed here is a special rate and needs a supervisor.
Interest Simple or Compounded.
Compounded At maturity, Monthly or Quarterly. With Compounded and Monthly or Quarterly, the interest is added to the principal every 30 or 90 days, and the days left over earn simple interest on that amount. With At maturity the interest is simple.
At maturity What to do at maturity: Pay out principal and interest, Renew the principal, pay out the interest, Renew principal plus interest or Credit principal and interest to a savings account. It is the choice offered when the placement is settled.
Savings account to credit at maturity The member's ordinary or special savings account for the last choice. The list fills when the member is chosen and shows each account's available balance.
Money from Cash (teller drawer), Check (type the Check no. and Bank) or Transfer from the member's savings (choose the From savings account).
Certificate no. (blank = next) Leave blank for the next certificate number (the grey text shows it, for example TD-000003). A typed number must not be used already.
Supervisor approval For a special rate. See Supervisor approval.

To place a time deposit:

  1. For cash or a check, open your teller shift first.
  2. Choose the Member and the Product, and type the Principal.
  3. Check the Duration (days) and the Date opened. Type a Special rate % (blank = product tier) only for a rate other than the product's.
  4. Choose Interest, Compounded and At maturity, and the Savings account to credit at maturity if the deposit goes to savings at maturity.
  5. Choose Money from and fill in the check or the savings account.
  6. Click Place time deposit and confirm. The placement opens with the message, for example, "Time deposit placed (transaction 0000011). Print the certificate."
  7. Click Certificate to print the certificate.

The placement records:

Account Debit Credit
Cash: the cash account of your teller shift. Check: the account of code CKTD (in the sample, 1110 Checks and Other Cash Items). Transfer: the GL account of the savings product, under the member's name Principal
The GL account of the time deposit product (for example 2310 Time Deposits) Principal

Cash and check placements are a Savings Transaction numbered in the Savings Transaction Center series (a deposit slip on the teller reports); a transfer from savings is a Cooperative Journal Voucher, and the savings account gets a TRO entry. The time deposit account gets a CTD entry (CKTD for a check). A check placement waits on Check Clearing like any check deposit.

One placement

The placement shows the member panel and three figures: Principal (with the rate and the term), Maturity value (with the maturity date) and Interest earned to date (with the interest already accrued in the books). The details give the Certificate, the Account, Product, Placed, Interest (method and compounding), At maturity, Status, and, when they apply, Settled, Placement voucher, Renewed from, Renewed as, Certificate reprints and Notes. Interest accruals lists the accruals posted: Through, Posted, Amount and Voucher.

Certificate prints the TIME DEPOSIT CERTIFICATE: the cooperative heading, the certificate number and placement date, the text of the time deposit certificate template (Document Templates), the principal and the maturity value with its date, and the signature lines of the Treasurer and the General Manager (names from Cooperative Profile & Policies). The first print is the original; a reprint asks for the reason and is marked as a copy. A placement that is no longer active prints with its status across the certificate. Printing needs the Print/Export right on the page.

Monthly interest accrual

The Monthly interest accrual card books the interest the active placements have earned since their last accrual, as an expense and a payable, without paying anything to the member.

Field What to enter / what it means
Accrue through The date up to which the interest is accrued. It starts as the last day of the previous month.
Posting date The date of the voucher. It starts as the same day.

Click Preview to see each placement's Earned to date and To accrue, with the Total. Click Post accrual and confirm. The message gives the number of placements and the amount, or says "Nothing to accrue: every placement is accrued through that date." Running it again for the same date adds nothing. The interest earned never goes beyond the maturity date.

Account Debit Credit
The interest expense account of the product (or the module default Interest Expense on Time Deposits), under the member's name Interest earned since the last accrual
The accrued interest payable account of the product (or the module default Accrued Interest Payable on Deposits) The same amount

Settling at maturity and pre-terminating

Click the time deposit and Close or, at maturity, Rollover on the toolbar (Create right). The same choices are on the time deposit itself (Ledger): on or after the maturity date Settle at maturity; before it, Pre-terminate.

Field What to enter / what it means
Date The settlement date. Today by default; it cannot be in the future. A date before the maturity date pre-terminates the placement.
What to do One of the four At maturity choices. It starts with the choice made at placement.
Savings account to credit For Credit principal and interest to a savings account: the member's savings account.
New term (days) For the two renewals: the term of the new placement. It starts with the old term.
Remarks Recorded in the audit trail.
Supervisor approval Already open on a pre-termination, which needs a supervisor unless you have the Approve right on the page.

The interest paid is:

  • At maturity: the maturity value less the principal. For days held after the maturity date, the product's setting "Days held after maturity earn" adds interest on the maturity value at the placement rate, at a fixed rate, or nothing.
  • On pre-termination: simple interest from the placement date to the settlement date at the product's pre-termination rate (zero when none is set). The product's pre-termination fee is taken from the amount paid. The card shows the rate and the fee in yellow.

To settle a placement:

  1. Click the time deposit on the list (Filter, Maturing within 7 days shows the placements due soon) and Close or Rollover.
  2. Check the Date and choose What to do. Choose the savings account or the new term when asked.
  3. For a payout, make sure your teller shift is open: the cash is paid from your drawer.
  4. For a pre-termination, let the supervisor fill in Supervisor approval.
  5. Click Settle (or Pre-terminate) and confirm. The message is "Time deposit settled." On a renewal it adds "Renewed as a new placement." and the new placement opens.
  6. Print the voucher or the new certificate as needed.
Example

TD-000001 of Pedro Bautista is ₱100,000.00 at 4.00 % for 180 days, placed 07/01/2026, maturing 12/28/2026 with a maturity value of ₱101,972.60. Pre-terminated on 10/11/2026 (102 days) at the product's pre-termination rate of 1.00 %, the interest is ₱100,000.00 × 1.00 % × 102 ÷ 365 = ₱279.45. Less the fee of ₱100.00, the member receives ₱100,179.45.

The settlement records:

Account Debit Credit
The GL account of the time deposit product (for example 2310 Time Deposits) Principal
The accrued interest payable account Interest already accrued
The interest expense account of the product Interest not yet accrued
The pre-termination fee income account of the product (or the module default Pre-termination Fees) Fee (pre-termination only)
Pay out: the cash account of your teller shift. Credit to savings: the GL account of the savings product, under the member's name. Renew principal plus interest: the time deposit GL account Principal plus interest less the fee

With Renew the principal, pay out the interest, the principal is credited to the time deposit GL account for the new placement and the interest less the fee is paid in cash. When more interest was accrued than is paid (a pre-termination), the extra accrual is reversed: the payable is debited and the interest expense credited. A voucher that pays cash is a Savings Transaction of the Savings Transaction Center series (a withdrawal slip on the teller reports); otherwise it is a Cooperative Journal Voucher.

The time deposit account gets a CTDW entry for the principal and is closed. A savings account credited gets a TDM entry. The placement becomes Paid, Renewed or Pre-terminated. A renewal is a new placement with a new certificate number, at the product's rate tier in effect on the settlement date.

Good to know

  • Placing, settling and posting the accrual need the Create right; a special rate and a pre-termination need a supervisor unless you have the Approve right on the page.
  • "Pre-termination of Time Deposit needs supervisor approval (no pre-termination rate is set on the product)." Without a pre-termination rate on the product, only a user with the Approve right on the page can pre-terminate, and the interest is zero.
  • "The minimum placement for Time Deposit is ...", "The maximum placement for Time Deposit is ...".
  • "No interest rate is set up for Time Deposit at 100,000.00 for 180 days (Savings & Deposit Products > rates)." Add a rate tier that covers the amount and the term, or type a special rate.
  • "Certificate number ... is already used."
  • "Open your teller shift first (Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count)." Cash and check placements need an open shift.
  • "The time deposit is not active." It was already settled.
  • A placement voucher cannot be voided once the placement has accruals or has been settled; void the accrual vouchers first. Voiding a settlement makes the placement active again.

Batch Deductions (Payroll / CBU)

A batch posts a whole list of member payments at once: the payroll deductions an employer remits, the monthly capital build-up, or a collector's list. Each line is a deposit to a savings or share capital account, or a payment on a loan. The lines are checked first; nothing is posted until the checked batch is posted, as one voucher.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Batch Deductions (Payroll / CBU).

New batch

Field What to enter / what it means
Date The date of the batch and of its voucher.
Source Payroll deduction, Capital build-up, Collector's list or Other.
Money from (debit) The account debited for the total: 1230 Payroll Deductions Receivable until the employer remits (the default when the account exists), or the bank or cash account the money is in.
Description For example "DepEd payroll October 15". It goes into the memo of the voucher.
Product for lines given by member no. The product credited when a line gives a member number instead of an account number. Also the product used by the tick box below.
Or a CSV file A file of lines (CSV or text, up to 2 MB). Its lines are added to the ones typed.
Lines: account no., loan no. or member no., then the amount (one per line) One payment per line, for example "01100001031, 500.00" for a share capital account, "01-6-0000001, 1833.60" for a loan and "100004, 300" for a member number. The columns may be separated by commas, tabs or semicolons, and a third column is kept as the remarks. Lines starting with # are skipped. In the typed lines, a first line whose amount is not a number is taken as a heading and skipped.
Add every active member with an account of the product above, for / Amount per member Ticked, every active member who is not on hold and has an active or dormant account of the chosen product gets a line of the amount per member, for example the monthly CBU.
Tip

Type the amounts without a thousands separator (1833.60, not 1,833.60): a comma separates the columns. Lines pasted from a spreadsheet are separated by tabs. A heading row at the top of the file (such as member_no, amount, remarks) is skipped.

Step by step

To post a batch:

  1. Fill in the Date, Source, Money from (debit) and Description.
  2. Paste or type the lines, or choose the CSV file. For member numbers, choose the Product for lines given by member no.
  3. Click Check the lines. The batch opens as a draft. Nothing is posted yet.
  4. Look at Lines with errors. Lines with errors are listed first, in yellow, with the error in Remarks / error.
  5. Correct each error: change the reference or the amount in the boxes of the line and click Fix, or click Remove to take the line off.
  6. When there are no errors, click Post the batch and confirm. The message gives the voucher number.

The batch page shows the Lines, the Total, the Lines with errors and the Status (with the voucher once posted), and the lines: Reference, Account / loan, Member, Amount and Remarks / error. Print prints the BATCH DEDUCTIONS list with the signature lines Prepared by, Checked by and Approved by. Discard the draft drops a draft that will not be posted. The Batches list shows each batch with its date, description, lines, total and status (Draft, Posted or Voided).

Each line is checked for:

  • "The amount must be more than zero." and "Amount missing or not a number (line 3)".
  • "No account, loan or member 100099."
  • "Member number given: choose the product to credit, or give the account number." and "Member 100004 has no account of the chosen product."
  • "Account ... is closed.", "Member on hold.", "Account of another branch." and "Time deposits are placed on their own page."
  • "Loan ... is ..." (not open), "More than the payoff of loan ...", and "Loan ... is on the batch twice: put its payment on one line."

What it records

One Cooperative Journal Voucher dated the batch date, with the batch number as its reference:

Account Debit Credit
Money from (debit), for example 1230 Payroll Deductions Receivable Batch total
The GL account of each savings or share capital line's product, under the member's name Line amount
For each loan line: the loan accounts of the payment (penalty, interest, principal), as on a loan collection Line amount

Each savings line gets a PDD (Payroll Deduction Deposit) entry in its account ledger. Each loan line is applied to the loan like a collection, split in the "Payment allocation order" of Cooperative Profile & Policies (penalty, interest, then principal by default). When the policy "Compute penalties when a payment is received" is Yes, the penalties through the batch date are charged first. When the employer remits, record the money received against 1230 Payroll Deductions Receivable.

To undo a posted batch, void its voucher (open it from the Status figure): the batch becomes Voided and the member ledgers and loans are reversed with it.

Good to know

  • Creating and posting a batch need the Create right, fixing and removing lines the Edit right, and discarding a draft the Delete/Void right.
  • "... line(s) have errors. Correct them (or remove them) first."
  • "Only a draft batch is posted, and only once."
  • "Choose the account the money comes from (payroll deductions receivable, bank or cash)." and "Choose the product to fill the list from."
  • "The file is larger than 2 MB."
  • The batch date follows the date rules of member transactions when the batch is posted.

Check Clearing

A check deposited to a savings account (or placed as a time deposit) is in the balance at once but cannot be withdrawn until it clears. This page lists those checks. When the bank clears a check, you mark it cleared and the amount becomes available; when the bank returns it, you record the return and the deposit is taken back out of the account.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Check Clearing.

Due to clear lists the checks whose clearing date is today or earlier. All uncleared lists every check still waiting, with their number on the button. The clearing date is the deposit date plus the clearing days of the deposit code. A check is never cleared by itself on that date: it stays unavailable until it is marked cleared here.

Column What it shows
Deposited The date of the deposit.
Clears The clearing date, in bold when it is today or earlier.
Account, Member The account (click it to open the account) and the member.
Particulars The deposit code and description, and the official receipt number.
Amount The amount of the check.

To clear checks:

  1. Tick the checks the bank has cleared (the box in the heading ticks all).
  2. In Cleared by the bank, choose the bank account in Deposited to and check Date cleared.
  3. Click Clear and confirm. The message "... check(s) cleared; the amounts are now available to the members." shows.

To record a returned check:

  1. Tick the returned check.
  2. Check Date cleared: it is also the date of the return.
  3. In Returned by the bank, type the Reason, for example "Drawn against insufficient funds".
  4. Click Returned and confirm. The deposit is taken back out of the member's account.

A returned check of a savings deposit can also be recorded from R Check on the Savings Transaction Center, which numbers it in the savings transaction series. The Clear Cheque button of Savings Account Transaction opens this page for the account.

Checks received for loan payments and fees

A loan payment or a fee paid by check on an official receipt is applied to the loan at once, and the check waits in the checks and other cash items account. The second list of the page, Checks received for loan payments and fees, shows those receipts: Received, OR no. (click it to open the receipt), Member, Check (number and bank) and Waiting to clear.

To clear them: tick the checks, choose Deposited to and the Date cleared, and click Clear. The program posts a journal voucher: debit the bank account, credit checks and other cash items. When the bank returns such a check, void the official receipt with the reason (for example "Returned check: DAIF"): the payment comes off the loan and the check leaves the clearing account. A receipt whose check was already cleared is voided only after its clearing voucher is voided.

What it records

A cleared check is a Cooperative Journal Voucher dated the date cleared:

Account Debit Credit
The bank account chosen in Deposited to Amount
The account of the deposit code (in the sample, 1110 Checks and Other Cash Items) Amount

The amount leaves the Checks clearing figure of the account and becomes available. A returned check is a Cooperative Journal Voucher too:

Account Debit Credit
The GL account of the account's product, under the member's name Amount
The account of the deposit code (1110 Checks and Other Cash Items) Amount

The account ledger gets an RTC (Returned Check) entry with the reason.

Good to know

  • Clearing and returning checks need the Create right on the page.
  • "Tick the checks."
  • "Choose the bank account the checks were deposited to."
  • "Enter the reason the check was returned (e.g. DAIF, account closed)."
  • "That check deposit is not waiting for clearing." It was cleared, returned or voided in the meantime.

Savings & Deposit Products

A product is a kind of account the cooperative offers: regular savings, special savings, paid-up share capital, time deposits. The product sets the GL account the members' balances are kept in, the balance and withdrawal rules, dormancy, the interest and its rate tiers, and, for time deposits, the terms of a placement.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Savings & Deposit Products.

Column What it shows
Code, Product The product code and name. Click the name to open the product.
Type Ordinary savings, Special savings, Share capital (fixed savings) or Time deposit.
GL account The account the balances are kept in.
Rate from The rate of the lowest balance tier in effect today.
Accounts The number of accounts that are not closed.
Balance The total balance of the product's accounts.

An inactive product is greyed and marked "inactive". Click New product to add one.

Fields

The Product card:

Field What to enter / what it means
Code A short code, for example 01. It must not be used by another product. With the reference number format, the digits of the code are part of the account numbers.
Name The product name shown on the lists, passbooks and reports.
Type Ordinary savings, Special savings, Share capital (fixed savings) or Time deposit. It cannot change once the product has accounts.
Description A longer description.
GL account (deposit liability; share capital: equity) The account the members' balances are kept in, for example 2300 Savings Deposits. Left at Module default for the type: Savings Deposits (default liability), Time Deposits or Paid-up Share Capital - Common from Module Default Accounts. It cannot change while accounts of the product hold balances.
Interest expense account The account the interest is charged to. Blank: the module default (Interest Expense on Savings Deposits, or on Time Deposits).
Passbook type The passbook layout of the product's accounts, or No passbook.
Open to member types (none ticked: all) Tick the member types that may open the product. None ticked, every member type may.
Order on lists The order of the product on the lists.
Active (new accounts may be opened) Untick to stop opening new accounts of the product. An inactive product is not offered for new accounts, interest posting or the dormancy run.

The Balances and transactions card:

Field What to enter / what it means
Opening / minimum placement For a time deposit, the smallest principal that can be placed. The program does not check it when a savings account is opened.
Maintaining balance (to earn interest) An account whose lowest balance in the interest period went below this earns no interest for the period (not checked when the product computes on the ending balance).
Minimum balance after a withdrawal What must stay in the account after a withdrawal or transfer out, unless it is a closing withdrawal.
Maximum balance (0 = none) A deposit that would take the balance above this is refused. For a time deposit, the largest principal.
Minimum deposit The smallest deposit at the counter or on a batch. Transfers in and interest are not checked.
Minimum withdrawal The smallest withdrawal or transfer out.
Withdrawals allowed Unticked, withdrawals and transfers out of the product's accounts are refused.
Every withdrawal needs a supervisor Ticked, every withdrawal and transfer out needs supervisor approval.
Lock-in after opening (months) No withdrawal or transfer out until this many months after the opening date.
Dormant after (months without a transaction) Months without a member transaction before the dormancy run marks an account dormant. 0: never. Used for ordinary and special savings.
Dormancy fee The fee charged to a dormant account by the dormancy run, never more than the balance.
Fee charged Monthly, Quarterly or Yearly: the fee is charged once per calendar month, quarter or year.
Dormancy fee income The income account of the fee. Blank: the module default Dormancy Fees.

The Interest card:

Field What to enter / what it means
Computed on Average daily balance, Lowest balance of the period or Ending balance: the balance the interest of a period is computed on.
Method Simple or Compounded. Savings interest is posted as simple interest for each period on Post Savings Interest; a time deposit takes the method chosen on its placement form.
Days in a year 365 days or 360 days, used to turn the yearly rate into the interest of the days.
Withholding tax on interest (%) The tax withheld from the interest of each account when it is posted. The note under it reminds you that member deposits may be exempt under the cooperative's tax exemption: leave 0 unless the certificate of tax exemption says otherwise.
Withholding tax account The liability account of the tax. Blank: the module default Withholding Tax Payable (Expanded).

The Time deposit terms card (time deposits only):

Field What to enter / what it means
Standard term, Unit The usual term in Days, Months or Years. Required for a time deposit; the placement form starts with it in days.
Accrued interest payable The payable of the monthly accrual. Blank: the module default Accrued Interest Payable on Deposits.
Pre-termination rate (% a year; blank = not allowed without approval) The rate a year paid when a placement is ended before maturity. Blank: only a user with the Approve right on Time Deposits can pre-terminate, and no interest is paid.
Pre-termination fee The fee taken from the amount paid on pre-termination.
Fee income The income account of the fee. Blank: the module default Pre-termination Fees.
Days held after maturity earn The placement rate, Nothing or A fixed rate: the interest for the days between maturity and a later settlement.
Fixed rate after maturity (%) The rate for A fixed rate.

The Share capital card (share capital only) holds the Par value per share (used on the share capital certificate of a member who has no par value of their own), the Class of shares (Common or Preferred, kept with the product) and Earns interest on share capital: only the share capital products ticked here count when an Interest on Share Capital batch is computed (see Interest on Share Capital and Patronage Refund).

Interest rates by balance and term

The last card holds the rate tiers. Each row is one tier:

Column What to enter
Balance from, Balance to The balance range of the tier. Leave Balance to blank for "and over".
Term from (days), Term to (days) For time deposits, the term range of the tier. 0 and 0: any term.
Effective The date the rate takes effect.
Rate % a year The yearly rate.
Posted Monthly, Quarterly, Semi-annually, Annually or At maturity, kept with the rate for reference. Interest is posted when you run Post Savings Interest for the period you choose.

The tier whose balance range holds the account's balance (and, for time deposits, whose term range holds the term) applies; on a given date, the latest effective rate wins. A rate already in effect (its effective date before today) is shown in grey, marked "in effect", and stays on file as history. To change a rate, click Add a rate and add a row with the new rate and the date it starts. Rows dated today or later can be changed or removed. Placed time deposits keep the rate they were placed at.

Example

Regular Savings has two tiers effective 01/01/2020: 0.00 to 9,999.99 at 1.0000 % and 10,000.00 and over at 1.5000 %, posted quarterly. To raise the second tier to 1.75 % from 01/01/2027, add a row 10,000.00, blank, 0, 0, 01/01/2027, 1.75, Quarterly. Interest periods ending before 01/01/2027 keep 1.50 %.

To set up a product:

  1. Click New product.
  2. Type the Code and Name, and choose the Type. The cards for time deposits or share capital show when that type is chosen.
  3. Choose the GL account and the interest expense account, or leave the module defaults.
  4. Fill in the balance, withdrawal and dormancy rules.
  5. Fill in the interest settings and add the rate tiers.
  6. Click Save product.

Good to know

  • Adding a product needs the Create right; changing one the Edit right.
  • "Enter the product code and name." and "Product code 01 is already used."
  • "Enter the standard term of the time deposit."
  • "The minimum balance is above the maximum."
  • "The product already has accounts; its type cannot change."
  • "Accounts of this product hold balances in the current liability account. Move the balances with a journal voucher first, or keep the account."
  • "Each rate needs its effective date." and "A new rate takes effect today or later (rates already in effect are kept as history). Rate 1.25 % dated 10/01/2026."

Savings Transaction Codes

Every savings transaction has a transaction code, for example CSD for a cash deposit or INT for interest, and the code decides the journal entry: the side of the member's account, and the account on the other side. The code prints in the passbook and on the Savings Transaction Center. The page is titled Savings Transaction Code Setup: the form on the left, the codes on the right.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Savings Transaction Codes.

Column What it shows
Code, Description The code and its description. A check deposit code shows its clearing days; an error-correct code "negates" and the code it corrects.
Savings Entry CREDIT (into the account) or DEBIT (out of the account).
Opposite Account The GL account of the other side, "User Account" for the teller's cash, "chosen on the memo" for a memo code, or "set by the process" for the codes the program posts itself.
Used The number of transactions with the code.

Click a code to open it in the form. New code (or Clear) empties the form for a new one.

Field What to enter / what it means
Transaction Code New codes only: 1 to 10 letters or numbers. It cannot be changed later.
Description The description shown with the code.
Opposite Account GL Account and the account, for a fixed account (for example 1110 Checks and Other Cash Items for a check deposit code): the account box opens as soon as GL Account is chosen, also on a new code; or User Account, the cash account of the teller who transacts: the drawer of the open shift, else the cash account on the user, else the cash account of the user's profile (User Role Configuration).
Memo Ticked, the code is a debit or credit memo: the GL account is chosen on each memo (Memo on the Savings Transaction Center). A memo code has GL Account.
Savings Entry Side Debit (withdrawal: out of the account) or Credit (deposit: into the account).
Check Deposit, Clearing Days Ticked, a deposit with the code is floating (not available) for the clearing days, until it is cleared on Check Clearing; the cheque details are required on the deposit. A check deposit code needs its clearing days.
Is Error Correct, Related Trans Code Ticked, the code negates a transaction of the related code entered by mistake, for example ECSD for CSD. It has the same Savings Entry Side as the related code: the correction prints negative in the same column of the passbook.
Active Only active codes are offered on Savings Account Transaction.

On a code the program posts itself, the side and the opposite account cannot be changed. Delete deletes a code no transaction uses, that is not a system code and is not the related code of an error-correct code; otherwise untick Active.

Account closing codes (under the form): the Closing withdrawal code (CSW) and the Closing fee code (CF) that Close Account on the Savings Account Center posts. The closing withdrawal needs User Account or a GL account of its own; the closing fee needs GL Account, and with no account of its own it goes to Other Cooperative Income. Click Save the closing codes (Edit right).

To add a code of the former program, for example UBDD - Unionbank Direct Deposit or CHK3 - Local Check Deposit 3 Days Clearing: click New code, type the code and the description, choose GL Account and the account (the Unionbank account for UBDD, the checks clearing account for CHK3), Credit, and for CHK3 tick Check Deposit with 3 Clearing Days. Click Save. Add its error-correct code too (for example ECHK3: the same GL account, Credit, Is Error Correct, Related Trans Code CHK3) so a mistake with it can be corrected.

The codes of the sample set-up:

Code Used for
CSD, CSW Cash deposits and withdrawals on Savings Account Transaction (User Account).
CK3, CK4, CK5, CK7, CKTD Check deposits with 3, 4, 5 or 7 clearing days, and a time deposit placed by check (1110 Checks and Other Cash Items).
ECSD, ECSW, ECK3 ... ECKTD, ECM, EDM The error-correct codes of CSD, CSW, each check deposit code, CM and DM.
CM, DM Credit and debit memos (the GL account chosen each time).
RTC A returned check (R Check on the Savings Transaction Center, or Check Clearing).
CTD, CTDW, TDM, TDI, CAIW Time deposits: the placement, the principal leaving at settlement, the proceeds credited or renewed, the interest credited, and the cash accrued interest withdrawal.
CF The closing fee of an account closed on the Savings Account Center (the closing withdrawal is CSW).
TRO, TRI Transfer Between Accounts.
INT, WTX Post Savings Interest: the interest and the tax withheld.
DF, PDD, LPY, LP, LC, ISC, PR, OB, PTF The dormancy fee, batch deductions, a payment from savings, loan proceeds and charges, interest on share capital, patronage refund, opening balances and the pre-termination fee.

Good to know

  • Adding a code needs the Create right; changing one the Edit right.
  • "The transaction code is 1 to 10 letters or numbers." and "Code CK9 exists."
  • "Enter the description."
  • "Choose the GL account of the opposite side, or choose User Account."
  • "An error-correct code needs the related transaction code (the code it negates)." and "An error-correct code has the same Savings Entry Side as the code it negates (CREDIT for CSD): it is printed negative in the same column."
  • "Code ... is used by transactions, so it stays. Untick Active to stop using it."
  • "A check deposit code needs its clearing days."
  • "Savings transaction code ... is not set up (Savings & Deposits > Savings Transaction Codes)." A process needs a code that is missing.

Savings and deposit reports

The savings and deposit reports are listed under Savings & Deposits > Reports in the sidebar and in the Savings & Deposits card of the Report Center. The common report tools (the filters, Refresh, Modify Report, Memorize, printing and exporting, and clicking a number to open what is behind it) are explained in Working with reports. A user limited to one branch sees only that branch.

Savings List

Lists the savings, share capital and time deposit accounts with their status, holds, checks clearing, balance and available balance today.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Savings List.

Filters: Product, Status (left at All, closed accounts are left out), Branch, and Search (part of the account number or member name, the member number or the passbook number).

The columns: Account (click it to open the account), Member (click it to open the member), Member no., Product, Opened, Status, Passbook, Last transaction, On hold, Uncleared, Balance and Available, with totals of the amounts. Member no. and Passbook are hidden at first; switch them on with Columns.

Savings Journal

Lists the entries of the account ledgers for a period: every deposit, withdrawal, transfer, interest and fee. Use it to check a teller's entries of the day.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Savings Journal.

Filters: Dates (today by default), Teller / user (who recorded the entry), Product, Transaction code, Branch and Voided entries (Leave them out or Show them).

The columns: Date, Account, Member, Product (the code), Code, Description, OR / voucher (click it to open the voucher), Withdrawals, Deposits, Balance (the running balance of the account after the entry) and User. The withdrawals and deposits are totalled. A voided entry, when shown, starts with "VOID" and has no amounts.

Savings Account Balances

The balance of each account on a date, worked out from the account ledgers. Use it for the balances at a month end or year end.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Savings Account Balances.

Filters: As of, Product, Branch and Zero balances (Leave them out or Show them).

The columns: Account, Member, Member no., Product and Balance, with the total and a chart of the balances by product. The note says the balances are recomputed from the account ledgers as of the date, voided entries left out.

Schedule of Savings Accounts

One line per member with the member's balance in each product on a date and the total: the schedule of member deposits and capital that supports the balance sheet.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Schedule of Savings Accounts.

Filters: As of and Branch.

How to read it: Member and Member no., then one column per product that has balances (for example Regular Savings, Paid Up Share Capital - Common, Time Deposit) and the Total. Members with no balance are left out. The note compares the schedule with the GL accounts of these products on the same date, for example "The control accounts of these products show 693,835.19 in the general ledger as of 10/11/2026, the same as the schedule." When they differ, the schedule total shows in red.

Tip

When the schedule and the general ledger differ, run the Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation to find the difference.

Dormant Accounts

Lists the dormant savings accounts, and if you choose, the accounts that will soon become dormant, so the members can be reached first.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Dormant Accounts.

Filters: As of, Product (ordinary and special savings), Branch and Show (Dormant accounts, or Dormant and becoming dormant within 60 days). Only products with a dormancy period are included.

The columns: Account, Member, Product, Last transaction, Months inactive, Dormant from, Status and Balance. Status is Dormant for an account already marked dormant and Becomes dormant for an active account whose inactivity period ends by the date (or within 60 days after it, with the second choice).

Note

This report counts from the account's last transaction of any kind, interest included. The dormancy run on Post Savings Interest does not count interest and fees, so it can mark an account dormant that this report does not list yet.

Interest Posting Register

The interest runs posted on Post Savings Interest, with the interest, the tax withheld and the net credited, and if you choose, the interest of each account.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Interest Posting Register.

Filters: Dates (the posting dates; this fiscal year by default), Product and Show (Runs only or Each account).

How to read it: each run is a line with its posting date, product and period ("(VOIDED)" when its voucher was voided), the number of Accounts, the Interest, Withholding tax, Net credited and the Voucher. With Each account, the accounts follow each run with the Basis, Days, Rate %, interest, tax and net. The last line is the total, voided runs left out.

Time Deposits by Product

Lists the time deposit placements by product, with their terms, maturity values and the interest accrued in the books.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Time Deposits by Product.

Filters: Product, Status (Active and matured by default, or one status such as Paid out, Renewed, Pre-terminated or Cancelled), Branch and Search (certificate number, member name or member number).

The columns: Certificate (click it to open the placement), Member, Product, Principal, Rate %, Term, Placed, Maturity, Maturity value, Interest accrued, At maturity (hidden at first; switch it on with Columns) and Status. Principal, maturity value and interest accrued are totalled.

Time Deposit Maturity Schedule

The placements maturing in a period, month by month: the cash the cooperative needs on each maturity, for liquidity planning.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Time Deposit Maturity Schedule.

Filters: Dates (the maturity dates; this quarter by default), Product and Branch.

How to read it: the placements are grouped by month of maturity, each month with a total line. The columns are Certificate, Member, Mobile (hidden at first; switch it on with Columns), Maturity, Principal, Interest at maturity, Maturity value and At maturity (what the member chose). The last line is the total. Only active placements are listed.

Tip

Placements set to "renew principal plus interest" need no cash at maturity; those set to pay out need the full maturity value in the teller's drawer or the bank.

Time Deposit Accrued Interest

The interest the members have earned on their active time deposits up to a date, and how much of it is already accrued in the books.

Where to find it: Savings & Deposits > Reports > Time Deposit Accrued Interest.

Filters: As of and Product.

The columns: Certificate, Member, Principal, Rate %, Placed, Maturity, Earned to date, Accrued in the books and Not yet accrued, with totals.

Tip

The Not yet accrued total is what the monthly interest accrual on Time Deposit Center would post through the same date. After the accrual is posted, it is zero.

Savings Account Statement

The statement of one account for a period: the beginning balance, each entry with its running balance, and the ending balance. Print it for a member who asks for a statement.

Where to find it: Only in the Savings & Deposits card of the Report Center; it is not in the sidebar.

Filters: Dates (this fiscal year by default) and Search: type the account number. Until an account number is typed, the report says "Enter the account number in the search box."

How to read it: the first line is the Beginning balance on the From date. Each entry shows the Date, Code, Description, Reference (the OR or voucher number; click it to open the voucher), Withdrawals, Deposits and the Balance after it. The last line is the Ending balance, with the total withdrawals and deposits of the period. The note gives the account number, product, member and member number. Voided entries are left out.

Loan Application Center

The Loan Application Center lists the loan applications and shows where each one stands: new, waiting for the credit investigation, validated, waiting for approval, approved and waiting for release, or returned for correction. Start a new application here, and click an application to work on it.

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Application Center. The Applications button on Loan Accounts Center and on an application, and Application Center on Loan Release, also open it.

The tiles at the top count the applications by status: New, For credit investigation, Validated, Waiting for approval, Approved, to release and Returned. Click a tile to list only those applications.

The list

Filter What to enter / what it means
Find Part of the application number or of the member's name, or the full member number.
Status In process lists every application that is not released, cancelled or disapproved. Choose one status to list only those: New application, Validated, For credit investigation, For approval, Approved, Disapproved, Returned for correction, Released or Cancelled.
Product One loan product, or All products.

Click Show to apply the filters. All, including closed lists every application, released, cancelled and disapproved ones included.

Column What it shows
Application The application number, for example LA-000010. Click it to open the application.
Date The application date.
Member The borrower, with the member number below.
Product The loan product.
Applied, Approved The amount applied for, and the amount approved once an approver has acted.
Term The term and the mode of payment, for example "12 months, monthly".
Officer The loan officer of the application.
Status Where the application stands. An application waiting for approval shows how many levels have already approved, for example "1 level(s) approved".
Delete The Delete button of an application never released (see below).

Applications waiting for approval come first, then approved ones, then those for credit investigation, validated, returned and new; within each group the newest come first. Up to 300 applications are listed. On a phone each application is a card.

Deleting an application

Each application that was never released has a Delete button at the end of its row (users with the Delete right on the Loan Application Form).

  1. Click Delete on the row. The window Delete the loan application shows the application number and the member.
  2. Type the Reason (required, recorded in the audit trail), for example "Encoded twice".
  3. Click Delete. The message "Application LA-000012 of Garcia, Liza deleted." appears and the application is off the list. Keep it closes the window without deleting.

The application is removed with its co-makers, collateral, approvals, requirements and credit investigation. It cannot be undone: the application as it was is kept in the audit trail (with the reason), and its number is not used again. An uploaded requirement file stays in storage.

  • The application of a released loan cannot be deleted: "The loan of this application is released. A released loan and its application stay on record."
  • An application whose release was posted and later voided cannot be deleted either (the cancelled loan account still points to it): "Loan 01-2-0000010 was released from this application (the release was voided). Cancel the application instead."
  • To keep an application on record but stop it, cancel it on the Loan Application Form instead.

To start a new application: click New application. The Loan Application Form opens.

Loan Application Form

A loan application records the loan a member asks for: the product, amount, term and mode of payment, the purpose, the member's capacity to pay, the co-makers and the collateral. The same page then takes the application through the cooperative's loan process: the eligibility checks, the credit investigation when the product needs one, the approval by each level of the approval matrix, and the documents to complete before release. An application records nothing in the books; the loan is booked when it is released.

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Application Center, then New application for a new application, or click an application number to open it. Loan application on a member's Member Information page starts an application for that member.

The steps of an application

The strip at the top of a saved application shows its steps: Application, Validated, Credit investigation (only when the product needs one), For approval, Approved and Released. The steps already done have a green bar and the current step a blue one. A returned application is shown at Application with "Returned for correction" at the end of the strip; a disapproved or cancelled one shows "Disapproved" or "Cancelled" at the end.

Status What it means and what comes next
New application Saved, not yet validated. Run the eligibility checks.
For credit investigation The checks passed, and the product needs a credit investigation that is not on file yet. Record the Credit Investigation, then submit the application for approval.
Validated The checks passed, or failed checks were overridden. Submit it for approval.
For approval Waiting for the approvers. Each level approves, returns or disapproves it.
Approved Every level has approved. The loan can be released on Loan Release.
Released The loan account is open. Loan account opens it.
Returned for correction An approver sent it back. Edit it, run the checks again and submit it again.
Disapproved Refused by an approver. It goes no further.
Cancelled Cancelled with a reason. It goes no further.

Fields

Loan applied for:

Field What to enter / what it means
Member The borrower. Type part of the name, the member number, or an account or loan number, and pick the member. Only active members are listed.
Date The application date. The rate by the product and the amortization preview use it.
Loan product The loan product applied for. Only active products are listed. Choosing a product fills in Mode of payment, the term unit and Amortization from the product, and shows the amounts and terms the product allows under Amount applied for, for example "From 20,000.00 to 1,000,000.00, 6 to 36 months".
Amount applied for The amount the member asks for.
Term, in The term, in months or days.
Mode of payment How often the member pays: Daily, Weekly, Semi-monthly, Monthly, Quarterly, Semi-annual, Annual or Lump sum at maturity.
Rate By the product (term / amount) takes the product's rate for the term and amount on the application date: the rate of a matching term or amount range of the product, else its default rate. Or choose one of the rates of the product. The rate is kept on the application when you save.
Amortization Equal loan (equal amortization): the same amount every period, interest on the declining balance. Fixed principal (equal principal): the same principal every period plus the interest on the declining balance, so the amortizations go down. On a straight (add-on) product the interest of the whole term is split evenly over the amortizations whichever you choose.
Purpose The purpose of the loan, from the Loan purposes list on Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists.
Details of the purpose What the money is for, in a few words.
Renewal or restructuring of Shown when the member has open loans and the form is opened for that member (for example with Loan application on Member Information) or edited. Choose the open loan that the new loan pays off from its proceeds, or No, a new loan. The loan chosen does not count against the product's limit of open loans, and its overdue amortizations do not fail the arrears check.
Restructuring of a loan in arrears Tick it when the old loan is restructured rather than renewed. The new loan is then classified as Restructured.

Capacity to pay: Monthly income, Spouse income, Net disposable income, Dependents, Employer, Business and Spouse. These entries are saved with the application and are where the borrower's next loan starts: when a new application is opened for a member (or the member is chosen on it), the seven boxes are filled from the member's last loan application, as last saved, and the note under them says which one, for example "Filled from the borrower's last loan application, LA-000011 (saved 10/21/2026). Change what is no longer true: what is saved here is where the borrower's next loan starts." Change what is no longer true and save: the updated entries are then the start of the following loan; what you leave stays as it was. For the member's first loan, they are filled from the Financial Information tab (the incomes, the net disposable income, the employer, the spouse and the dependents). The application shows them in a Capacity to pay card. The eligibility check of the net disposable income reads the member's Financial Information tab.

Co-makers (one row each, Add a co-maker adds a row):

Column What to enter / what it means
Member A co-maker who is a member. The member must be active and cannot be the borrower.
or name (non-member) The name of a co-maker who is not a member.
Address The co-maker's address.
Guaranteed The amount the co-maker guarantees.

Collateral (one row each, Add collateral adds a row):

Column What to enter / what it means
Type From the Collateral types list on Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists. Left blank on a row with a description, it is saved as "Other".
Description What the collateral is.
TCT / OR-CR / ref. The title, registration or other reference number.
Fair market value, Loan value The appraised value and the value the cooperative lends on.
Document expiry, Insurance expiry When the document and the insurance expire. The loan account shows them in red once past.

A row with neither a type nor a description is ignored. The at the end of a row removes it.

At the bottom: Loan officer (it starts with your name) and Remarks.

Amortization preview: the panel on the right works the loan out as you type. It shows the Rate, the first Amortization with the mode and the number of payments, the Total interest, the Charges of the product with each charge, the Net proceeds (or Proceeds when the product's charges are not deducted), the Maturity, and the schedule: #, Due, Principal, Interest, Amortization and Balance. The first amortization is one payment period after the application date. Until the product, the amount and the term are filled in it says "Choose the product, the amount and the term."

Step by step

To enter a loan application:

  1. Open Loans > Loan Application Center and click New application, or click Loan application on the member's page.
  2. Choose the Member and check the Date.
  3. Choose the Loan product. Check the Mode of payment, the term unit and the Amortization it fills in.
  4. Type the Amount applied for and the Term.
  5. Leave Rate at By the product (term / amount), or choose one of the product's rates.
  6. Choose the Purpose and type the Details of the purpose.
  7. For a renewal or restructuring, choose the old loan in Renewal or restructuring of, and tick Restructuring of a loan in arrears if it is a restructuring.
  8. Check the Capacity to pay figures.
  9. Add the Co-makers and the Collateral the product asks for.
  10. Check the figures in Amortization preview.
  11. Click Save application. The program gives the application the next number and says "Application saved. Validate it to run the eligibility checks."

When you save, the product's documents to complete before release are copied to the application's Documents to complete before release list.

To run the eligibility checks (validate):

  1. Open the application. The Eligibility card lists the checks as of the last save or validation, each with a tick or a cross and the detail.
  2. Click Run the checks.
  3. When every check passes, the application becomes Validated ("Validated. Submit it for approval."), or For credit investigation when the product needs one that is not yet on file ("Validated. The product needs a credit investigation next.").
  4. When a check fails, the program says what failed. Click Edit, correct the application and run the checks again, or have an officer override the failed checks.

To override failed checks (needs the Approve right on Loan Application Center):

  1. Run the checks. When some fail, the card shows Reason for overriding the failed checks.
  2. Type the reason and click Override and validate. The reason is recorded in the audit trail.

To submit the application for approval: click Submit for approval in the Eligibility card ("Submitted for approval."). The button is shown on a validated application and on one for credit investigation; when the product needs a credit investigation, it stays greyed until the investigation is saved.

To approve, return or disapprove (needs the Approve right on Loan Application Center):

  1. Open an application that is For approval. The Approval card shows the level you are acting for, for example "Approval: level 1 of 2".
  2. Check the Approved amount. It starts with the amount applied for, or the amount an earlier level approved. You may lower it, but not raise it above the amount applied for.
  3. Type any Remarks.
  4. Click Approve, Return for correction, or Disapprove (the program asks you to confirm). The program says what was recorded, for example "Recorded: Approved."

When the last level approves, the application is Approved and the Release the loan button appears for users who may release loans. The remarks of a return or a disapproval are kept as the application's remarks.

To mark the documents to complete before release:

  1. In Documents to complete before release, find the requirement, for example "Signed promissory note".
  2. Choose Complete (or Not yet), and choose a scanned copy (an image or a PDF) if you have one.
  3. Click Save on that line ("Requirement updated."). The line then says "complete" in green instead of "to complete".

To ask for something the product's list does not have, type it in Another requirement and click Add. The release is refused until every requirement is complete. A file saved with a requirement opens from its name on the line.

To edit an application: click Edit. Only a new, validated, returned or for-credit-investigation application can be changed; once it is submitted for approval it is locked.

To cancel an application: click Cancel the application, type the reason and click Cancel the application in the window ("Application cancelled."). A released application cannot be cancelled. A cancelled application stays on the list (Status: Cancelled).

To delete an application (encoded twice, or by mistake): click Delete the application at the end of the page, type the reason and click Delete. The same is the Delete button of its row on the Loan Application Center.

The other buttons at the top: Credit investigation (when the product needs one) opens the Credit Investigation, Release the loan (on an approved application) opens Loan Release, Loan account (once released) opens the loan, and Applications goes back to the list.

The Amortization (if released today) card shows the schedule of the approved amount (or the amount applied for) as if the loan were released today.

The eligibility checks

The checks come from the loan product (set on Loan Products). A check the product does not use is not listed.

Check When it passes
Member is active The member's status is Active.
No hold on the member The member is not on hold. The detail shows the hold reason.
Amount at least the minimum, Amount within the maximum The amount applied for is within the product's minimum and maximum.
Term within ... to ... months The term is within the product's shortest and longest term. A term in days is compared at 30 days a month.
Member for at least ... month(s) The member joined at least that many months ago.
Age within the product limits The member's age is within the product's ages. It fails when the member has no date of birth.
Paid-up share capital of at least ... The member's paid-up share capital reaches the product's requirement, a fixed amount or a percentage of the amount applied for.
Savings of at least ... The member's savings reach the product's requirement, a fixed amount or a percentage of the amount applied for.
Net disposable income of at least ... The net disposable income on the member's Financial Information tab reaches the product's minimum.
Member belongs to the required segmentation The member is in the segment the product requires.
Within the maximum of ... open loan(s) of this product The member's open loans of the product plus the other applications of the product still in process stay under the product's limit. The loan being renewed does not count.
No amortization in arrears on other loans No other open loan of the member has an unpaid amortization past its due date. The loan being renewed does not count.
Co-maker exposure (loans guaranteed, for information) Always passes. It shows the balance of the loans the member guarantees as a co-maker.
Co-makers: ... required Added when you run the checks: at least the number of co-makers the product requires are listed.
Collateral listed Added when you run the checks, for a product that requires collateral: at least one collateral is listed.
Credit investigation on file Added when you run the checks, for a product that needs a credit investigation. It never fails: without an investigation on file the application goes to For credit investigation.

The approval levels

The levels an application needs come from the Loan Approval Matrix: every active level whose amount range covers the amount applied for, set for this product or for all products. The Approvals card lists the levels needed, for example "Level 1 needed: Credit Committee", and then every decision with its date and time, the approver and position, the decision with the approved amount, and the remarks.

  • The levels act in order. Each approval is recorded for the next level still pending.
  • A level tied to a role can be approved only by a user with that role.
  • The same user cannot approve an application twice.
  • When no level of the matrix covers the amount, one approval by a user with the Approve right approves the application.
  • After a return for correction, the approvals start again from level 1.

Who may do what

Task Right needed (User Role Configuration)
Enter an application Create on Loan Application Form.
Edit it, run the checks, submit it for approval, mark the documents, cancel it Edit on Loan Application Form.
Override failed checks, approve, return or disapprove Approve on Loan Application Center.

What it records

Nothing is posted to the general ledger or to the member's accounts. The audit trail records the creation and every change of the application, each validation (with the override reason when checks were overridden), the submission, each approval, return or disapproval, the requirement updates and the cancellation. The loan is booked when it is released on Loan Release.

Good to know

  • "Some checks failed. Enter the reason to override them (recorded in the audit trail), or correct the application." is shown to a user who may approve; other users see "Some checks failed. Correct the application, or ask an officer who may approve to override."
  • "Choose the member.", "Choose an active loan product.", "Enter the application date.", "Enter the amount applied for." and "Enter the term." appear when a required field is missing.
  • "Choose one of the product's rates." The rate chosen is not one of the product's rates.
  • "A member cannot be a co-maker of their own loan." and "Co-maker ... is not an active member."
  • "The loan to renew or restructure must be an open loan of the same member."
  • "The product needs a credit investigation before approval." Save the credit investigation first.
  • "The approved amount cannot exceed the amount applied for (50,000.00)." The approved amount can only be the same or lower.
  • "Level 2 is approved by the role ..." That level belongs to users with the role named.
  • "You have already approved this application." Another user must approve the next level.
  • "You are not allowed to approve loan applications." You need the Approve right on Loan Application Center.
  • A renewal or restructuring application can also be started from Restructure / Renew / Write Off.

Credit Investigation

The credit investigation (CI) records what the investigator found on the applicant: the residence, the children, the sources of income, the assets, the character references, the findings and a recommendation. A product with Credit investigation before approval ticked on Loan Products cannot be submitted for approval until the investigation is saved. An application has one investigation; saving again updates it.

Where to find it: the Credit investigation button on a loan application whose product needs one (see Loan Application Form). Opened any other way, the page goes back to the Loan Application Center with "Open the credit investigation from a loan application."

The borrower's next loan: a new investigation starts from the borrower's last one (of another application), as last saved: the spouse, the addresses, the residence, the areas and values, the informants, the children, the sources of income, the assets and the character references. A note at the top says which one, for example "Filled from the borrower's last credit investigation, LA-000004 of 02/04/2026", with its findings for reference. The date is today and the investigator is you; the remarks, the score and the recommendation are this investigation's own. Change what is no longer true and save: the investigation then shows its own entries, and is where the borrower's next one starts. For a first investigation, the spouse comes from the application and the address from the member.

Fields

Residence:

Field What to enter / what it means
Date of the investigation Required. It starts with today.
Spouse It starts with the spouse of the borrower's last investigation, or for a first one the spouse on the application.
Present address It starts with the address of the borrower's last investigation, or for a first one the member's address.
Previous address Where the applicant lived before.
Residence Owned, Rented, Living with parents / relatives or Free use.
House Single detached, Duplex, Row house / apartment or Condominium.
Building Concrete, Semi-concrete, Wood or Light materials.
Structure Residential, Residential and commercial or Commercial.
Storeys, Rooms The number of storeys and rooms.
Lot area (sq m), Floor area (sq m) The areas in square metres.
Estimated value The investigator's estimate of the value of the residence.

Findings:

Field What to enter / what it means
Remarks The findings of the investigation.
Informants (neighbours, barangay officials, employer) Who gave the information.
Investigated by It starts with your name.
Score A score, if the cooperative rates applicants.
Recommendation Required: Recommended, Recommended with conditions or Not recommended.

The lists (Children and Assets beside the residence, Sources of income across the page, Character references beside the findings; click Add for a row). A row is saved when any of its boxes is filled, for example an income with its employer and net monthly income but no source name; an empty row is left out. On a phone each row shows as a small card with the name of each box above it.

List Columns
Children Name, Level / grade, School, Age.
Sources of income Source, Business / employer, Type, Position, Years, Net monthly income.
Assets Description, Type, Estimated value.
Character references Name, Occupation, Contact.

Step by step

To record a credit investigation:

  1. Open the loan application and click Credit investigation.
  2. Fill in the Residence card.
  3. Add the Children, Sources of income, Assets and Character references.
  4. Type the Remarks and the Informants, check Investigated by, and choose the Recommendation.
  5. Click Save the investigation. The application opens again with "Credit investigation saved. Submit the application for approval next." (the second sentence appears when the application is for credit investigation).
  6. Click Submit for approval on the application.

An investigation saved before the application is validated is already on file when the checks are run, so the application goes straight to Validated.

To print the investigation: click Print (shown once the investigation is saved). The CREDIT INVESTIGATION REPORT prints with the cooperative heading, the application number and the date of the investigation; the applicant, member number and loan applied for (product, amount and term); the spouse; the addresses, residence, lot and floor areas and estimated value; the lists that have rows; the findings and remarks, the informants, the recommendation and score; and the signature lines Investigated by and Noted by (Credit Committee). The Credit Committee name comes from Credit Committee chair / Loans officer on Cooperative Profile & Policies.

Good to know

  • Nothing is posted to the books. The audit trail records the investigation and its changes.
  • Once the application is approved, released, cancelled or disapproved, the investigation is shown read-only. Saving then gives, for example, "The application is approved; the investigation can no longer change."
  • "Enter the date of the investigation." and "Choose the recommendation of the investigator."
  • Saving needs the Create right on Credit Investigation (Edit to change a saved one); printing needs the Print right.

Loan Release

Loan Release pays out an approved loan on the Loan Release Form, laid out like the form of the cooperative's former program. You choose the Release Type (Cash or Cheque) and the cash or bank account the loan is released from, and you can add, delete or change the deductions. In one step the program opens the loan account with its own copy of the rate, terms and penalty terms, builds the amortization schedule, takes the deductions, pays off the old loan of a renewal, and posts the voucher of the release through Banking: a Cash Voucher for a release in cash, a Check Voucher (Banking > Write Checks) for a release by cheque, each with its class. The page also lists the approved applications waiting for release and the loans released in a period.

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Release. Release the loan on an approved application, and Release on Loan Accounts Center, also open it. The form of a released loan opens from its loan number on this page, Release form on Loan Release Vouchers and More > Loan Release Form on the loan.

The lists

Approved, waiting for release lists every approved application, oldest first: Application (number and date), Member, Product, Approved, Term, and Requirements, which says "complete" or how many documents are still "to complete". Release on a line opens the Loan Release Form.

Released lists the loans released between Released from and To (this month to today at first; click Show after changing them):

Column What it shows
Date, Loan, Member, Product The release date, the loan number (click it to open the Loan Release Form of the loan), the borrower and the product.
Principal The principal of the loan.
Charges The deductions taken on release.
Net proceeds What the member received.
Voucher Cash Voucher, Check Voucher, or Loan Release Voucher for a release made before version 33, with the voucher ID, for example LR-000009. A voided release is greyed and marked Void.

The footer gives the number of loans and the totals, voided releases excluded. Loans brought over by the opening balances import are not listed. Loan release vouchers at the top opens Loan Release Vouchers.

The Loan Release Form

A yellow box above the form lists any requirement still to complete: "Requirements still to complete: ... The release is refused until they are marked complete on the application." The left side holds the borrower and the payment details, the right side the release date, the net proceeds, the release type and the co-makers, and the Loan Charges grid runs under both.

Field What to enter / what it means
Member Code, Member Name The borrower's member number and name.
