Loan Code, Loan Name The code and name of the loan product.
P.N No The promissory note number. Leave it blank to get the next number (the grey text shows it), or type the number of a pre-printed note.
Installment Mode Fixed Principal (the same principal in every amortization, the interest on the declining balance) or Equal Loan (the same amortization every time). It starts with the application's.
Term, Mode of Payment They start with the application's terms (MONTH(S) or DAY(S)) and can be changed at release.
Interest Rate The approved rate a year. Only a user with the Approve right on Loan Application Center can change it here; for others it is locked ("The rate is the approved rate; an officer who may approve loans can change it here.").
Start Payment Date The due date of the first amortization. It is one payment period after the release date and follows the release date and the mode of payment until you change it by hand. It cannot be before the release date.
Due Date The maturity, worked out from the start and the term (it cannot be typed).
Release Date The date of the voucher. It starts with today.
Net Proceeds The Approved Amount, and in large figures what the member receives: the approved amount less the deductions and the payoff of a previous loan.
Release Type Cash or Cheque. Cash posts a Cash Voucher, Cheque a Check Voucher of Write Checks, as the note under the account says.
Cheque No. For a cheque: the number of the cheque issued (required). It is the number of the check in Write Checks; the same number cannot be used twice on one bank account.
Cash/bank Account Where the loan is released from. For Cash the list has the cash accounts of the chart of accounts (the teller drawers, the vault, the revolving fund, the petty cash fund ...), and starts with the cash account of your open teller shift. For Cheque it has the bank accounts (a Bank-type account with a bank account number, or "Bank" in its name).
Co-Makers The co-makers of the application, with their member numbers.
Loan Charges The deductions: see below. Total Charges adds them up, and Payoff of the previous loan shows the payoff of a renewal.
Renewal of / Restructuring of Shown when the application names an old loan. That loan is paid off in full from the proceeds.
Pay off an open loan from the proceeds (renewal) Shown when the application names no old loan and the member has open loans. Choose a loan to pay off, or No, a new loan only. Tick Restructuring of a loan in arrears for a restructuring.
Class The class of the voucher (Financial > Class List). It is required, and the voucher and every line of its entry carry it.
Loan officer The officer of the loan. It starts with the officer of the application.

The deductions (Loan Charges)

The grid opens with the product's charges worked out for the approved amount and the term: Charge Name, Charge Account (the account it goes to: the member's share capital or savings account number for a savings charge, or "new ... account" when the member has none yet and one will be opened; the general ledger account for the others), Rate/Amount (the rate, % of the loan, of a charge worked out as a rate) and Calculated Amount. Under each charge name the grey text says how its amount was arrived at, for example "3.00 %", "fixed 100.00" or "as entered".

  • To add a deduction, click Add deduction and choose the charge in the new row; the list has every active charge of Loans > Loan Charges, the product's own first. Its amount is worked out at once.
  • To delete a deduction, click at the end of its row.
  • To change a rate, type it in Rate/Amount: the amount follows (for example 4 % of 15,000.00 = 600.00).
  • To change an amount, type it in Calculated Amount: it is taken as typed ("as entered").

The net proceeds, the total charges and the due date are worked out again on every change. A charge listed twice is refused ("... is listed twice in the deductions."), and so are deductions larger than the loan. Deductions changed from the product's own are written in the audit trail of the release, for example "Deductions changed on the release form: Share Capital Build-up: 750.00 to 600.00; Filing Fee: added 100.00".

How the deductions are taken depends on the product's Charges are setting:

Charges are Principal of the loan Net proceeds
Deducted from loan approved The approved amount Approved amount less the charges and the previous loan payoff
Collected separately The approved amount Approved amount less the previous loan payoff. The charges are not on the voucher; they are collected on an official receipt.
Added to the loan (capitalized) The approved amount plus the charges Approved amount less the previous loan payoff

When you have a release limit, the page shows it under the class, for example "Your release limit: 50,000.00." or "Your release limit: none, a supervisor approves every release."

Step by step

To release a loan:

  1. Open Loans > Loan Release and click Release on the application, or click Release the loan on the approved application.
  2. If a yellow box lists requirements still to complete, mark them complete on the application first.
  3. Check the Release Date, the Installment Mode, the Term, the Mode of Payment and the Start Payment Date. Leave P.N No blank, or type the number of the pre-printed note.
  4. Choose the Release Type. For Cash, choose the cash account in Cash/bank Account (your drawer is chosen when your shift is open). For Cheque, type the Cheque No. and choose the bank account.
  5. Check the Loan Charges: add, delete or change the deductions as agreed with the member.
  6. For a renewal not named on the application, choose the loan in Pay off an open loan from the proceeds (renewal).
  7. Choose the Class, and check the Net Proceeds.
  8. If the approved amount is above your release limit, have a supervisor approve under Supervisor approval: the supervisor types their username and password, or gives you a one-time approval code.
  9. Click Release Loan. The program asks "Release the loan and pay out 18,400.00? The loan account, its schedule and the voucher are created at once." with the class under it. Click Release Loan.

The form opens again on the released loan with, for example, "Loan 01-6-0000010 released: Check Voucher 546345436 (voucher ID LR-000010). Print the voucher and the documents below." The buttons under the form, greyed until the release, now print its papers, each in a new tab:

Button What it prints or opens
Print To Passbook The loan passbook on Passbook Printing (when the loan product has a passbook type).
Disbursement The Cash Voucher or the Check Voucher of the release (see below).
Promissory, Disclosure, Amortization The promissory note, the disclosure statement and the amortization schedule of Loan Documents.
Co-Maker The co-maker statement (a list when there are several co-makers).
Loan Release Stmt The Loan Release Statement.
Example

Cruz, Ana's Regular Loan of ₱15,000.00 is released by cheque 546345435 from 1010 Cash in Bank - BDO Checking. On the form the share capital build-up is brought down to 4 % (₱600.00) and a filing fee of ₱100.00 is added to the 3 % service fee (₱450.00): the net proceeds are ₱13,850.00, posted as a Check Voucher of Write Checks, voucher ID LR-000009. Bautista, Pedro's Emergency Loan of ₱5,000.00 is released in cash from 1006 Revolving Fund: a Cash Voucher CAV-00003 of ₱4,900.00 after the ₱100.00 filing fee.

What it records

A voucher of Banking dated the release date, with the loan number as its reference and the member as the payee, under the class of the form:

  • Released in cash: a Cash Voucher (Banking > Cash Voucher), numbered in the CAV series, paid from the cash account chosen.
  • Released by cheque: a check of Banking > Write Checks numbered with the cheque no., drawn on the bank account chosen. It is in the bank register and is reconciled with the bank statement like any check.
  • A release that pays nothing out (the deductions and the payoff of a previous loan take the whole loan) is a Loan Release Voucher (a journal entry), numbered with its voucher ID.

Every release gets a voucher ID of the LR- series (Members & Others > Document Numbering, "Loan release voucher ID"), printed on the voucher and kept with the loan, so the vouchers of the loan releases are monitored together on Loan Release Vouchers. The entry:

Account Debit Credit
The product's receivable account (by default Loans Receivable - Current), memo "Release of loan payable in 12 month(s)" Principal of the loan
Each financial charge: the charge's account, or Service Fees on Loans Charge
Each non-financial charge (payable to a third party): the charge's account, or Loan Insurance Payable Charge
Each savings charge (for example a share capital build-up): the ledger account of the member's account in the charge's savings or share capital product Charge
A renewal: the old loan's receivable account, Interest Receivable on Loans (for the interest already accrued) and the old loan's interest income account, and its penalty account (by default Fines, Penalties and Surcharges) The payoff, split into principal, interest and penalty
The cash account (Cash Voucher) or the bank account (check) of the voucher Net proceeds

With the voucher the program:

  • Opens the loan account (number by the Loan account number format of Cooperative Profile & Policies, for example 01-6-0000008), with status Current, or Restructured for a restructuring, and its schedule. The loan ledger gets a Release line with the principal and the scheduled interest.
  • Keeps each deduction with the loan (its name, how it was worked out, its amount and, for a savings charge, the member's account) and credits each savings charge to the member's account in that product (code LC). If the member has no account in the product of a savings charge, one is opened.
  • Pays off the old loan of a renewal on its ledger (a Restructure line), marks it Renewed (paid by a new loan), and releases the savings held for it. On a diminishing-balance loan the scheduled interest not yet earned is cancelled.
  • Records cash paid from your teller drawer as a cash-out of your open shift (numbered with the cash voucher), so the expected cash of the drawer goes down by the net proceeds and the release is on the Daily Collection & Disbursement Summary. Cash from another cash account (the vault, the revolving fund ...) is not in the drawer.
  • Marks the application Released and records the release in the audit trail.

The Cash Voucher and the Check Voucher

Disbursement on the form, Print voucher on Loan Release Vouchers and Print on the voucher in Banking print it in the format of the former program: the cooperative's name, address and telephone number; Cash Voucher or Check Voucher with the Class and the branch; Voucher ID, Date, Paid to and Check no. (a cash voucher shows its CAV no.); the entry with Account ID, Account Name, Description, SL Code (the member number), Debit and Credit, and the Totals; then Prepared by (the user who released the loan), Paid by, Received by, Approved by (the supervisor who approved the release above the limit, else the approver of the loan), Checked by (the bookkeeper of Cooperative Profile & Policies) and the print date. A second print asks for a reason and is marked as a copy.

Voiding a release

The voucher of a release is never edited or deleted: Edit and Delete are not offered on it in Banking, and the Cash Voucher and Write Checks pages refuse it ("... is the release of loan 01-6-0000008: it is not edited. Void it to cancel the release."). To correct a release, open its voucher in Banking (Open on Loan Release Vouchers), click Void, type the reason and confirm. Voiding needs the Delete/Void right on that Banking page, and a supervisor's approval unless you have the Approve right there. The voucher stays on file marked VOID, and the general ledger and member ledgers are reversed with it: the loan is cancelled, the application goes back to Approved (so it can be released again, with a new voucher), the savings credits are reversed and an old loan that was paid off is open again. A release made before version 33 is voided on its loan release voucher.

A release cannot be voided when the loan already has payments or adjustments ("Loan ... already has payments or adjustments. Void those first, or restructure the loan."), or when the loan is fully paid, written off or renewed.

Good to know

  • An application is released once: "This application was already released as loan 01-6-0000008." Only an approved application can be released: "Only an approved application can be released (this one is ...)."
  • "Pre-release requirements not yet complete: ..." Mark the requirements complete on the application.
  • The release limit is the special permission "Cooperative: max loan release amount without supervisor approval". Above it: "Supervisor approval is required: loan release above your limit (60,000.00). Ask a supervisor to enter their username and password below, or type a one-time approval code." A user with the Approve right on Loan Release approves their own release. Approval codes are made on Approval Codes.
  • "Open your teller shift first (Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count)." A release in cash from your own drawer needs an open shift when the policy Cash transactions need an open teller shift is Yes. Open it on Teller Shift & Cash Count, or release from another cash account.
  • "Choose the cash account the loan is released from (Cash/bank Account)." and "Choose the bank account the cheque is drawn on (Cash/bank Account)."; "Enter the cheque no."; "Cheque no. ... of ... is already used (Banking > Write Checks). Enter the number of the cheque issued."
  • "The start of payment must be on or after the release date."
  • "The charges and the previous loan payoff (...) exceed the approved amount." The net proceeds would be negative.
  • The member must be active and not on hold: "Member ... is on hold: ..."
  • The release date cannot be in the future or in a closed period, and back-dating beyond the days the policy allows needs the special permission "Cooperative: can record back-dated member transactions".
  • A user who releases loans does not need rights on the Banking pages: the voucher is posted by the release, printed from the form and from Loan Release Vouchers.

Loan Release Vouchers

Loan Release Vouchers is the monitoring of every voucher of a loan release, apart from the other cash vouchers and checks of Banking: the Cash Vouchers of the loans released in cash, the Check Vouchers of the loans released by cheque, and the loan release vouchers of the releases made before version 33.

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Release Vouchers, or Loan release vouchers on Loan Release. The page has the rights of Loan Release when it is added by the upgrade.

The date range at the top right (This month at first; Custom shows the dates) and the search box (the member's name, the loan number, the voucher ID or number, the member number) change the list as you type. Filter opens Voucher (Cash Vouchers, Check Vouchers or the loan release vouchers before version 33), Cash / bank account, Class and Status (Posted or Void).

Column What it shows
Date, Voucher ID The release date and the voucher ID (LR-).
Voucher Cash Voucher, Check Voucher or Loan Release Voucher, with its number (the CAV no. or the cheque no.) and the cash or bank account. A void voucher is struck through.
Paid to, Loan The member with the member number, and the loan with its product.
Loan amount, Deductions, Paid out The principal, the deductions with the payoff of a previous loan, and the net proceeds paid.
Class, Prepared by The class of the voucher and the user who released the loan (on wide screens).

Click a voucher and use the toolbar: Print voucher (also a double-click) prints the Cash or Check Voucher; Open opens it in Banking (or the loan release voucher); Loan opens the loan; Release form opens its Loan Release Form. A right-click (on a phone the button) adds Loan Release Statement. Print list prints the list as shown and Export saves it as a CSV file. The footer gives the number of vouchers and the totals of the loans, the deductions and the amounts paid out, voids left out.

Loan Collection

Loan Collection is the Loan Collection Form of the cooperative's former program: the teller receives a member's payment on one loan and issues the official receipt. The form shows the member's open loans, what is due on the chosen loan as of the date, and how the payment is applied to the penalties, the interest and the principal. Under the form, Loan Collections lists the collections of a date range, where a collection is deleted or printed again. Error Correct negates an erroneous payslip, and Update Passbook prints the loan passbook.

Where to find it: Teller > Loan Collection (under Loans before version 33). Collection on a loan in Loan Accounts Center and Collect on an open loan open the form on that loan. Every payslip of the form and its error corrections are on the teller's Daily Collection & Disbursement Summary.

When the policy Cash transactions need an open teller shift is Yes and your shift is not open, a yellow box says "Your teller shift is not open. Open it on Teller Shift & Cash Count before receiving payments." The status bar at the bottom says what the form needs next (for example "Select the loan to be paid in Open Loans."), how the payment is applied, or what is wrong, and shows the teller, the workstation (the shift and its cash drawer), the date and the time.

OR Entry

Field What to enter / what it means
Official Receipt # Leave it empty to get the next official receipt number, with the prefix of the member's branch (the grey text shows the next number). Type a number to use a pre-printed receipt.
Receive from The member who pays: type part of the name or the member number and choose the member. The form opens again with the member's open loans; what you typed in OR Entry is kept.
Payment Mode One of three:
  • Cash: the payment is paid in cash.
  • Check: the payment is paid by one or more cheques (cheque issuances), entered in Cheque Payment Details.
  • Bank: the payment was deposited directly to the cooperative's bank account and the member presents the deposit slip.
For a member who has resigned, been terminated or died, a fourth mode, Savings (settlement of a member who has left), pays the loan from the member's own deposits with a supervisor's approval.
Cash Account For Cash, the cash account of your shift (your drawer). For Check, the clearing account where the cheques wait until they are cleared on Check Clearing (by default Checks and Other Cash Items). For Bank, the label reads Bank Account: choose the cooperative's bank account the member deposited to.
Cheque Payment Details Open for the Check mode only: Cheque No, Bank Name, Date and Amount of each cheque. Add a cheque adds a row, the x removes one. Cheques shows their total.
Transaction Date Today. It can be changed only with Back Entry ticked.
Cash Amount The payment amount. For the Check mode it is the total of the cheques, filled in as you type them. With Full payment it is the payoff of the loan, and with Override Payment the total of Amount Paid.
Total Amount The payment applied to the loan: the total of the three Amount Paid boxes.
Back Entry Ticked, the Transaction Date can be set to an earlier date. Going back more days than the policy allows needs the special permission "Cooperative: can record back-dated member transactions".

Open Loans

The member's loans that can be paid (current, past due or restructured), oldest release first: Loan Name (with the loan number), Date Released, Date Due (the maturity), PN No. (the promissory note) and two buttons. Click a row (or move to it with the arrow keys and press Enter) to select the outstanding loan to be paid; a member with one open loan has it selected already.

  • View Amortization shows the amortization schedule of the loan: each amortization's due date, principal, interest, amortization and balance after it, what was paid of it, and whether it is paid or overdue.
  • View Subsidiary shows the member's individual loan ledger: every entry of the loan (release, payments, penalties, error corrections, adjustments ...), the principal, interest and penalty paid or charged, the balances after each entry and the teller's ID.

On a phone the two columns are headed Schedule and Ledger, and the release date, the PN number and the maturity are listed under the loan name.

Amortization Due Details & Payments

For the loan selected, the heading shows the product, the loan number and the next due date, and the grid shows for the Principal, the Interest and the Penalty:

Row What it means
Current Balance The loan balance, the interest balance and the penalty balance (the penalties posted plus the penalty earned up to the date and not yet posted).
Amortization Due As of the Transaction Date: the principal of the amortizations due by the schedule and not yet paid, the interest due by the schedule, and the penalty due as of the date.
#of Amort. How many amortizations the amount due makes: the principal due over the principal of one amortization, the interest due over the interest of one amortization; for the penalty, the number of overdue amortizations.
Amount Paid (yellow) The payment applied to the principal, the interest and the penalties. It follows the Cash Amount as you type it.

Beside the grid: Last Payment Date, Override Payment, and Full payment (payoff ...) with the payoff amount. The figures are worked out again when the date, the amount or the loan changes.

How a payment is applied

  1. When the policy Compute penalties when a payment is received is Yes, the penalty earned up to the payment date is first posted to the loan ledger. When it is No, only the penalties already posted by Post Loan Penalties & Interest Accrual are collected.
  2. When penalties or interest are due, the application of payment priorities is 1.) Due Penalties 2.) Due Interest 3.) Principal, oldest amortization first. The order is the policy Payment allocation order on Cooperative Profile & Policies; the note under the grid shows the order in use.
  3. What is left after the dues goes to the principal, up to the principal balance. When no penalty and no interest is due, the whole payment goes to the principal by default: for example 16,000.00 paid before the first amortization date is Principal 16,000.00, Interest 0.00, Penalty 0.00. An advance is not taken as interest of the amortizations not yet due; that interest is paid when it falls due. In the schedule, the principal paid settles the oldest amortizations' principal first.
  4. Only a payment beyond the whole principal pays the interest not yet due, as a full payment does (the interest to date of a diminishing-balance loan, the scheduled interest still unpaid of a straight loan).
  5. More than the loan can take is refused: the status bar shows the excess in red. Lower the amount, or tick Full payment; receive the rest as a savings deposit on Savings Account Transaction.

Override Payment (for users with the special permission "Cooperative: can override the payment allocation (penalty, interest, principal) on a loan collection"; greyed out for the others) overrides the application of payment: the Amount Paid boxes turn white and you type the principal, interest and penalty paid; the Cash Amount becomes their total. The penalty cannot exceed the penalty due, the interest the interest balance, and the principal the principal balance. Click the button again to go back to the default application. The override is recorded in the audit trail with the receipt.

Full payment sets the amount to the payoff of the loan on the date and pays the loan in full: the principal balance, the interest due and the penalties, plus for a diminishing-balance loan the daily interest on the principal from the last amortization date to the payment date, or for a straight (add-on) loan all the scheduled interest still unpaid. When a diminishing-balance loan is paid in full, the scheduled interest not yet earned is cancelled (a Rebate line on the loan ledger). The loan becomes Fully paid and the savings held for it are released.

Memo, the print choices and the buttons

Item What it does
Memo A note added to the receipt's memo.
Print to Passbook Select if the payment is printed to the loan passbook: after Save, the passbook printing window opens for the lines not yet printed. The tick is remembered on the computer.
Show Print Preview after saving Select to see the machine validation of the payslip after Save, ready to print on the payslip. The tick is remembered on the computer.
Error Correct The error correction of an erroneous payslip: see below. Greyed out when the member has no payment that can be corrected.
Update Passbook Prints the loan passbook of the selected loan: the entries not yet printed, from the next line of the current page. Entries that go on to the next page print in the same run: the print window asks you to turn the passbook to page 2 (3 and so on), as on Savings Account Transaction.
Clear Empties the form (OR Entry, the member, the loans, the figures and the memo) for the next payment.
Save Saves the payment: the program asks "Save official receipt for 3,000.00 on loan Regular Loan 02-6-0000006?" with the class of the entry; click Save.
Class (under Loan Collections) The class of the entry, from the Class List of the accounting books (in the former program, Branch, Division and Segment). It is required, and starts on the class you chose last.

Step by step

To collect a loan payment:

  1. Open Teller > Loan Collection. Leave Official Receipt # empty, or type the number of the pre-printed receipt.
  2. In Receive from, type the member's name or number and choose the member.
  3. Choose the Payment Mode. For Bank, choose the bank account; for Check, type each cheque in Cheque Payment Details.
  4. In Open Loans, click the loan to be paid. Check the balances and the amortizations due.
  5. Type the Cash Amount (for a check it is filled in from the cheques). Check the Amount Paid to the principal, interest and penalty, and the status bar.
  6. Choose the Class under Loan Collections if it is not set, and type a Memo if needed.
  7. If the receipt is above the policy amount A receipt above this needs supervisor approval, have a supervisor approve under Supervisor approval.
  8. Click Save, then Save in the question.
  9. With Show Print Preview after saving, the Machine Validation window shows the validation: insert the payslip in the printer and click Print. Once it is printed, the validation window closes by itself and you are back on the form. With Print to Passbook, the passbook window follows: place the passbook at the page and line it says, click Print, and answer Yes, mark them printed when the lines came out right.

The form opens again on the member and the loan, with the new balances and "Official receipt 000165 saved: 3,000.00 on loan 02-6-0000006, Torres, Edgar." Validation shows the validation again and Official receipt prints the receipt.

To pay off a loan: select the loan, tick Full payment, choose the payment mode and click Save.

Example

Torres, Edgar pays ₱3,000.00 by two cheques (₱1,800.00 and ₱1,200.00) on his Regular Loan, which is past due. The Cash Amount becomes 3,000.00 as the cheques are typed. The payment is applied to the penalty due first, then the interest due, and the rest to the principal; the official receipt debits the checks clearing account, where the cheques wait until they are cleared on Check Clearing.

The machine validation of the payslip

The validation prints two lines on the loan payslip: the OR number (the voucher number for an error correction), the loan number, the date and time; then the member's name, LP (loan payment) or ECLP (an error correction, with the amount negative), the amount and the teller ID. Where it prints on the slip is set under Slip validation: margins on Passbook Types. The first print is the original; each later print is a counted copy and asks for the reason. The validation window closes by itself once it is printed.

Error Correct of an erroneous payslip

Error Correct negates in full a payment entered by mistake (the wrong member or loan, the wrong amount). The correction is a line of its own on the loan ledger and in the loan passbook, printed EC with the amounts negative, so the passbook keeps both lines.

  1. Choose the member in Receive from (and select the loan, so its latest payment is chosen for you). Click Error Correct.
  2. In Payment entered by mistake, choose the payment: the list shows the OR number, the date, the loan, the amount and the payment mode of the member's payments not yet corrected, a loan already paid off by the payment included.
  3. Check the Date (today; not earlier than the payment), the Class, type the Reason, and keep or untick the two print choices.
  4. Click Error Correct, then Post the error correction in the question.

The loan's balances, its amortization schedule, its status and its last payment date go back as they were before the payment; a loan the payment had paid off is open again (the rebate of its unearned interest is undone and the holds on the member's deposits come back). The receipt's journal entry is reversed line for line: a cash payment is paid back from your drawer by a Member Disbursement (cash voucher, numbered in the voucher series, counted in your shift); a check or bank payment by a Cooperative Journal Voucher, and the cheques no longer wait on Check Clearing. The original receipt stays on file, marked as corrected.

Correcting another teller's payment, or one of an earlier day, needs a supervisor's approval (under Supervisor approval in the window). These are corrected another way, and the window says how: a receipt that also holds other items (void it with the reason on the official receipt and enter it again), a cheque already cleared to the bank (a returned check, or a loan adjustment), and a payment from savings (void the receipt).

What it records

An Official Receipt voucher numbered with the OR number:

Account Debit Credit
The cash account of your shift (Cash), the checks clearing account (Check, by default Checks and Other Cash Items (clearing)), the bank account chosen (Bank), or the ledger account of the member's savings (the settlement of a member who has left) Total
The loan's receivable account (by default Loans Receivable - Current) Principal
Interest Receivable on Loans The part of the interest already accrued into income
The loan's interest income account (by default Interest Income on Loans) The rest of the interest
The loan's penalty account (by default Fines, Penalties and Surcharges) Penalty

When a diminishing-balance loan with accrued interest is paid in full, the accrued interest that will no longer be earned is taken back out: debit the interest income account, credit Interest Receivable on Loans.

With the voucher the program writes a Payment line on the loan ledger (with the OR number), marks the amortizations paid, updates the loan's balances and status, records the receipt on your teller shift, and records the posting in the audit trail. The receipt lines read, for example, "Penalty Regular Loan 02-6-0000006". An error correction records the voucher described above, a Correction line for each line the receipt made on the loan, and an "error_correct" entry in the audit trail with the reason.

Loan Collections

The list of loan collections under the form: the payments and error corrections of the period, latest first.

  • Date range: Custom Date, This Day (at first), This Week (Monday to Sunday) or This Month. Choosing one sets From and To; changing a date makes it Custom Date. Search narrows the list by the member's name or number, the loan number or the OR number as you type. Query filters the list again. The address bar keeps the filter, so Back and Refresh show the same list.
  • The list: Transaction Date, Trans. RefNo (the OR number, or the voucher number of an error correction, marked "Error correct"; a corrected payment is marked "corrected" and one printed in the passbook "in passbook"), Member name, Loan Name (with the loan number) and Amount Paid (an error correction negative, in red), with the count and the total.
  • Click a collection to select it. Delete deletes (voids) the selected collection, with the reason: it stays on file marked void and the loan and the books are reversed. A collection already printed in the passbook, or one that was corrected, cannot be deleted (correct it with Error Correct; delete the correction first). Another teller's collection, or an earlier day's, needs a supervisor.
  • Print prints the Official receipt (or voucher) of the selected collection, its Payslip validation again, or The list of loan collections with the dates and the search of the list. A reprint asks for the reason and is counted.

Loans due on the water cashier page

When the water cashier opens a water-service account on Water Billing > Collections (Cashier), a Loan Due panel under the receivables shows what the member of that account (the member with the account's name) has due on their open loans as of the Date of Transaction: Principal, Interest, Penalties and Total, the same figures as the Amortization Due Details of this page. Pointing at an amount (or tapping it on a phone) shows it by loan: the loan product, the loan number, the next due date and the amount due. Changing the date works them out again.

The panel is for reference only: nothing is collected or posted there. The cashier sends the member to the teller, or the teller takes the payment here. A water-service account whose owner is not a cooperative member shows no panel; a member with no open loan shows "No open loans."

Good to know

  • "The payment on loan 02-6-0000006 is 500.00 more than the loan can take (payoff 31,964.92). Lower the amount, or tick Full payment; receive the rest as a savings deposit on Savings Account Transaction."
  • "The override (...) must add up to the amount paid (...)." Correct the Amount Paid boxes, or the Cash Amount.
  • "Penalty paid cannot exceed the penalty due (...)", "Interest paid cannot exceed the interest balance (...)" and "Principal paid cannot exceed the principal balance (...)."
  • "The cheques (...) must add up to the receipt total (...)." The cheques are the payment of a Check receipt.
  • "Choose the bank account the payment went into." A Bank payment names the cooperative's bank account.
  • "Tick Back Entry to record the payment on an earlier date."
  • "Official receipt number ... is already used." Leave Official Receipt # empty or type the right number.
  • "Open your teller shift first (Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count)." With the policy Cash transactions need an open teller shift set to Yes, every collection needs an open shift.
  • "Choose the class of the loan payment: it is required, and its journal entry carries it." Choose the Class under Loan Collections.
  • "Member ... is on hold: ..." Payments are refused for a member on hold.
  • "That payment was already corrected." A payment is corrected once; "The correction cannot be dated before the payment (...)."
  • Collecting, Error Correct and Delete need the Create right on Loan Collection; printing needs its Print right, and the passbook the Print right on Passbook Printing.

Loan Accounts Center

The Loan Accounts Center is laid out like the Loan Accounts Center of the cooperative's former program: a toolbar over the grid of the released loans. From the selected loan you collect a payment, open its Loan Ledger Form (printable, in the passbook format), its Internal Ledger (with the additional interest and penalty it has earned when it is past due), its amortization schedule and its passbook. Double-click a loan to open it: its summary, schedule, ledger, penalty computation, co-makers and collateral, and the notices and documents printed.

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Accounts Center. A loan number on Loan Release or Loan Collection, and Loan account on a released application, open that loan. Import Loans on its title bar brings the loans of the former system in from a CSV file (Importing loans).

When the page opens, the program brings the status of every open loan up to date (Current or Past due by its schedule).

The grid

The open loans (current, past due and restructured), grouped by loan product and then by account number:

Column What it shows
Account No. The loan number, with the status under it when the loan is not current (for example Past due, in red).
Member name, Loan Name The borrower with the member number, and the loan product.
Release Date, Due Date The release date and the maturity. A maturity that has passed while the loan is open is red.
Principal Loan, Principal Balance The principal released and what is left of it.
Interest Balance The scheduled interest not yet paid.
Current Balance The principal balance plus the interest balance.

The footer gives the number of loans (and how many are past due) and the totals of the principal loans, principal balances, interest balances and current balances. On a phone the grid keeps the account number, the member and the current balance.

The toolbar

Click a loan, then a button. The buttons that need a loan stay greyed until one is selected. A right-click on a loan (on a phone the button or a long press) opens the same actions with Open the loan, Documents and Loan Release Form.

Button What it does
Collection Opens the Loan Collection Form on the loan (an open loan; the Create right on Loan Collection).
Ledger The Loan Ledger Form of the loan (see below).
Internal Ledger The Internal Ledger Form of the loan (see below).
View Schedule The amortization schedule with what is paid of each amortization, printable.
Update Passbook Opens Passbook Printing on the loan, to print the lines not yet in the loan passbook (a product with a passbook type).
Refresh Shows the grid again with the latest figures.
Filter Status (Open (current, past due, restructured), Due in 7 days, Matured, unpaid, one status such as Fully paid or Written off, or Every loan), Loan (the product), Officer and Branch.
Print Prints the grid as shown, with its totals.
Search Type every word of the member's name, part of the account number, or the member or PN number: the grid follows as you type.

Loan Ledger Form

The account number, the Member Name, the Loan Name, the Due Date and the Loan Term, over the ledger in the format of the loan passbook: Date, Reference No (the promissory note number for the release, the OR number of a payment, EC and the number for an error correction), Release, Prin. Payments, Prin. Balance, Int. Payments and Fines/Surc. (the penalties paid), with the Totals. An error correction is negative, as in the passbook.

  • Passbook View (at first): the release and the payments, as the member's passbook shows them.
  • View All: every entry of the loan ledger; the penalties charged, the adjustments, the waivers, the rebates and the payoff by a renewal are told under the reference (for example "Penalty, penalty charged 522.20").
  • Print prints the form of the view chosen on a page of its own, under the cooperative's name: the loan ledger in the passbook format.

Internal Ledger Form

The internal ledger of the loan: Date and Memo, then the Release/Additional Charges, the Payments and the Balances, each by Principal, Interest and Penalty, with the totals. After the entries of the loan ledger come the charges the loan has earned to the date in Computed to (today at first) because it is past due or did not keep to its amortization schedule, worked out by the set-up of its loan product. They are in italics on a yellow ground, and are not on the loan ledger yet:

  • Additional interest, by the product's Additional interest when past due (Loan Products), per day at the loan's interest rate on the days in a year of the product:
    • on the principal of each amortization not paid by its due date, from that date (a payment on it lowers the base from the day it is made), for example "Additional interest: amortization #2 due 07/05/2026, 108 day(s) at 18.00 %";
    • or on the principal balance after the maturity date.
    The standard setting takes the first for a diminishing-balance loan and the second for an add-on loan. On a loan brought over with Import Loans it counts from the cut-off date ("... 10 day(s) from 10/31/2026 at 18.00 %"): the interest before that date came with the import.
  • Penalty not yet charged, by the loan's penalty terms (rate, grace days and base), as on the Penalty computation tab, for example "Penalty: amortization #3 due 08/05/2026, 25 day(s) on 1,974.94". It is charged when the loan is collected, or by the penalty run.

Under the grid: what is On the loan ledger, the Additional interest and the Penalty ... not yet charged to the date, and the Balance with the additional charges; the note under it gives the product's settings. Change Computed to to see them on another date. Print prints the form on a page of its own.

Note

The additional interest is worked out for the Internal Ledger: it shows what the loan has earned by its product's terms. It is not posted: the Loan Collection Form collects the amortizations, the interest and the penalty of the loan ledger. To collect it, add it to the loan's interest on Loan Adjustments (an increase of the interest) and receive it with Override Payment on the Loan Collection Form.

One loan

The buttons at the top of a loan:

  • Collect (an open loan, for users with the Create right on Loan Collection) opens the Loan Collection Form on the loan.
  • Documents opens Loan Documents.
  • More: Waive interest or penalty (Interest / Penalty Waiving), Adjust balances (Loan Adjustments), Renew, restructure or write off or, for a written-off loan, Record a recovery (Restructure / Renew / Write Off), Loan Release Form, the Cash Voucher or Check Voucher of the release, Application, Print the Loan Ledger (passbook view) and Print the Internal Ledger. Each item appears only when it applies and you may open its page.
  • Loans goes back to the Loan Accounts Center.

A note above the member panel says when the loan was written off (with the amount recovered so far), when it was paid off by a newer loan, or which older loan it paid off. For an open loan the tiles show the Principal balance (of the principal released), Due as of the date (with the number of amortizations unpaid), the Penalty (with the part not yet posted), Payoff today (with the interest to date, or "all scheduled interest" for a straight loan) and Days past due, counted from the oldest unpaid amortization, with its age group (Non-delinquent, 1 to 30 days, 1 to 3 months, 3 to 6 months, 6 to 12 months or Over 12 months) and the next due date.

Summary tab

The loan's terms and balances: Loan no., Promissory note, Product, Status, Principal, Rate (a year, with the interest method), Term, Amortization, Released (date and how; for a loan brought over from the former system, its release date and the date of its balances), First amortization, Maturity, Scheduled interest, Charges, Net proceeds, Previous loan paid off, Interest balance, Interest accrued (in income), Penalty balance, Waived, Penalty terms, Last payment, Penalty computed through, Officer, Branch and Closed. A line with nothing to show is left out. The rate, terms and penalty terms are the loan's own copy, taken on release; a later change to the product does not touch them.

On the right:

  • Dues as of (open loans): choose a date and click Compute to see the Penalty, Interest due, Principal due, Total due, the interest from the last amortization date with the number of days, and the Payoff amount on that date. The tiles follow the same date.
  • Charges on release: each charge with its basis and how it was taken.
  • Note: a short note on the loan, with Save note and Recompute balances.

Amortization schedule tab

Each amortization with #, Due, Principal, Interest, Amortization, Balance after, Principal paid, Interest paid and Paid: the date it was fully paid in green, or "overdue" in red. An unpaid amortization past its due date is shaded red. The footer gives the totals.

Loan ledger tab

Every entry on the loan in date order: Date, Entry (the kind and its memo), Voucher (the OR or voucher number; click it to open the voucher), Charged and Paid for the Principal, Interest and Penalty, and the three Balances after the entry. A voided entry is struck through.

Entry What it is
Release The principal and the scheduled interest charged when the loan was released.
Payment A payment on an official receipt. On a loan brought over with Import Loans, also the payments made in the former system ("Former system: amortization 3 paid"), on the due dates.
Error correction The reversal of a payment by Error Correct on the Loan Collection Form.
Penalty Penalty posted, on a collection or by the penalty run; on an imported loan, the penalties up to the cut-off date.
Past due interest On a loan brought over with Import Loans: the past due interest up to the cut-off date, given in the file or worked out ("Past due interest to 10/31/2026").
Paid off by a new loan The payoff of this loan by a renewal or restructuring.
Interest rebate Scheduled interest cancelled when a diminishing-balance loan was paid in full.
Adjustment, Waiver, Write-off, Recovery Entries from Loan Adjustments, Interest / Penalty Waiving and Restructure / Renew / Write Off.
Interest accrual Interest recognized as income by the monthly accrual. It is greyed: "(income recognized; the member balance does not change)".

Penalty computation tab

Penalty still to post as of the date shows, for each overdue amortization, the Amortization, Due, Counted from, Base, Days and Penalty, and the total Not yet posted. The line under it gives the loan's formula, for example "Penalty = base × 36.00 % ÷ 100 × days ÷ 365, counted from the end of the 3-day grace period or from the last computation (10/11/2026), on loan arrears (unpaid principal + interest)." The base is the unpaid principal and interest of each overdue amortization, its unpaid principal only, or the outstanding principal, by the loan's penalty terms. Penalties posted lists the penalties already on the ledger.

Co-makers and collateral tab

  • Co-makers: each co-maker (a member's name opens the member) with the address and the amount guaranteed.
  • Savings held for this loan: the savings accounts with an amount held for the loan, the reason, and whether it is still held or released.
  • Collateral: the type, description, reference, market value and loan value, the document and insurance expiry dates (red once past), and Held or Released with the date.

Notices and prints tab

Collection notices lists the payment reminders and demand letters recorded on Loan Documents, with the date, type, notes, amount due and who recorded it. Documents printed lists every print of the loan's documents with the date and time, the document, "Original" or the copy number with the reprint reason, and who printed it.

Notes, collateral release and recomputing

To save a note on the loan: type it in Note on the Summary tab (up to 255 characters) and click Save note ("Note saved."). The change is recorded in the audit trail.

To release collateral to the member:

  1. Open the Co-makers and collateral tab.
  2. Click Release on the collateral and confirm "Mark this collateral returned to the member?".

The collateral is marked Released with today's date ("Collateral marked released.") and the release is recorded in the audit trail. While the loan is still open, the button is greyed unless you have the Approve right on Loan Accounts Center ("The loan is still open. Releasing its collateral early needs the Approve right on this page.").

To recompute a loan's balances: click Recompute balances on the Summary tab and confirm. The program rebuilds the schedule's paid columns and the running balances from the loan ledger and sets the status again ("Balances and status recomputed from the loan ledger."). Nothing is posted to the books.

Saving a note, releasing collateral and recomputing need the Edit right on Loan Accounts Center.

Importing loans (Import Loans)

For the move from the former system, the members' loans are brought in from a CSV file, one row per loan, as it was released (product, principal, terms, mode of payment, amortization type, rate and release date) with the totals paid on it up to a cut-off date. Each loan is made Active with its amortization schedule, a loan ledger from its release, and its balances at the cut-off date; from then on it is collected like any loan released here. Import the members first (Import Member).

Where to find it: Import Loans on the title bar of the Loan Accounts Center. Reading and checking a file needs the Create right on Loan Accounts Center; uploading it needs the Approve right there.

The page. Choose the Balancing GL account: the other side of the loans' journal entry, normally Opening Balance Equity for a migration. Each loan's principal balance is debited to the loan receivable account of its product and credited to this account. Enter the Cut-off date: the date of the totals paid in the file. Both fill the cells the file leaves blank; a row can give its own. Download the CSV template gives the columns with sample rows.

Column What to enter
Member code, Member name Required. The member number, and the name, checked against it: every word of the last name and the first name must be there, in any order. The member must be Active and not on hold.
Loan name Required. The loan product: its code or its name, as on Loan Products, for example 6 or Regular Loan.
Principal loan Required. The principal released.
Terms (months / days) Required. 12, 12 months or 12M; 180 days or 180D; 1 year.
Mode of payment Required. Daily, Weekly, Semi-monthly, Monthly, Quarterly, Semi-annual, Annual or Lump sum at maturity.
Amortization type Required. Diminishing, equal amortization; Diminishing, equal principal; or Straight (add-on).
Interest rate % a year 18 or 18% a year; 1.5% a month is taken as 18 a year. Blank: the rate of the product.
Release date Required. YYYY-MM-DD or MM/DD/YYYY.
Principal paid, Interest paid, Penalties paid Required (0 when none). The totals paid on the loan up to the cut-off date, each one amount.
Cut-off date The date of the totals paid. Blank: the date chosen on the page. Not after today, and after the closing date of the books.
Past due interest Optional: the past due interest still unpaid at the cut-off date, as worked out by hand. Blank: worked out by the program.
Overdue penalties Optional: the penalties still unpaid at the cut-off date, as worked out by hand. Blank: worked out by the program.
First due date Optional. Blank: one period after the release date (a lump sum: at the end of the term).
Loan no., PN no. Optional: the loan number of the former system (blank: the next loan number) and the promissory note number (up to 30 characters). A number of the former system that this program would also draw is kept: the program passes over every loan number already on file when it numbers a loan.
Accrued interest Optional: the unpaid interest already booked as income in the former books (in Interest Receivable at the cut-off date). It is debited to Loan Interest Receivable in the voucher, and the interest accrual does not book it again. Blank: none, and the first interest accrual books the interest due and unpaid as income.
Balancing GL account Optional: the code of the GL account. Blank: the account chosen on the page. Not a loans receivable or loan interest receivable account.

How each loan is rebuilt. The program draws the loan's amortization schedule from its release terms, as for a loan released here, then lays the totals paid on it:

  • The principal paid and the interest paid are taken as paid on the due dates of the amortizations, in order, oldest first, though the file gives them as one total each. The amortizations due by the cut-off date and not covered are the loan's past due amortizations; what was paid beyond the amortizations due is taken as paid in advance at the cut-off date.
  • Past due interest, when the column is blank: worked out only for a loan that has matured by the cut-off date, on the principal still unpaid, from the maturity date to the cut-off date, at the loan's rate on the days in a year of the product, for example "3,000.00 × 12 % × 95 days ÷ 365". Interest paid beyond the scheduled interest is taken as paid on it. None when the product's Additional interest when past due is None.
  • Overdue penalties, when the column is blank: worked out by the product's penalty terms (rate, grace days and base) on each amortization past due at the cut-off date, considering the payments laid on it, less the penalties paid.
  • An amount given in the file is used as it is; the row then shows what the program would have worked out, for comparison.

The review. The file is read for review as on Import Member: every row is checked at once, a row with a problem is in red with its messages and the cells they name outlined, and it is checked again as you correct it; Remove the rows with a problem takes the red rows out. A row without a problem shows the loan as it will be: the member and the product, the number of amortizations and the maturity, the balance at the cut-off (principal, interest, penalties), and the amortizations past due with their amount, or current; then how the past due interest and the overdue penalties were found, for example "Overdue penalties worked out: 534.57 on the past-due amortizations, less 250.00 already paid = 284.57." The bar adds up the principal of the loans. A row is refused, for example, when:

  • the member is not on file, has left or is on hold, or the name is not the member's;
  • "Loan "..." is not a loan product.", or the product is not active; the mode of payment, the amortization type, the terms, the rate or a date cannot be read;
  • the release date is after the cut-off date, the cut-off date is after today or not after the closing date of the books;
  • "The principal paid (...) is more than the principal loan (...)."; the interest paid is more than the scheduled interest and the past due interest;
  • "The principal, interest and penalties paid cover the whole loan: there is nothing left to import.";
  • the same loan (member, loan, release date and principal) is already on file, or twice in the file; the loan number is already on file; the PN no. is longer than 30 characters;
  • the balancing account is a loans receivable account; the accrued interest is more than the interest unpaid at the cut-off date.

What the upload saves. Upload (for example Upload 5 loans) asks to confirm and saves every loan, or none if a row still has a problem ("5 loan(s) imported and Active, 49,000.00 in principal balances: 01-6-0000010, ..."). For each loan:

  • the loan, Active (Current, or Past due when an amortization due by today is unpaid), released "Opening" on its release date; its Summary says "brought over from the previous system with its balances as of" the cut-off date;
  • the amortization schedule with what is paid on each amortization; the past due interest is one more amortization, due at the cut-off date and numbered among the others by its date, so a collection takes it before the amortizations due after the cut-off date;
  • the loan ledger: the release, a payment on each due date covered ("Former system: amortization 3 paid"), the past due interest and the penalties up to the cut-off date, and what was paid at the cut-off date;
  • one journal voucher for each cut-off date and balancing account: Dr the loan receivable of each product for the principal balances, Dr Loan Interest Receivable for the accrued interest given, Cr the balancing account; each line in the class of the member's branch. The release opens this voucher; the payments of the former system have no voucher of their own.
Note

The payments of the former system are history: they are not on Loan Collection's list of collections, the Loan Collections report or what Home shows as collected today, and not in the teller's cash, and they are marked as already in the member's loan passbook, so Update Passbook prints only what is collected here. From the day after the cut-off date the loan runs as any other: new penalties count from then, the Internal Ledger works out the additional interest from the cut-off date, and the loan is collected on the Loan Collection Form. The import is kept on Opening Balances Import's history and on the audit trail with the file name and the number of loans. To correct an imported loan, use Loan Adjustments: its voucher cannot be voided.

Loan Documents

Loan Documents prints the papers of one loan: the promissory note, the disclosure statement, the release statement, the amortization schedule, the co-maker statements and the statement of account. It also records and prints the collection notices: payment reminders and demand letters. The promissory note, disclosure statement, release statement, co-maker statements and notices are controlled documents: the first print is the original, and every later print is a copy that needs a reason.

Where to find it: the Documents button on a loan in Loan Accounts Center. Opened any other way, the page goes back to the Loan Accounts Center with "Open the documents from a loan account."

Each document has a Print button and, once printed, says how many times. The Co-maker statement has one button per co-maker ("no co-maker" when there is none), and the Statement of account has a date. The documents open in a new tab, ready to print. The wording of the promissory note, the disclosure notes, the co-maker statement and the letters comes from Document Templates: the template in effect on the release date (for a notice, on its date), or the program's standard wording when there is none.

The documents

Document What it contains
Promissory note PROMISSORY NOTE with the PN number, the release date and the loan number; the wording of the template, filled with the borrower, amount (in figures and words), rate, term, amortization, due dates, penalty terms and co-makers; and the signature lines Borrower, one Co-maker line per co-maker, and Witness.
Disclosure statement DISCLOSURE STATEMENT ON LOAN / CREDIT TRANSACTION under R.A. 3765, Truth in Lending Act. After the loan facts: 1. the loan granted, less each charge and any payoff of a previous loan; 2. the net proceeds; 3. the finance charges (the scheduled interest, and the service and other financial charges) with their total; 4. the non-finance charges (insurance, capital build-up, others); 5. the finance charges as a percentage of the amount financed; 6. the effective interest rate; 7. the payments; 8. the penalty on late payment. Then the disclosure notes of the template and the lines Prepared by (the loan officer), Conforme (borrower) and Date received.
Loan release statement Loan Release Statement in the format of the former program, without the amortization schedule: see The Loan Release Statement below.
Amortization schedule AMORTIZATION SCHEDULE dated today: the loan facts and the schedule with a Paid column (the date paid, or "partial").
Co-maker statement CO-MAKER'S STATEMENT for one co-maker: the template wording with the amount the co-maker guarantees, the co-maker's member number or "(non-member)" and address, and the lines Co-maker, Principal borrower and Witness.
Statement of account STATEMENT OF ACCOUNT as of the date chosen: for an open loan the amortizations past due, principal balance, principal and interest due, penalties, total amount due, next due date and the amount to pay off the loan (for a closed loan, its status); then the ledger up to that date (accruals and voided entries left out), charges positive and payments negative; and the lines Prepared by and Noted by.
Payment reminder, Demand letter PAYMENT REMINDER or DEMAND LETTER numbered after the loan (for example 02-6-0000006-N3): the template wording with the amount due, the due date, the penalty, the balance, the days past due and, for a demand, the settle-by date; signed Very truly yours by the Credit Committee chair / Loans officer (reminder) or the General Manager (demand), with Received by / date.

The signatory names come from Cooperative Profile & Policies.

The Loan Release Statement

The statement follows the format of the cooperative's former program, without the amortization schedule:

  • The cooperative's name, main address and contact number, and the title Loan Release Statement (the division name of Cooperative Profile & Policies is not printed on it).
  • On the left Name of Member, Address, Loan Type, Mode of Payment and Date Release; on the right P.B. No. (the member's passbook number, Member Information), Terms (mos), Interest Rate, Total Interest and Date Due.
  • In a box: Loan Release (the principal of the loan); Less: each deduction from the loan release and the payoff of a previous loan; Net Loan Proceeds. Charges collected separately are named under it.
  • Amortization Details: one column for each year of the schedule with the Principal, the Interest and the Total due in that year, for example 2026 and 2027 for a 12-month loan released in October.
  • Prepared By (the user who released the loan), Approved By (the supervisor who approved the release above the limit, else the approver of the loan), and Loan Proceeds Received By with the line for the Signature Over Printed Name.

The effective interest rate

Item 6 of the disclosure statement is the rate per payment period that discounts the amortizations back to the amount financed, and that rate times the number of periods in a year, for example "1.6543 % a month, 19.85 % a year". The amount financed is what the borrower actually gets the use of: the net proceeds, plus the payoff of the borrower's own previous loan when there is one. When charges are deducted from the proceeds, the effective rate is higher than the stated rate. When it cannot be worked out, the line shows "n/a".

Originals and reprints

The first print of the promissory note, disclosure statement, release statement, a co-maker statement or a notice is the original. Printing it again shows "This ... was already printed 1 time. A reprint is marked as a copy and recorded in the audit trail." Type the Reason for the reprint and click Reprint (or Cancel). The copy is marked "COPY 1 (reprint)", and the reprint and its reason go into the audit trail and the Notices and prints tab of the loan. The statement of account and the amortization schedule are not controlled: they print as often as needed without a reason, and each print is still listed.

Collection notices

The Collection notice card is shown for an open loan. A notice is first recorded on the loan, then printed; each notice is its own document.

To send a payment reminder or a demand letter:

  1. On the loan, click Documents.
  2. Choose the Notice: Payment reminder or Demand letter. It starts with Demand letter when amortizations are due, otherwise with Payment reminder.
  3. Check the Date, and for a demand the Settle by (demand) date (ten days after today at first).
  4. Type the Notes (delivery, received by).
  5. Check the line under the fields, for example "Due today: 14,410.25 (5 amortization(s)); next due 11/10/2026."
  6. Click Record and print. The notice opens in a new tab, ready to print.

The notice records the amount due on its date: the total due, or the next unpaid amortization when nothing is overdue. It is listed under Collection notices on the loan's Notices and prints tab.

Good to know

  • Printing and recording notices need the Print right on Loan Documents.
  • "The loan is not open." Notices are recorded only for an open loan.
  • "Enter the date of the notice."
  • Nothing on this page posts to the books.

Loan Adjustments

Loan Adjustments corrects the balances of an open loan when something was recorded wrongly: a principal that is too high or too low, interest that should not have been charged, or a penalty to remove or add. Each of the three balances (principal, interest and penalty) can be increased or decreased, with a reason, and the adjustment can be printed in the member's loan passbook at once (Print to Passbook). The same page lists the adjustments already made.

To forgive interest or penalties that were correctly charged, use Interest / Penalty Waiving instead: a waiver is requested by one person and approved by another.

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Adjustments. From a loan in the Loan Accounts Center, More > Adjust balances opens the page for that loan.

Opened from the menu, the page asks for the loan in the box Borrower or loan no.: click it and the borrowers with outstanding loans are listed, each with the loan no., the loan product and the principal balance ("Past due" in red when it is); type part of a name, of the loan no. or of the loan product and the list narrows as you type. Click a loan (or press Enter on the one highlighted) and the page opens for it. Typed and sent with Open, a loan no., or a name or loan product that finds one outstanding loan, opens it too; otherwise the page says "No loan no. is ... and it does not find one outstanding loan." Opened for a loan, the page title is "Adjust" and the loan number, the member card is shown, Update Passbook (with the number of lines of the loan not yet printed) prints the loan passbook, and Loan goes back to the loan account.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Principal, Interest, Penalty One line for each balance. Balance shows the balance now. In Change choose Decrease or Increase, and type the Amount. Leave the amount blank on the lines you do not change.
Principal adjusted against (account) The account on the other side of a principal change, for example the account the wrong entry went to. Needed only when the principal line has an amount.
Date The date of the adjustment. It cannot be in the future or in a closed period.
Reason Why the balance is changed. Required. It is the memo of the ledger line and of the voucher.
Supervisor approval Needed when you do not have the Approve right on this page (see Supervisor approval).
Print to Passbook Beside Record the adjustment, ticked at first. Ticked, the Print to Passbook window opens as soon as the adjustment is recorded, to print it in the loan passbook (see "In the loan passbook" below). Untick it to print the adjustment later with Update Passbook, here or on the Loan Collection Form. It is shown to users who may print passbooks (the print right on Passbook Printing).

The note under the form sums up the rules: a principal change posts to loans receivable against the account chosen; interest and penalty changes adjust the member ledger only, because they are income only when collected; a decrease settles the oldest unpaid interest and the last principal amortizations.

Step by step

To adjust a loan balance:

  1. Open the loan in the Loan Accounts Center and choose More > Adjust balances, or open Loans > Loan Adjustments, click Borrower or loan no. and choose the loan (type part of the name, the loan no. or the loan product to find it).
  2. On the line to change, choose Decrease or Increase and type the Amount.
  3. For a principal change, choose the account in Principal adjusted against (account).
  4. Check the Date and type the Reason.
  5. If you do not have the Approve right on this page, open Supervisor approval: the supervisor types their username and password (Supervisor is here), or you type a one-time code (Approval code, see Approval Codes).
  6. Leave Print to Passbook ticked to print the adjustment in the loan passbook now, or untick it to print it later.
  7. Click Record the adjustment and confirm with Record the adjustment. The page shows "Adjustment recorded on", the loan number and the change, for example "interest less 120.00".
  8. With Print to Passbook ticked, the Print to Passbook window opens: place the loan passbook in the passbook printer at the page and line it names, click Print, and once it has printed answer Yes, mark them printed (or No, print again later). The window prints every line of the loan not yet printed, the adjustment last, over as many pages as needed (see Passbook Printing). Print to Passbook on the message opens the window again.
Example

An interest increase of ₱120.00 was recorded on loan 02-6-0000006 by mistake. To take it back, on the Interest line choose Decrease, type 120.00, and give the reason "Reverses the interest increase recorded by mistake". No voucher is made: the list shows "ledger only" in the Voucher column, and the interest balance falls from ₱797.09 to ₱677.09.

Tip

On a straight (add-on) loan the payoff includes every scheduled interest, even of the amortizations not yet due. An Interest decrease on this page is how a rebate of that interest is given before the loan is paid off.

The adjustment list

Choose From and To (the default is from January 1 of this year to today) and click Show. Opened for a loan, the list shows only that loan.

Column What it shows
Date The date of the adjustment.
Loan The loan number (click it to open the loan ledger) and the member.
Principal, Interest, Penalty The change: an increase with +, a decrease with -.
Reason The reason, who made the adjustment and, for a principal change, the account it was made against.
Voucher The voucher number for a principal change (click it to open the voucher), or "ledger only".

An adjustment whose voucher was voided is struck through.

In the loan passbook

An adjustment is a line of the loan passbook with the code AJ, printed with Print to Passbook or by Update Passbook like any other line:

  • a decrease prints like a payment, in the column of the balance it changes (principal, interest or fines), and an increase prints negative there;
  • the loan balance of the line is the principal balance after the adjustment;
  • the reference is the voucher no. of a principal change, or ADJ when only the interest or the penalty changed (no voucher).

The passbook view of the Loan Ledger Form shows the adjustments the same way, the reference followed by "(adjustment)". The adjustments recorded before version 37 were never meant for the passbook: the upgrade 2027_01_31_adjustment_passbook.sql marks them as passed over, so they do not come out on the next printing. They come out again only if the print line is reset to a date before them on Passbook Printing.

What it records

Every adjustment adds an adjustment line to the member's loan ledger and is listed on the page and in the Waivers and Adjustments report. A decrease of interest settles the oldest unpaid interest of the schedule; a decrease of principal settles the last amortizations first.

Only a principal change reaches the general ledger, as a Cooperative Journal Voucher dated the adjustment date with the memo "Loan adjustment", the loan number and the reason:

Change Debit Credit
Principal increase The loans receivable account of the loan The account chosen in Principal adjusted against (account)
Principal decrease The account chosen The loans receivable account of the loan
Interest or penalty, either way No voucher. The member ledger only: interest and penalties reach income when they are collected.

Good to know

  • Adjusting needs the permission "Cooperative: can waive loan interest and penalties, and adjust loan balances" (see User Role Configuration). Without it the page shows: "Adjusting loan balances needs the "can waive loan interest and penalties, and adjust loan balances" permission."
  • Without the Approve right on the page: "Supervisor approval is required: loan balance adjustment. Ask a supervisor to enter their username and password below, or type a one-time approval code." The supervisor must have the Approve right on Loan Adjustments.
  • Closed loans (fully paid, renewed, written off, cancelled) are not adjusted: "The loan is fully paid; closed loans are not adjusted."
  • "Enter at least one amount." and "Enter the reason for the adjustment."
  • "Choose the account the principal adjustment is charged against."
  • A decrease cannot be more than the balance: "The interest decrease exceeds the interest balance (797.09)."
  • A principal adjustment is undone by voiding its voucher. The loan ledger and the schedule are worked out again without it.
  • "The product of this loan has no passbook type (Loan Products)": the adjustment is recorded, but there is no passbook to print it in. Choose the passbook type of the loan product on Loan Products.

Interest / Penalty Waiving

A waiver forgives part or all of the interest or penalty a member owes on a loan, for example by a board or committee resolution. It takes two people: one user requests the waiver, and another user who may approve posts it or rejects it. Nothing changes on the loan until the waiver is posted.

Where to find it: Loans > Interest / Penalty Waiving lists the waivers. To request one, open the loan in the Loan Accounts Center and choose More > Waive interest or penalty. Users who may approve waivers see "Waivers waiting for approval" in the alerts on the Home dashboard.

Opened for a loan, the page shows the member card, the balances of the loan and the Request a waiver form on the left, and the waivers of that loan on the right.

The balances

Line What it shows
Interest balance (scheduled, unpaid) All the interest of the schedule not yet paid, due or not. This is the most interest a waiver can take.
Of which already due The unpaid interest of the amortizations already due.
Penalty posted The penalties already on the member ledger. This is the most penalty a waiver can take.
Penalty earned, not yet posted Penalty earned through today that is not yet on the ledger.
Interest in income already (accrued) Interest of this loan already taken into income by the interest accrual and not yet collected.

A penalty can be waived only after it is posted. When there is penalty earned but not yet posted, click Post the penalty through today first and confirm: the penalty through today goes on the member ledger ("Penalty through today posted:" and the amount), and can then be waived.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Interest to waive Up to the interest balance.
Penalty to waive Up to the penalty posted.
Date The date the waiver takes effect. The waiver is posted with this date when it is approved.
Reason / board or committee resolution Required. Shown in the list and on the loan ledger.

Step by step

To request a waiver:

  1. Open the loan and choose More > Waive interest or penalty.
  2. To waive penalty not yet posted, click Post the penalty through today first and confirm.
  3. Type the Interest to waive and/or the Penalty to waive.
  4. Check the Date and type the Reason / board or committee resolution.
  5. Click Request the waiver and confirm. The page shows "Waiver request ... recorded. It takes effect when an approver posts it."

To approve or reject a waiver:

  1. Open Loans > Interest / Penalty Waiving. Show is set to Waiting for approval.
  2. In the Status column of the waiver, type Remarks if you have any. Remarks are required to reject.
  3. Click Approve and confirm to post the waiver to the loan ("Waiver posted to the loan."), or click Reject ("Waiver rejected."). A rejected waiver keeps its reason with "Rejected:" and your remarks added.

The Approve and Reject buttons appear only for users who may decide, and never on a waiver you requested yourself (an administrator excepted).

The waiver list

Show picks Waiting for approval, Posted, Rejected or All. The columns are Date, Loan (with the member), Interest, Penalty, Reason, Requested / decided (who requested it, then who approved or rejected it and when) and Status (Waiting for approval, Posted, Rejected, or Voided when the voucher of a posted waiver was voided).

What it records

A request records nothing on the loan. When the waiver is posted:

  • The member ledger gets a waiver line ("Waiver:" and the reason) that lowers the interest and penalty balances. Waived interest settles the oldest unpaid interest of the schedule first.
  • The loan keeps the totals of interest waived and penalty waived.
  • Waived interest that was already accrued into income comes out of income, in a Cooperative Journal Voucher dated the waiver date ("Interest waiver" and the loan number). The rest of the waiver is on the member ledger only, because unpaid interest and penalties are not yet in the general ledger.
Account Debit Credit
The interest income account of the loan Waived interest, up to the accrued interest of the loan
Interest Receivable on Loans The same amount

Good to know

  • Requesting needs the Create right on this page. Approving or rejecting needs the Approve right on this page and the permission "Cooperative: can waive loan interest and penalties, and adjust loan balances": "Approving waivers needs the Approve right on this page and the waiver permission."
  • "A waiver is approved by someone other than the user who requested it."
  • "Enter the interest or penalty to waive." and "Enter the reason for the waiver."
  • "The waiver exceeds the balance (interest 720.00, penalty 150.00)." Lower the amounts, or post the penalty first.
  • "The balances have changed since the request; the waiver now exceeds them." The loan was paid or adjusted after the request. Reject it and request a new one.
  • "Enter the reason for rejecting the waiver."
  • Waivers can be requested only on open loans: "The loan is not open."

Restructure / Renew / Write Off

This page handles the end of a loan other than by full payment. A loan is renewed or restructured by a new loan application whose release pays off the old loan from the proceeds. A loan that cannot be collected is written off, and money collected on it afterwards is recorded as a recovery.

A renewal or restructuring application starts with the Capacity to pay of the borrower's last loan application, and its credit investigation with the borrower's last investigation (see Loan Application Form and Credit Investigation).

Where to find it: Loans > Restructure / Renew / Write Off. From a loan in the Loan Accounts Center, More > Renew, restructure or write off (on a written-off loan, Record a recovery) opens the page for that loan.

Opened from the menu, the page lists two kinds of loans. A current loan is not listed: renew it from its loan account.

List What it shows
Loans in arrears The past-due and restructured loans: Loan, Member, Product, Oldest unpaid (the due date of the oldest unpaid amortization), Days (days since that date, 0 when it is not yet due), Balance (the principal balance) and Status. Open opens the loan on this page.
Written off Loan, Member, Written off (the date), Principal (the amount released), Recovered and Reason. Recovery opens the loan to record a recovery.

Opened for an open loan, the page shows the member card and four figures: Principal balance, In arrears (the principal, interest and penalty due, the number of amortizations due and the days past due), Payoff today and Accrued interest in income. Below are the Renew or restructure form and the Write off form. A loan that is fully paid, renewed or cancelled shows only a note that there is nothing to restructure.

Renew or restructure

A renewal gives the member a new loan that pays the old one off. A restructuring gives new terms to a loan in arrears.
