Both are made the same way: the form creates a loan application, which is validated, approved and released like any other in the Loan Application Center. The old loan is paid off only when the new loan is released.

Field What to enter / what it means
This is a Renewal (a new loan that pays this one off) or Restructuring (new terms for a loan in arrears). It starts at Restructuring for a past-due loan and at Renewal otherwise.
Date The application date.
Product The product of the new loan. It starts at the product of the old loan.
Amount The amount applied for. The program suggests the smallest whole-peso amount that still covers today's payoff after the charges the loan's product deducts.
Term, in The term of the new loan, in months or days. It starts at the term of the old loan.
Mode The mode of payment: Daily, Weekly, Semi-monthly, Monthly, Quarterly, Semi-annual, Annual or Lump sum at maturity.
Amortization Equal loan (equal amortization) or Fixed principal (equal principal).
Reason / committee action Added to the purpose of the application and kept as its remarks.

To renew or restructure a loan:

  1. Open the loan: Open in Loans in arrears, or More > Renew, restructure or write off on the loan account.
  2. Choose This is a, then check the Product, Amount, Term, Mode and Amortization.
  3. Type the Reason / committee action.
  4. Click Create the application and confirm. The new application opens: "Application ... created. Validate, approve and release it as usual; the release pays off ... from the proceeds."
  5. Add co-makers and collateral on the application if the product requires them: they are not copied from the old loan. The purpose is Renewal or Restructuring, and the incomes and net disposable income are taken from the member's financial information.
  6. Validate, approve and release the application as usual (see Loan Application Center and Loan Release).

The loan being paid off does not count against the product's limit of open loans or against the check for amortizations in arrears on other loans. While an application already renews or restructures the loan, the form is not shown and the page names that application instead.

What the release records: the payoff of the old loan is worked out on the release date (principal balance, interest due, penalties, and for a diminishing-balance loan the interest to date; for a straight loan every scheduled interest) and is taken from the proceeds. The release voucher credits the old loan's receivable, interest and penalty accounts for it. The old loan's ledger gets the payoff line ("Paid off by renewal" or "Paid off by restructuring" and the new loan number), and the old loan becomes Renewed (paid by a new loan). The new loan starts as Current after a renewal, or Restructured after a restructuring. A restructured loan keeps that status until it is closed, so it stays visible in the portfolio reports.

Write off

Writing off removes an uncollectible loan from loans receivable. Only a user with the Approve right on this page sees the form; others see "Writing off a loan needs the Approve right on this page."

Field What to enter / what it means
Date The write-off date.
Against the allowance for probable losses Ticked (the default), the principal is charged to the allowance for probable losses. Unticked, it is charged to the provision for probable losses (an expense).
Board resolution no. / reason Required. Kept on the loan and shown in the lists and reports.

To write off a loan: open the loan on this page, fill in the Write off form and click Write off. The program asks to confirm, stating the principal balance that leaves loans receivable and that the interest and penalty balances are dropped from the member ledger. After the write-off the loan account opens: "Loan ... written off (voucher ...). Recoveries are recorded on this page."

What it records: a Cooperative Journal Voucher ("Loan write-off", the loan number and the reason):

Account Debit Credit
Allowance for Probable Losses on Loans (or Provision for Probable Losses on Loans when the box is unticked) Principal balance
The loans receivable account of the loan Principal balance
The interest income account of the loan Accrued interest not yet collected
Interest Receivable on Loans Accrued interest not yet collected

The accounts named here are the cooperative's Module Default Accounts. On the member ledger a write-off line clears the principal, interest and penalty balances to zero, and the loan becomes Written off.

Recovery after write-off

Opened for a written-off loan, the page shows when and why it was written off, the Principal written off and the Recovered so far, the Record a recovery form, and the Recoveries already recorded (date, receipt number and amount).

Field What to enter / what it means
Amount The amount received.
Date The date received.
Received in Cash (teller drawer): the money goes to the cash drawer of your open teller shift, shown under the form. Bank deposit: choose the Bank account.
OR no. The official receipt number. Leave it blank for the next number of the branch.

To record a recovery:

  1. Open the loan: Recovery in the Written off list, or More > Record a recovery on the loan account.
  2. Type the Amount and check the Date.
  3. Choose Received in. For cash, the money goes to the drawer of your open teller shift (see Teller Shift & Cash Count). For a bank deposit, choose the Bank account.
  4. Type the OR no., or leave it blank.
  5. Click Record the recovery and confirm. The official receipt opens: "Recovery of ... recorded on OR ...".

What it records: an Official Receipt numbered with the OR no. ("Recovery on written-off loan" and the loan number). A cash recovery is counted in your teller shift.

Account Debit Credit
The cash drawer of the shift, or the bank account Amount
Recovery of Loans Written Off Amount

The loan ledger gets a recovery line with all balances still zero, and the amount is added to the loan's recovered total. The loan stays written off.

Good to know

  • Renewing or restructuring needs the Create right on this page and on the Loan Application Form. Recording a recovery needs the Create right on this page. Writing off needs the Approve right on this page.
  • At release: "The charges and the previous loan payoff (...) exceed the approved amount." The approved amount must cover the payoff on the release date plus the charges deducted.
  • "Enter the board resolution / reason."
  • "Recoveries are recorded on written-off loans only."
  • "Open your teller shift first (Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count)." Shown for a cash recovery when the policy Cash transactions need an open teller shift is on.
  • "Official receipt number ... is already used."
  • Dates cannot be in the future or in a closed period.

Post Loan Penalties & Interest Accrual

This page runs three batch jobs on the open loans: posting the penalties earned through a date to the member ledgers, accruing the interest earned on diminishing-balance loans into income, and refreshing which loans are current or past due. Each job shows a preview first where it makes sense, and each can be run again safely: a second run for the same date adds nothing.

Where to find it: Loans > Post Loan Penalties & Interest Accrual.

Penalties

Penalties are also worked out at the teller window when a loan is paid, unless the policy Compute penalties when a payment is received is set to No on Cooperative Profile & Policies. Posting them here puts them on the member ledgers in one run, for month-end statements and the aging reports. With that policy set to No, only the penalties posted here are collected.

The penalty of a loan is worked out from the penalty terms the loan was released with (see Interest and penalty on Loan Products):

  • An amortization is overdue when it is unpaid and its due date is before the Compute through date.
  • Counting starts the grace days after the due date, or on the date penalties were last computed through, if that is later.
  • Penalty = base × penalty rate ÷ 100 × days ÷ days in a year (365 or 360). The base is the unpaid principal and interest of each overdue amortization, its unpaid principal only, or the whole principal balance, as the product's Penalty on says.

To post penalties:

  1. Type the Compute through date (today at the latest) and click Preview.
  2. Check the list: Loan (click it to see the loan's penalty computation), Member, From (the date counting starts on the first overdue amortization), Amortizations (how many are charged) and Penalty, with the number of loans and the total at the bottom.
  3. Click Post penalties and confirm with Post penalties. The page shows "Penalties through ... posted on ... loan(s): .... They are income when collected."

The preview lists only loans with a penalty to add. A loan whose penalties were already computed through that date or later is left out, and posting again for the same date adds nothing. The run covers the open loans of every branch.

What it records: on each loan, a penalty line ("Penalty through" and the date) on the member ledger that raises the penalty balance. Nothing goes to the general ledger: the penalty income account is credited when the penalty is collected.

Interest accrual (diminishing-balance loans)

The accrual takes into income the interest that open diminishing-balance loans have earned but members have not yet paid, usually at month end. For each loan released before the Interest earned through date, the interest earned is the scheduled interest of the amortizations due by that date, plus the interest on the principal balance for the days since the last of them. From that the program takes away the interest already collected, waived or adjusted down, and the interest already accrued and still outstanding. What is left is accrued. Straight (add-on) loans are not accrued, nor the loans of products whose Accrue interest monthly is No.

To accrue interest:

  1. Type Interest earned through (it starts at the last day of last month) and the Posting date (the date of the voucher; it starts at the same date).
  2. Click Preview. The list shows each Loan, Member and To accrue, with the total. "Nothing to accrue through ..." means every loan is accrued already.
  3. Click Post the accrual and confirm with Post accrual. The page shows "Interest accrued on ... loan(s): ... (voucher ...)."

What it records: one Cooperative Journal Voucher dated the posting date ("Loan interest accrual through" and the date), with two lines for each loan, carrying the member's name and the class of the loan's branch:

Account Debit Credit
Interest Receivable on Loans Interest to accrue
The interest income account of the loan Interest to accrue

Each loan ledger gets an accrual line that does not change the member's interest balance. When the member pays, the payment credits Interest Receivable on Loans first, up to what was accrued, and interest income for the rest. A waiver or write-off takes accrued interest back out of income, and so does a diminishing-balance loan paid off early, for interest accrued but no longer earned. Like the penalty run, the accrual covers every branch.

Loan statuses

A loan becomes past due the day after an amortization is missed, and current again when it is caught up. The Loan Accounts Center and the Home dashboard refresh this when they open. Refresh statuses does it now for every branch and shows how many loans are current and past due ("Statuses refreshed: ... current, ... past due."). Restructured loans keep their status.

History

  • Accrual vouchers lists the last accrual vouchers with their date, number (click it to open the voucher), memo and amount. A voided voucher is greyed.
  • Recent runs lists the last penalty runs, accruals and status refreshes: when, what, who, the number of loans (or, for a refresh, the number of past-due loans before and after) and the amount.

Good to know

  • Anyone who may open the page can preview. Posting penalties, posting the accrual and refreshing statuses need the Create right on the page.
  • "Enter a date up to today." Penalties cannot be posted through a future date.
  • "No interest to accrue: every loan is already accrued through that date."
  • To correct an accrual, void its voucher; the accrued interest of each loan is worked out again without it.

Loan Products

A loan product (Regular Loan, Emergency Loan, Productive Loan) holds the terms every loan of that kind starts from: the amounts and terms allowed, the interest method and rates, the penalty, who may borrow, the charges taken at release and the accounts. Set the products up before taking loan applications.

Every released loan keeps its own copy of the rate, method, term, penalty terms, accounts and schedule. A later change to the product never touches loans already released ("Loan product saved. Loans already released keep their own terms.").

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Products. The Loan Product List report prints the same list.

The list shows each product's Code, Product (with its loan type), Interest (the method, and the amortization for diminishing balance), Rate (the default rate), Payment (the mode of payment), Term, Amount (smallest to largest, or "no limit"), Open loans and Balance (their principal balance). Inactive products are greyed. Click a product name to open it; click New loan product to add one.

Product

Field What to enter / what it means
Code Up to 10 characters, saved in capitals, not used by another product. With the usual loan number format the code is the middle part of the loan number: 01-6-0000002 is a loan of product 6 at branch 01.
Name The product name, for example Regular Loan.
Loan type / group A grouping word such as Provident, Productive or Emergency. Shown under the name in the list.
Description Optional notes.
Loans receivable account The asset account the loans of this product are kept in. Module default uses Loans Receivable - Current from Module Default Accounts. It cannot be changed while the product has open loans.
Interest income account Where interest is credited when collected or accrued. Module default uses Interest Income on Loans.
Penalty income account Where penalties are credited when collected. Module default uses Fines, Penalties and Surcharges.
Loan passbook The passbook type the loans of this product print in on Passbook Printing, or None. Passbook types are set up on Passbook Types.
Order The position of the product in the drop-downs.
Active Untick to stop new applications for the product. Its open loans are not affected.

Amount, term and payment

Field What to enter / what it means
Smallest loan, Largest loan (0 = no limit) The amounts a member may apply for. The application's eligibility checks test them.
Open loans per member How many loans of this product a member may have at once (at least 1). Open loans and applications still in process both count.
Term in Months or Days: the unit of the shortest and longest term.
Shortest term, Longest term The terms allowed. The longest term must be more than zero and not less than the shortest.
Mode of payment The usual mode for the product: Daily, Weekly, Semi-monthly, Monthly, Quarterly, Semi-annual, Annual or Lump sum at maturity. An application starts with it and may change it.
Charges are How the charges ticked under Charges on release are paid:
  • Deducted from loan approved: the loan is the approved amount and the charges come out of the net proceeds.
  • Collected separately: the loan is the approved amount, the net proceeds are not reduced, and the charges are not part of the release voucher; collect them on an official receipt.
  • Added to the loan (capitalized): the charges are added to the principal, so the member receives the approved amount and repays the charges with the loan.

Interest and penalty

Field What to enter / what it means
Interest method Diminishing balance: interest each period on the principal still owed. Straight (add-on): interest for the whole term on the full principal, split evenly over the amortizations.
Amortization Shown for diminishing balance. Equal loan (equal amortization): the same amount every period; the interest part falls and the principal part grows. Fixed principal (equal principal): the same principal every period plus the interest on the balance, so the amortizations get smaller. An application may choose the other type.
Default rate (% a year) The yearly interest rate when no row of Rates by term and amount matches. A zero rate on a diminishing-balance product needs the Zero-interest product box ticked.
Option 1, Option 2, Option 3 Other yearly rates. The Rate list of a loan application offers the product rate (by term and amount) or any of the default rate and these options.
Interest period Same as the mode of payment: each period's interest is the yearly rate for one period (one month is 1/12 of a year; a semi-monthly period is 15 days, a week 7 days and a day 1 day, of the days in a year). Actual days between amortizations: each period's interest is worked out on the real number of days between its amortization dates.
Days in a year 365 or 360. Used for day-based periods, terms in days, the interest to date on a payoff and the penalties.
Accrue interest monthly Yes or No (income when collected). With Yes, the interest accrual on Post Loan Penalties & Interest Accrual takes the open diminishing-balance loans of the product; with No, they are left out and their interest is income when it is collected.
Penalty (% a year) The yearly penalty rate on late amounts. 0 means no penalty.
Grace days Days after a due date before the penalty starts. The grace days themselves are not charged.
Penalty on The base of the penalty: Loan arrears (unpaid principal + interest) of each overdue amortization, Principal in arrears (its unpaid principal only), or Outstanding balance (the whole principal balance, from the first overdue amortization).
Additional interest when past due (Internal Ledger) How the Internal Ledger of a loan works out the additional interest it earns when it is past due, per day at the loan's rate: By the interest method (the standard: diminishing-balance loans on each missed amortization, add-on loans after maturity), On the principal of each missed amortization, from its due date, On the principal balance after the maturity date, or None.
Zero-interest product (allowed with a zero default rate) Tick to save a diminishing-balance product with a zero default rate.
Example

₱20,000.00 at 18% a year, diminishing balance, equal loan, monthly for 12 months: 12 amortizations of ₱1,833.60, total interest ₱2,003.20. The Regular Loan charges a penalty of 36% a year on loan arrears with 3 grace days. If the ₱1,833.60 due 09/30/2026 is still unpaid, the penalty computed through 10/11/2026 counts from 10/03/2026: 1,833.60 × 36 ÷ 100 × 8 ÷ 365 = ₱14.47.

With the mode Lump sum at maturity, the loan has one amortization at the end of the term, and its interest is worked out like add-on interest for the whole term.

Who may borrow

These are the eligibility rules. The loan application runs them, with the checks every product has (the member is active, has no hold, and has no amortization in arrears on other loans). An officer with the Approve right on the Loan Application Center may override a failed check with a reason (see Loan Application Center). A rule left at 0 or blank is not checked.

Field What to enter / what it means
Member for at least (months) Months since the membership date.
Co-makers required How many co-makers the application must list.
Net disposable income at least The smallest net disposable income, taken from the Financial Information tab of the member.
Paid-up capital rule, Amount or % At least the amount (a peso amount) or At least % of the loan (a percentage of the amount applied for) of paid-up share capital.
Savings rule, Amount or % The same for the member's savings.
Age from, Age to (0 = none) The age range. A member without a date of birth fails this check.
Only members of the segmentation Limits the product to one member segmentation, or Any. Segmentations are set up on Member Types, Clusters & Other Lists.
Credit investigation before approval Ticked, a validated application goes to credit investigation and cannot be submitted for approval without one.
Collateral required Ticked, the application must list at least one collateral item.
Documents to complete before release (one per line) The pre-release checklist, one document per line. Each application lists these documents, and the loan cannot be released until all are marked complete.

Charges on release

Tick the charges of Loan Charges that the product takes at release. Each one shows how it is computed, with the rate or amount for a fixed charge. Only active charges are taken. Charges are (above) decides whether they are deducted, collected separately or added to the loan.

Rates by term and amount, with effective dates

Rate rows give a different rate to certain terms or amounts, and let a new rate start on a set date. Click Add a rate for a row:

Column What to enter / what it means
Term from, Term to The range of the term. A Term to of 0 means any term.
Amount from, Amount to The range of the amount applied for. An Amount to of 0 means any amount.
Effective The date the rate starts. Required, today or later.
Rate % The yearly rate.

When an application uses the product rate, the program looks at the rows effective on or before the application date, the most recent first, and takes the first row whose term and amount ranges both fit. If none fits, the Default rate applies. Rows already in effect are greyed with "in effect" and cannot be changed: they are kept as history. To change a rate, add a new row with a later effective date.

Example

The Regular Loan has a default rate of 18% and one row in effect since 01/01/2020: term 25 to 36, any amount, 16.5%. A Regular Loan for 30 months gets 16.5%; one for 12 months gets 18%.

Step by step

To set up a loan product:

  1. Open Loans > Loan Products and click New loan product.
  2. Fill in Product: the Code, Name and the accounts (or leave the module defaults).
  3. Fill in Amount, term and payment.
  4. Fill in Interest and penalty.
  5. Fill in Who may borrow and the documents to complete before release.
  6. Tick the Charges on release.
  7. Add rows to Rates by term and amount if some terms or amounts get a different rate.
  8. Click Save loan product.

Good to know

  • Adding a product needs the Create right on the page and changing one the Edit right; without it the form is read-only.
  • "Enter the code and name." and "Product code ... is used."
  • "Check the term range." and "Check the amount range."
  • "The default rate is zero. Tick "zero-interest product" if that is intended."
  • "Open loans of this product are in the current receivable account; they keep it. Make a new product for a different account."
  • "Each term / amount rate needs its effective date." and "A new rate takes effect today or later; rates already in effect are kept as history."

Loan Charges

Loan charges are the fees and deductions taken when a loan is released: service fee, filing fee, loan protection insurance, capital build-up. Each charge is set up once here and then ticked on the loan products that take it (see Charges on release).

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Charges.

The list shows each charge's Code, Charge, Type, Computed (how, with the rate or amount of a fixed charge), Credited to and Products (how many products take it). Inactive charges are greyed. Click Edit to change a charge in the form on the right.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Code A short code not used by another charge. It is entered only for a new charge and cannot be changed later.
Name The name shown on the release and the loan.
Type Financial (income of the cooperative), such as a service or filing fee. Non-financial (payable to a third party), such as an insurance premium or notarial fee collected for someone else. Savings (credited to a member savings / share capital product), such as capital build-up retention.
Credited to (account) For a financial or non-financial charge: the account the charge is credited to, an income account for a financial charge and a payable for a non-financial one.
Credited to the member's For a savings charge: the savings or share capital product. At release the charge is credited to the member's account in that product; if the member has none, the program opens one.
Computed as How the amount is worked out:
  • None: nothing is charged.
  • Fixed rate (% of the loan): the rate times the approved amount.
  • Fixed amount: the same amount on every loan.
  • Customize rate by term range and Customize rate by amount range: a rate that depends on the term or on the amount.
  • Customize amount by term range and Customize amount by amount range: a peso amount that depends on the term or on the amount.
Rate % or amount The rate or the amount for Fixed rate and Fixed amount.
From (term or amount), To, Rate % or amount The ranges, shown for the four customize choices. By term: the term in months. By amount: the loan amount. Click Add a range for more rows. The ranges must not overlap.
Effective from The first release date the charge applies to. A loan released before it is not charged; blank means the charge always applies. The schedule preview of an application uses the date it is shown.
Active Untick to stop taking the charge on new releases.

A range applies when the term or amount is from its From to its To, both included. When no range fits, the charge is not taken on that loan.

Example

Loan Protection Insurance is computed as Customize rate by term range: 1 to 12 months at 1.00%, 12.01 to 36 months at 1.50%. On a ₱50,000.00 loan for 24 months it is ₱750.00, credited to 2360 Loan Insurance Payable. The Regular Loan takes the Service Fee (3%) and Share Capital Build-up (5%), deducted: a ₱50,000.00 Regular Loan pays ₱1,500.00 of service fee, ₱2,500.00 goes to the member's paid-up share capital, and the net proceeds are ₱46,000.00.

To add a charge:

  1. In New charge, type the Code and Name.
  2. Choose the Type, then the account (Credited to (account)) or the savings product (Credited to the member's).
  3. Choose Computed as and type the Rate % or amount, or the ranges.
  4. Click Save charge. The page shows "Charge saved. Loans already released keep the charges they were released with."
  5. Tick the charge on the loan products that take it.

What it records at release: with the product's charges deducted or added to the loan, the release voucher credits a financial or non-financial charge to its account, and a savings charge to the member's savings or share capital account, with a deposit on that account. With the charges collected separately, they are not part of the release voucher. Each released loan keeps its own list of the charges it was released with.

Good to know

  • Adding a charge needs the Create right on the page; changing one the Edit right.
  • "Enter a code that is not used yet." and "Enter the name."
  • "Choose the account the charge is credited to (income for financial charges, a payable for non-financial ones)."
  • "Choose the savings or share capital product the charge is credited to."
  • "Enter the ranges of the charge." and "The ranges overlap (12.00 and 12.00)." Start each range above the end of the one before.

Loan Approval Matrix

The approval matrix says who must approve a loan application, by amount and by product. Each row is one approval level. A loan needs every active level whose amount range covers the amount applied for, in the order of the level numbers, each by a different person.

When no active level covers the amount and product of an application (for example when the matrix is empty), one approval by anyone with the Approve right on the Loan Application Center approves it.

Where to find it: Loans > Loan Approval Matrix.

The list shows each Level, the Amount range, the Product (or "Every product"), Approved by (the role, or "Anyone with Approve on the page", meaning the Approve right on the Loan Application Center) and the Position printed. Inactive levels are greyed. Click Edit to change a level.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Level The order of the approval: level 1 approves first.
From amount, To (0 = any) The range of the amount applied for that needs this level, both ends included. A To of 0 means any amount from From amount up.
Product One product, or Every product.
Role that approves The role whose members approve at this level, or Anyone with the Approve right (on the Loan Application Center). Roles are set up on User Role Configuration.
Position printed on the approval The title shown with the approval on the application, for example Credit Committee or General Manager.
Active Untick to stop using the level. Levels are not deleted.

To add a level: fill in New level and click Save level ("Approval level saved. Applications already approved are not affected.").

How the approval works

  • The levels are chosen by the amount applied for and the product of the application. The application shows the levels it needs ("Level 2 needed:" and the position) and each approval given.
  • Each approval fills the lowest level not yet approved. Every approver needs the Approve right on the Loan Application Center and, where the level names a role, that role.
  • The application is approved when every level it needs has approved. An approver cannot approve the same application twice, so each level is a different person.
  • When an application is returned for correction and submitted again, the approvals start over.
Example

The sample has level 1, from 0.00 to any amount, Credit Committee, and level 2, from 100,000.01 to any amount, General Manager. A ₱50,000.00 loan needs one approval. A ₱150,000.00 loan needs the Credit Committee approval and then the General Manager approval, by two different users.

Good to know

  • Changing the matrix needs the Create or Edit right on this page.
  • "The amount range is reversed." From amount is above To.
  • "Level 2 is approved by the role General Manager." You do not have the role the next level needs.
  • "You have already approved this application."
  • "You are not allowed to approve loan applications." You do not have the Approve right on the Loan Application Center.

Loan reports

The loan reports show the products, the loans receivable, releases, amounts due and collected, the age of overdue loans, the risk in the portfolio, and the waivers and write-offs. They share the report tools explained in Working with reports: Refresh, Columns, Print / PDF, Export to spreadsheet, Email link, Modify Report, Memorize, and clicking a loan or voucher number to open it.

All of them are in the sidebar under Loans > Reports and in the Loans card of the Report Center. Loan Releases, Loan Due vs. Collection, Loans Due for Collection, Loan Collection List, Loans With No Payments and Loan Statement of Account are under the heading Loans Summary; the three aging reports and Loan Portfolio Analysis are under Loans Aging. Where a report has a Branch filter, a user limited to one branch sees only the loans of that branch.

The Schedule of Loans Receivable, Loans With No Payments, the three aging reports and Loan Portfolio Analysis work out each loan's principal balance on the As of date from the loan ledger, and take the loans released by that date that still have a balance on it.

How the aging reports age a loan

An amortization is overdue when part of its principal or interest is unpaid at the As of date and its due date is before that date. An amortization due on the As of date itself is not yet late. The days past due go into these buckets:

Column Days past due
Non-delinquent 0 (not past due)
1 to 30 days 1 to 30
1 to 3 months 31 to 90
3 to 6 months 91 to 180
6 to 12 months 181 to 365
Over 12 months More than 365

The three reports put a loan's principal balance into these buckets in three ways:

  • By maturity: a loan ages only once its maturity date has passed. Until then the whole balance is non-delinquent, even with amortizations in arrears. After maturity the whole balance goes to the bucket of the days since maturity.
  • At PAR (portfolio at risk): one missed or short amortization puts the whole balance at risk. The whole balance goes to the bucket of the days since the oldest overdue due date.
  • By amortization: only the unpaid principal of each overdue amortization is delinquent, each in the bucket of the days since its own due date. The rest of the balance is non-delinquent. An overdue amortization whose principal is paid and only its interest unpaid adds nothing to the delinquent amount.
Example

On 10/11/2026, loan 02-6-0000006 has a balance of ₱31,949.16, its oldest overdue amortization is 123 days past due, and it matures on 03/10/2027. At PAR the whole ₱31,949.16 is in 3 to 6 months. By maturity it is all non-delinquent, because the loan has not matured. By amortization only the unpaid principal of the overdue amortizations is delinquent, each by its own age, and the rest is non-delinquent.

Loan Product List

Lists every loan product with its terms and how much is lent under it.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Loan Product List.

The columns: Code, Product (click it to open the product), Interest (the method), Rate % (the default rate), Term, Payments (the mode of payment), Amount (smallest to largest, or "any"), Charges, Penalty (the rate and grace days, or "none"), Open loans, Outstanding (the principal balance of the open loans) and Status (Active or Inactive). The total line adds the open loans and the outstanding balance. The report has no filter.

Schedule of Loans Receivable

Lists every loan with a principal balance on a date, by product, and checks the total against the general ledger. File it with the balance sheet as the schedule of loans receivable.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Schedule of Loans Receivable.

Filters: As of, Product, Branch and Officer.

The columns: Loan (click it to open the loan), Member, Member no. (add it with Columns), Released, Maturity, Rate %, Principal (the principal of the loan at release), Balance (on the As of date) and Status (today). Each product has a total, and the last line gives the number of loans and the grand total.

The note above the report gives the loans receivable in the general ledger on the same date and says whether it is "the same as the schedule" or "(differs from the schedule)". With a filter, or for a user limited to one branch, the note says "Filtered: compare with the general ledger on the unfiltered report." To find a difference, use Schedule of Loans vs GL.

Loan Releases

Lists the loans released in a period, with the charges, the previous loan paid off and the net proceeds.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Loan Releases.

Filters: Dates (with From and To), Product, Branch and Officer.

The columns: Released, Loan, Member, Product, Term, Rate %, Principal, Charges, Previous loan paid off (on a renewal or restructuring), Net proceeds, Released by (cash, cheque or savings), Officer and Voucher (click it to open the release). The total line adds the principal, charges, payoffs and net proceeds. Voided releases are left out.

Loan Due vs. Collection

Compares, loan by loan, what fell due in a period with what was collected in it, and gives the collection efficiency.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Loan Due vs. Collection.

Filters: Dates, Product, Branch and Officer.

The columns: Loan, Member, Product and Officer (add them with Columns), Due in the period (the principal and interest of the amortizations due in the period), Principal collected, Interest collected, Total collected, Penalties collected and Collection efficiency. Collections are the payments and the payoffs by renewal in the period. Only loans with something due or collected in the period are listed.

The collection efficiency of a loan is the principal and interest collected in the period, counted up to what fell due, divided by what fell due. Advance and past-due collections count in the period they were received. On the total line it is the total collected divided by the total due.

Loans Due for Collection

The collector's list: the unpaid amortizations of the open loans that fall due in a period, with the member's mobile number.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Loans Due for Collection.

Filters: Dates (this week at first), Product, Branch, Officer, and Include: Due in the period only, or Arrears from before the period to add the older unpaid amortizations.

The columns: Due, Loan, Member, Mobile, # (the amortization number), Principal, Interest, Amount due (what is still unpaid of the amortization) and Days late (days past the due date as of today, blank when not yet due). Penalties are not included: they are added at the window. Click a loan to open Loan Collection for it.

Loan Collection List

Lists every loan payment received in a period, split into penalty, interest and principal.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Loan Collection List.

Filters: Dates, Teller, Product and Branch.

The columns: Date, OR / voucher (the receipt number, or the voucher number; click it to open it), Loan (click it to open the loan ledger), Member, Product (add it with Columns), Penalty, Interest, Principal, Total, a column that says "Renewal payoff" when the old loan was paid by a renewal or restructuring, and Teller. The total line adds the amounts.

Loans With No Payments

Lists the loans whose first amortization has fallen due without any payment. A loan that is never paid from the start is an early sign of a bad loan.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Loans With No Payments.

Filters: As of, Product, Branch and Officer.

The columns: Loan, Member, Mobile, Product, Released, First due, Days since first due, Principal, Balance and Officer. A loan appears when its first due date is before the As of date and no payment was recorded on it by that date.

Loan Statement of Account

The statement of one loan: every entry on its ledger, what is due, and the amount to pay it off. Give it to a member who asks for the balance of a loan.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Loan Statement of Account.

Filters: As of, and Borrower or loan no.: click the box and the borrowers with outstanding loans (current, past due or restructured) are listed by name, each with the loan no., the loan product and the principal balance. Each letter typed narrows the list: by the borrower's name (a word of the name that starts with what you type, so "ra" finds Ramos, Danilo), by the loan no. (any part of it) or by the loan product (typing "regular" lists every borrower with a Regular Loan). Every word typed must match. Choose a loan with a click, or with the arrow keys and Enter, and the statement opens at once. Until a loan is chosen the report says how to choose it. A loan of another branch than yours is not listed, and opening one gives "That loan belongs to another branch."

Typed and sent with Refresh without choosing: a loan no. opens that loan; a name or a product that finds one outstanding loan opens it; one that finds several lists them (Loan no., Borrower, Member no., Loan product, Status, Principal balance), each loan no. opening its statement; one that finds none says "No loan no. is ... and no borrower with an outstanding loan matches it". A loan that is fully paid is not listed, but its statement still opens by its loan no.

How to read it: the note above gives the loan number, product, member, release date, principal and rate. The lines are the ledger entries up to the As of date (release, payments, penalties, adjustments, waivers and the like; interest accruals are left out), with Date, Particulars (the kind of entry and its memo), Reference (the receipt or voucher number; click it to open it), Principal, Interest, Penalty and Principal balance. Charges are positive, payments negative. For an open loan two lines follow: "Due as of" the date, with the number of amortizations and the principal, interest and penalty due, and "Amount to pay off the loan:" with the payoff on that date.

Aging of Loans by Maturity

Ages the loan balances by maturity: a loan ages only once its maturity date has passed (see How the aging reports age a loan).

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Aging of Loans by Maturity.

Filters, columns and totals: the same as Aging of Loans at PAR. Days past due is the number of days since the maturity date.

Aging of Loans at PAR

Ages the loan balances as portfolio at risk: one missed or short amortization puts the whole balance at risk, aged from the oldest overdue due date (see How the aging reports age a loan).

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Aging of Loans at PAR.

Filters: As of, Product, Branch, Officer, and Show: Every loan or Delinquent loans only.

The columns: Loan (click it to open the loan), Member, Released (add it with Columns), Maturity, Balance, Days past due (since the oldest overdue due date) and the six buckets Non-delinquent, 1 to 30 days, 1 to 3 months, 3 to 6 months, 6 to 12 months and Over 12 months.

How to read it: the loans are grouped by product, each product with its total, then the TOTAL line. The last line, Delinquent, % of the portfolio, is the total balance outside Non-delinquent as a percentage of the total balance. The bar chart By age above the table shows the total of each bucket. In the sample, ₱43,949.16 of ₱275,538.36 is delinquent: 15.95%.

Tip

Post the penalties and refresh the statuses on Post Loan Penalties & Interest Accrual before printing the month-end aging, so the loan accounts and statements agree with it.

Aging of Loans by Amortization

Ages only what is actually late: the unpaid principal of each overdue amortization, each by its own due date. The rest of each balance is non-delinquent (see How the aging reports age a loan).

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Aging of Loans by Amortization.

Filters, columns and totals: the same as Aging of Loans at PAR. Days past due is counted from the oldest overdue due date. A loan can have amounts in several buckets.

Loan Portfolio Analysis

Sums up the loan portfolio on a date by product, by branch and by loan officer, with the portfolio at risk of each.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Loan Portfolio Analysis.

Filters: As of and Branch.

The columns:

Column What it shows
Loans, Outstanding The number of loans with a balance and their principal balance.
Share The outstanding balance as a percentage of the whole portfolio.
Average loan Outstanding divided by the number of loans.
Loans at risk, Portfolio at risk The loans with at least one overdue amortization, and their whole balance (as at PAR).
PAR Portfolio at risk as a percentage of the outstanding balance.
Amount in arrears The unpaid principal and interest of the overdue amortizations of those loans.
Restructured The balance of loans that are restructured or that were released to pay off an earlier loan.

The rows come in three groups, By product, By branch and By loan officer ("No branch" and "No officer" for loans without one), and end with TOTAL PORTFOLIO.

Co-maker / Guarantor Exposure

Lists, for each co-maker, the open loans they guarantee. A co-maker who guarantees many loans, or loans in arrears, is a concentrated risk.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Co-maker / Guarantor Exposure.

Filters: Search (part of the co-maker's name, or the exact member number) and Branch.

How to read it: each co-maker is a heading with the member number, or "(non-member)". Under it are the loans: the loan number (click it to open the loan), Borrower, Status, Guaranteed and Loan balance, then "Exposure of" the co-maker with the totals.

Collateral Register

Lists the collateral of the released loans, with the expiry of the documents and of the insurance.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Collateral Register.

Filters: Search (part of the member's name, the exact loan number, or part of the reference number), Branch, Collateral (All, Held or Released) and Show (Everything, or Expiring within 60 days or expired, by the document expiry or the insurance expiry).

The columns: Loan (click it to open the loan's co-makers and collateral), Member, Type, Description, TCT / OR-CR / ref., Market value, Loan value, Loan balance (the principal balance today), Document expiry, Insurance expiry and Status (Held, or Released with the date). The total line adds the market value, loan value and loan balance.

Schedule of Loans vs GL

Checks that the loan ledgers agree with the general ledger: the outstanding principal of the loans against each loans receivable account.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Schedule of Loans vs GL.

Filter: As of.

How to read it: each loans receivable account used by the loans is a heading. Under it each product shows its principal balance from the loan ledgers, and the line "Schedule of loans / general ledger" gives Loan ledgers, General ledger and Difference; click the words of that line to open the General Ledger of the account. When the As of date is today or later, a last line compares the accrued interest of the open loans with Interest Receivable on Loans.

Tip

A difference means something reached the loans receivable account in the general ledger without going through a loan, for example a journal entry made straight to the account. Open the General Ledger from the line to find it.

Waivers and Adjustments

Every change to a member's loan balance outside a payment, for review with the approvals.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Waivers and Adjustments.

Filters: Dates, Branch, and Show: Both, Waivers or Adjustments.

The columns: Date, Kind (Waiver with its status in brackets, Adjustment, or Adjustment (voided)), Loan (click it to open the waivers or adjustments of the loan), Member, Principal, Interest, Penalty, Reason, Requested / made by and Approved by (who approved or rejected a waiver). Decreases are negative and increases positive. Waivers waiting for approval and rejected waivers are listed too.

Written-off Loans and Recoveries

Lists the loans written off, what was dropped, what has been recovered since, and the net loss.

Where to find it: Loans > Reports > Written-off Loans and Recoveries.

Filters: Dates (all dates at first) and Branch.

The columns: Written off (the date), Loan (click it to record a recovery), Member, Product, Principal written off, Interest dropped, Penalty dropped, Recovered, Net loss (principal written off less recovered), Resolution / reason and Voucher (click it to open the write-off voucher). The total line adds the amounts.

Teller Shift & Cash Count

Every teller works from a cash drawer. At the start of the day the teller opens a shift on this page with the beginning cash in the drawer. Every cash receipt and cash payment the teller posts while the shift is open counts to it. At the end of the day the teller counts the cash, by denomination or as one total, and closes the shift. A difference between the cash counted and the cash expected is posted to Cash Over and Short at once, so the ledger agrees with the cash in the drawer.

The same page shows a closed shift with its count, prints its cash count sheet, and lists the recent shifts.

Where to find it: Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count. The Teller shift button on Official Receipt and the Teller Shift & Cash Count link in the warning shown on the receipt and disbursement pages when your shift is not open also open it.

The title bar has Cash count sheet (shown on a closed shift), Official receipt and Disbursement.

Fields: Open your shift

Shown when you have no open shift.

Field What to enter / what it means
Business date The day the shift belongs to. Today by default. A cash over/short entry is dated on it.
Beginning cash in the drawer The cash in the drawer when the shift starts (the change fund). 0.00 by default.
Cash account (drawer) The cash account of the drawer, for example 1009 Cash on Hand - Accounting Office. It shows the Teller cash account of your user account (User Accounts), or the Teller Cash on Hand default of Module Default Accounts when none is set. Only a user with the Approve right on this page can change it; other tellers always open on their own account.

Under the fields the page reminds you: "Every cash receipt and payment you post goes to this drawer. At the end of the day count the cash by denomination; a difference is posted to Cash Over and Short with a supervisor's approval." When a tolerance is set, it says above which amount a supervisor is needed instead.

The shift

The card Shift (number and teller) shows the status, the Business date, the Drawer, when it was Opened and Closed, and the Branch, then these figures:

Figure What it means
Beginning cash The cash typed when the shift was opened.
Cash received The receipts paid in cash on this shift: official receipts, and the cash deposits, loan payments and time deposit placements posted on Savings Account Transaction, Loan Collection and Time Deposit Center. Voided receipts drop out.
Cash paid out The cash payments of the shift: member disbursements, cash withdrawals, loans released in cash and time deposits paid out in cash. Voided ones drop out.
Expected in the drawer Beginning cash + cash received − cash paid out.
Counted Shown on a closed shift: the cash counted, with "balanced", "over" or "short" and the difference under it.

Under the figures, a table gives the Kind (Official receipt, Disbursement, Savings deposit / receipt or Savings withdrawal / payment: the last two are the transactions of the Savings Transaction Center), Paid by, Count and Amount of the documents of the shift. "Only cash counts toward the drawer; checks and bank payments are listed for the deposit slip."

While the shift is open, the box Cash count by denomination is under the card:

Field What to enter / what it means
Pieces The number of bills or coins of each denomination, from PHP 1,000 bill down to 1 centavos. The Amount of the line fills in.
Counted, Expected, Difference The total counted, the cash expected, and Balanced, Over or Short with the amount. They change as you type.
Or type the total counted The total, when you do not count by piece. A total typed here is the cash counted, whatever the pieces add up to.
Notes A remark on the count. It shows on the closed shift and on the cash count sheet.
Supervisor approval Needed when there is a difference and you cannot approve on this page (see below).

A closed shift shows the box Cash count instead: the denominations counted, the notes and Over/short voucher with the voucher number.

Step by step

To open a shift:

  1. Open Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count. The box Open your shift is shown when you have no open shift.
  2. Check the Business date.
  3. Count the cash in the drawer and type it in Beginning cash in the drawer.
  4. Check the Cash account (drawer).
  5. Click Open shift. The message "Shift 3 is open." appears and the shift is shown.

To close a shift:

  1. Open Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count. Your open shift is shown with the box Cash count by denomination.
  2. Count the cash. Type the Pieces of each denomination, or type the total in Or type the total counted.
  3. Compare Counted with Expected. Count again if Difference is not Balanced.
  4. Type Notes if needed.
  5. If a difference remains and it needs approval, a supervisor approves in Supervisor approval.
  6. Click Close the shift and answer "Close the shift with the cash counted? A difference is posted to Cash Over and Short." with Close shift.
  7. The message gives the result, for example "Shift closed. Expected 10,000.00, counted 9,961.00, short 39.00 posted to Cash Over and Short." or "..., balanced." The closed shift is shown.
  8. Click Cash count sheet, print it, sign it and hand it over with the cash.
Example

Shift 2 opened with ₱5,000.00 and received one cash official receipt of ₱5,000.00, so ₱10,000.00 was expected. The teller counted 9 PHP 1,000 bills, 1 PHP 500 bill, 4 PHP 100 bills, 3 PHP 20 bills and 4 pieces of 25 centavos: ₱9,961.00. The shortage of ₱39.00 was posted as CJV-000008: debit Cash Over and Short ₱39.00, credit 1009 Cash on Hand - Accounting Office ₱39.00.

To look at an earlier shift: click its number in the list on the right.

The cash count sheet

Cash count sheet appears in the title bar when the shift shown is closed and you have the Print/Export right. It opens the sheet and the print window. The sheet prints as CASH COUNT SHEET with the cooperative's heading, the shift number, the business date, the Teller, the Drawer and the Branch, and:

  • the count: Denomination, Pieces and Amount of each denomination counted ("Counted as a total, not by piece." when a total was typed), and Cash counted;
  • the reconciliation: Beginning cash, Add: cash received, Less: cash paid out, Expected in the drawer, Cash counted, and Over, Short or Difference;
  • the Documents of the shift by Paid by, with Count and Amount;
  • when the shift was opened and closed, the over/short voucher number and the notes;
  • the signature lines Counted by (teller), Verified by (the user who closed the shift, when it was not the teller) and Received by (treasurer) (the Treasurer named on Cooperative Profile & Policies).

Back to the shift returns to the page. Each print is recorded in the audit trail. A teller prints the sheets of their own shifts; a user with the Approve right prints any teller's.

The list of shifts

The card on the right lists the latest 25 shifts, open ones first: Shift (with "open" beside an open one), Teller, Date, Expected and Over / short (a shortage in brackets). A teller sees Your shifts; a user with the Approve right on this page sees Shifts, the shifts of every teller, and can open, close and print any of them.

What it records

Opening a shift posts nothing. Closing a balanced shift posts nothing. Closing with a difference posts a Cooperative Journal Voucher dated on the business date, with the memo "Teller shift 2 cash short 10/11/2026" (or "over"):

Account Debit Credit
Cash over: the drawer's cash account Difference
Cash over: Cash Over and Short Difference
Cash short: Cash Over and Short Difference
Cash short: the drawer's cash account Difference

Cash Over and Short is the account set for it on Module Default Accounts. The voucher opens from the closed shift and is listed on Cooperative Vouchers and in the Teller Over / Short report.

Good to know

  • With the policy Cash transactions need an open teller shift on Cooperative Profile & Policies (on when installed), receipts and disbursements are refused without an open shift: "Open your teller shift first (Teller > Teller Shift & Cash Count)."
  • A difference needs a supervisor when the user closing the shift has no Approve right on this page and the difference is above Cash over/short a teller may post without approval (Cooperative Profile & Policies; zero when installed, so every difference needs one): "Supervisor approval is required: cash over/short of ... on closing the shift." See Supervisor approval.
  • "You already have an open shift. Close it before opening another."
  • "The drawer must be an active cash account (an account of type Bank in the Chart of Accounts)."
  • One drawer has one open shift: "Cash account 1009 - Cash on Hand - Accounting Office is in use by the open shift of ..."
  • "You may only open a shift on your assigned cash account." A teller with a Teller cash account on their user account opens on it.
  • "No cash account is assigned to you (System Administration > User Accounts > Cooperative cash account)." Set the Teller cash account on User Accounts, or the Teller Cash on Hand default on Module Default Accounts.
  • "Only the teller or a supervisor can close this shift."
  • "Denomination counts cannot be negative."
  • A shift left open from an earlier day shows in the alerts: "Your teller shift from an earlier day is still open", and for users with the Approve right "Teller shifts of other users left open from an earlier day".
  • Once the shift is closed, its receipts can be voided only by a user who may approve on this page (see Official Receipt).
  • The Create right opens a shift. The Approve right lets a user choose the drawer, see and close other tellers' shifts, print their sheets, and post a difference without a supervisor.

Official Receipt (Member Collection)

The official receipt records money a member pays the cooperative other than savings and loan payments: the membership fee, fees, charges and other items, in any mix, on one receipt with one OR number. It is posted at once as one balanced voucher. The receipt is printed for the member and, when it was a mistake, voided with a reason.

Note

Savings deposits and share capital are received on Savings Account Transaction, each with its number of the Savings Transaction Center series; loan payments are received on Loan Collection. The official receipt no longer lists the member's accounts and loans; a note on the form links to the two pages.

The page is titled Official Receipt.

Where to find it: Teller > Official Receipt (Member Collection). The Official receipt button on Teller Shift & Cash Count and New official receipt on Official Receipt List also open it.

When your shift is open, the line above the form gives the shift number, business date and drawer, and "Received so far". When it is not and a shift is required, the warning "Your teller shift is not open. Open it on Teller Shift & Cash Count with your beginning cash before receiving money." is shown instead. The title bar has Receipts (the list of official receipts) and Teller shift.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Member Type part of the name, the member no., an account or a loan number, and choose the member from the list. Active members and applicants can pay. The member panel (photo, member no., type, status, branch, any hold or alert, and the balances of Share capital, Savings, Time deposits and Loans) appears.
Date The date of the receipt. Today by default. The box accepts earlier dates only within the days set in Days a transaction may be back-dated without the special permission (Cooperative Profile & Policies), unless you have the permission "Cooperative: can record back-dated member transactions". A receipt dated before today is marked as a back entry.
OR no. Leave blank to get the next number (the grey text shows it, for example "next: 000164"). The program puts the official receipt prefix of the member's branch in front, for example P000164 (see Branches). Type the number of a pre-printed receipt instead; it must not be used by another official receipt.

Fees and other items takes any other money received, one line each: Account, Description and Amount. The account list holds every GL account except those kept by their own ledgers: the accounts of the savings, share capital and time deposit products (and their interest payable), the loans receivable and loan interest receivable accounts of the loan products, the accounts receivable of the customers and of the water collection, and the inventory accounts of the items. Money for those is received on Savings Account Transaction, Loan Collection, Receive Payments or the water collection. Add a fee or other item adds a line. For an applicant, when a Membership fee is set on Cooperative Profile & Policies and no earlier receipt carried it, the form starts with a "Membership fee" line on the Membership Fees account for that amount.

The Payment box:

Field What to enter / what it means
Paid by Cash, Check, Bank deposit / transfer or Debit to the member's savings. The boxes below change with it.
Check no., Bank, Date, Amount For Check: one line per check; Add a check adds a line. The checks must add up to the total. "Checks wait in the checks clearing account until they are cleared on Check Clearing."
Bank account For Bank deposit / transfer: the bank account the member paid into.
From savings account (account no.) For Debit to the member's savings: the member's own ordinary or special savings account the payment is taken from, with the amount available.
Cash received For Cash: the money handed over. Optional; when typed, Change shows what to give back.
Memo A remark added to the memo of the voucher.

Total adds the amounts of the receipt as you type.

Step by step

To receive a payment from a member:

  1. Open your shift on Teller Shift & Cash Count if it is not open.
  2. Open Teller > Official Receipt (Member Collection).
  3. Find the member in Member. Check the member panel for a hold or an alert.
  4. Check the Date. Leave OR no. blank, or type the number of the pre-printed receipt.
  5. Add each fee or other item under Fees and other items: the Account, the Description and the Amount.
  6. Choose Paid by and fill in its boxes. For cash, type Cash received to see the change.
  7. Check the Total.
  8. Click Post official receipt. The program asks, for example, "Post official receipt for 500.00 for Mendoza, Ramon (100006)? The general ledger and the member ledgers are updated at once." Click Post receipt.
  9. The message "Official receipt P000164 posted: 500.00. Change: 500.00." appears and the posted receipt opens. Click Print and give the receipt to the member.
Example

An applicant, Mendoza, Ramon, pays the membership fee of ₱300.00 (the line the form starts with) and ₱200.00 for the pre-membership seminar kit on a line added under Fees and other items, and hands over ₱1,000.00 in cash. The total is ₱500.00 and the change ₱500.00. His share capital is then received on Savings Account Transaction.

To find a receipt: click Receipts, or use Official Receipt List.

To void a receipt:

  1. Open the receipt from the list.
  2. Click Void.
  3. Type the Reason (required, recorded in the audit trail).
  4. If you cannot approve on this page, a supervisor approves in Supervisor approval.
  5. Click Void P000163 (the button carries the number). The message "Official Receipt P000163 was voided. The member ledgers were reversed with it." appears.

"The voucher stays on file marked VOID; the general ledger and the member ledgers are reversed together." When a receipt cannot be voided, the reason is shown above it ("This voucher cannot be voided now: ...") and Void is greyed.

The posted receipt

The posted receipt shows Voucher, Date, Member, Branch / class, Reference, Memo, Payment (mode and account), Teller (with the shift), Approved by, Recorded, Status and the Total; the items of the receipt (Item, Amount) and any checks; the General ledger entry; and Member ledgers, the savings and loan entries the receipt made with the balance after each. Its buttons are Print, Void and Back.

The receipt prints as OFFICIAL RECEIPT with the cooperative's name, address, CDA registration number and TIN, the number and the date; Received from and the member no.; the mode; the Particulars and Amount of each item with the TOTAL; the amount in words; any checks; and the signature line Received by (teller). The first print is the original. A later print asks for the Reason for the reprint and is marked "COPY 1 (reprint)", "COPY 2 (reprint)" and so on.

What it records

One Official Receipt voucher numbered with the OR number, dated on the receipt date:

Account Debit Credit
Cash: the cash account of your shift's drawer. Check: the account of transaction code CK4 (or Checks and Other Cash Items). Bank: the bank account chosen. Savings: the ledger account of the member's savings product. Total
Each fee or other item: the account of the line Amount

The lines carry the member's name and the class of the member's branch. A payment from savings debits the savings account with code LPY ("Payment per OR ..."). The receipt is listed in the teller reports and, when paid in cash, counts to the shift's cash received.

Good to know

  • "Enter at least one item with an amount."
  • "Member Mendoza, Ramon is on hold: ..." Only active members and applicants pay fees and other items here.
  • A fee paid by check waits in the checks clearing account until it is cleared in Checks received for loan payments and fees on Check Clearing; a returned check is handled by voiding the receipt with the reason.
  • "Choose the bank account the payment went into." Paid by Bank deposit / transfer needs the bank account.
  • "The cheques (...) must add up to the receipt total (...)." and "Enter the cheque number."
  • "Cash received (...) is less than the total (...)."
  • "Payments by savings transfer come from the member's own account."
  • "Official receipt number P000164 is already used." Leave the number blank, or type the right pre-printed number.
  • A receipt above A receipt above this needs supervisor approval (Cooperative Profile & Policies; zero = no limit) needs a supervisor unless you may approve on this page: "Supervisor approval is required: receipt above the large transaction threshold (...)."
  • "The transaction date cannot be in the future.", "The books are closed through ..." (see The closing date) and "Back-dated entries beyond 0 day(s) need the "can record back-dated member transactions" permission."
  • "This form was already submitted, or the page is out of date. The entry was not saved a second time; refresh the page and check the list before entering it again." A receipt is never posted twice by clicking twice.
  • A receipt of a closed teller shift can be voided only by a user with the Approve right on Teller Shift & Cash Count: "The teller shift of this receipt is already closed. Only a user who may approve on Teller Shift & Cash Count can void it (or reverse it with an adjusting voucher)." A receipt whose void would overdraw a savings account cannot be voided until the later withdrawals are voided.
  • The Create right posts receipts, Print/Export prints them, Delete/Void voids them (with a supervisor unless the user also has Approve).
  • Savings deposits, share capital and loan payments are not taken on the official receipt: they are received on Savings Account Transaction and Loan Collection.

Member Disbursement (Cash Voucher)

The member disbursement pays money out to a member: withdrawals from savings and share capital, interest on share capital and patronage refund kept as payable, and other amounts the cooperative owes the member. It is paid in cash from the drawer or by cheque, posted at once as one voucher, and printed for the member to sign.

The page is titled Member Disbursement.

Where to find it: no longer in the menu (version 39: the cooperative asked for it to be taken out, as not needed); savings are withdrawn on Savings Account Transaction. The page opens from Settle the accounts on the page of a member who has resigned, been terminated or died and still has a balance, and from a member's name on the Unclaimed Distributions report. The disbursements already made stay on Official Receipt List.

The title bar has Disbursements, the list of member disbursements. When a shift is required and yours is not open, the warning "Your teller shift is not open. Open it on Teller Shift & Cash Count first." is shown.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Member Type part of the name, the member no., an account or a loan number, and choose the member. The page opens again for that member with the member panel and the accounts.
Date The date of the payment. Today by default. The back-dating rules of the official receipt apply.
Voucher no. Leave blank to get the next number (the grey text shows it, for example "next: MCV-000001"), or type one.
Withdrawals: Account, Available, Withdraw Each account of the member whose product allows withdrawals (time deposits are left out), with its account number and balance, and the amount Available. Type the amount to pay out in Withdraw. "No account of this member allows withdrawals." when there is none.
Closing withdrawal (the minimum balance may be taken out) Tick it when the member takes everything out, so the minimum balance of the product does not stop the withdrawal.
Interest on share capital and patronage refund to pay Shown when the member has distribution amounts kept as payable and not yet paid. Tick each one to pay (the batch number, the year and the amount).
Other payables to the member Other amounts owed to the member, one line each: Account (Other Current Liability and Accounts Payable accounts), Description and Amount. Add a payable adds a line.
Paid by Cash from the drawer or Cheque.
Bank account, Cheque no. For Cheque: the bank account the cheque is drawn on and its number.
Received by (when not the member) The person who receives the money for the member, for example the beneficiary or the estate of a deceased member (required then). It prints on the voucher and its Received by signature line.
Memo A remark added to the memo of the voucher.

Total to pay adds the amounts as you type.

Members who have left

For a member who has resigned, been terminated or died, the page says so at the top: the payment is the settlement of the member's accounts. The share capital and the deposits are paid out in full (the minimum balance and the lock-in period do not apply) and the payment always needs a supervisor's approval ("Supervisor approval is required: settlement of a resigned member ..."). Apply the deposits to the member's open loans first on Loan Collection (paid by the member's savings). A deceased member's claim is paid to the beneficiary or the estate: type the name in Received by, or the page refuses with "Enter who receives the payment for the deceased member (the beneficiary or the estate)."

Step by step

To pay a member:

  1. Open your shift if it is not open.
  2. Open the member's page and click Settle the accounts (or click the member on Unclaimed Distributions).
  3. Find the member in Member.
  4. Type the Withdraw amount on each account, tick the distribution amounts to pay, and add other payables.
  5. Choose Paid by. For a cheque, choose the Bank account and type the Cheque no.
  6. Check Total to pay.
  7. If the payment needs approval (see below), a supervisor approves in Supervisor approval.
  8. Click Post disbursement. The program asks "Pay out 1,000.00 to Mendoza, Ramon (100006)? The withdrawal is posted to the general ledger and the member ledgers at once." Click Post disbursement.
  9. The message "Disbursement MCV-000001 posted: 1,000.00." appears and the voucher opens. Click Print, count the cash out to the member and have the member sign.

The posted voucher, its Print, Void and Back buttons, and the void work as on the posted official receipt. It prints as DISBURSEMENT VOUCHER with Paid to / member, the particulars, the amount in words, the cheque, the ledger lines (Account, Name, Memo, Debit, Credit) and the signature lines Prepared by, Checked by (the Bookkeeper / Accountant of Cooperative Profile & Policies), Approved by (the approver, or the General Manager) and Received by (the member).

What it records

One Member Disbursement voucher numbered with the voucher number:

Account Debit Credit
Each withdrawal: the ledger account of the savings or share capital product Amount
Each distribution paid: Interest on Share Capital Payable or Patronage Refund Payable Amount
Each other payable: the account of the line Amount
Cash: the cash account of your shift's drawer. Cheque: the bank account chosen. Total

The debit lines carry the member's name. Each withdrawal is entered in the member's account with code CSW ("Withdrawal"), and each distribution line paid is marked paid with the voucher, so it leaves the Unclaimed Distributions report. A cash payment counts to the shift's cash paid out; a cheque does not.

Good to know

  • "Withdrawals above your limit, from dormant accounts, from products that need approval and share capital of an active member need a supervisor. So does every payment to a member who has resigned, been terminated or died ..." The limit is the role's "Cooperative: max savings withdrawal without supervisor approval" (User Role Configuration); A withdrawal above this needs supervisor approval on Cooperative Profile & Policies applies on top of it. A user with the Approve right on this page approves their own.
  • "Insufficient available balance on 02100006011: available ...", with the amount still clearing, on hold, or the minimum balance that must stay. Tick Closing withdrawal to take the minimum balance out too.
  • "Withdrawals are not allowed on ...", "The minimum withdrawal for ... is ...", "Account ... is within its lock-in period until ...", and "Account ... is restricted: ..."
  • "A distribution line is not payable any more. Refresh the page."
  • "Enter at least one item with an amount."
  • "Choose the bank account the cheque is drawn on." A cheque payment needs its bank account.

Official Receipt List

The list of the official receipts and member disbursements of a period, voided ones included. Click a number to open the receipt or voucher, where it can be printed or voided.

Where to find it: Teller > Official Receipt List. Receipts on Official Receipt opens the same list for receipts only. The list also holds the member disbursements made before version 39.

To find a receipt: type the From and To dates (the first of the month to today by default), type part of the number, the member's name, the member no. or the memo in Find, and click Show.

The columns are Date, No. (the OR or voucher number), Member (with the member no.), Memo, Mode (Cash, Check, Bank or Savings), Amount and Status (Posted or Void; a void line is greyed). The footer counts the vouchers and totals the amounts: "4 voucher(s), voids excluded from the total". Up to 500 lines are shown; a user limited to a branch sees that branch only. New official receipt and New disbursement start a new one.

Teller reports

The Teller reports are listed under Teller > Reports in the sidebar and in the Teller card of the Report Center. The common report tools (Refresh, Print / PDF, Export to spreadsheet, Email link, Modify Report, Memorize and clicking a number to open the transaction) are explained in Working with reports.

Teller Transaction Journal

Every official receipt and member disbursement of a period, one line each, in the order they were made. Use it to go over a teller's day.

Where to find it: Teller > Reports > Teller Transaction Journal.

Filters: Dates (Today by default), From, To, Teller, Kind (Official receipts or Disbursements) and Branch.

The columns: Date, Time, Teller, Receipt / voucher (click it to open the voucher), Kind (OR or CV), Member, Mode, Received, Paid out and Status ("VOID", or "Back entry" for a back-dated receipt). Voided receipts stay on the list marked VOID and are left out of the totals. The report also offers the columns Drawer and Shift, hidden at first; see Modifying a report.

Daily Collection & Disbursement Summary

Every transaction of the teller by day, with the money collected and paid out by payment mode and the net cash: the Loan Collection payslips and their error corrections, every transaction of the Savings Transaction Center (cash and check slips, memo codes, transfers and GL-opposite codes, with their error corrections), the official receipts of other items, the member disbursements and the loans released from the drawer. Voided transactions are left out.

Where to find it: Teller > Reports > Daily Collection & Disbursement Summary.

Filters: Dates (This week by default), From, To, Teller, Show (By transaction source, or One line per day and teller) and Branch.

The columns: Date, Teller, Transactions (the source), Documents, Collected, cash, Collected, checks, Collected, bank / savings / memo, Paid out, cash, Paid out, cheque / other and Net cash (cash collected less cash paid out). By transaction source each teller's day has one line per source, then a subtotal "Total teller1, 10/20/2026"; the grand total adds up the sources.

Transactions What it counts
Loan Collection The loan payments of the official receipts (Teller > Loan Collection), by cash, check, or bank and savings.
Loan Collection: error corrections An error-corrected payslip, negative in the column of the payment it negates: the cash paid back from the drawer, or the check or bank payment taken back by its journal voucher.
Savings Transaction Center: deposits, withdrawals Every transaction of the center: cash and check slips in the cash and check columns, memo codes, transfers and GL-opposite codes in the bank / savings / memo and cheque / other columns.
Savings Transaction Center: error corrections A corrected deposit taken back from what was collected, a corrected withdrawal from what was paid out.
Official receipts: savings and deposits, fees and other items The other lines of the official receipts (Teller > Official Receipt).
Loan releases from the drawer, Member disbursements The cash vouchers paid from the teller's drawer.

Teller Over / Short

The closed teller shifts of a period with their cash count and the over or short found.

Where to find it: Teller > Reports > Teller Over / Short.

Filters: Dates, From, To (the business date of the shift), Teller, and Show: All closed shifts or Only shifts with a difference.

The columns: Business date, Teller, Shift, Drawer, Beginning cash, Cash received, Cash paid out, Expected, Counted, Over, Short, Closed by and Voucher (the over/short voucher; click it to open it), with the totals of the cash received and paid out, the overs and the shorts.

Official Receipt Register

The official receipts (or the disbursement vouchers) of a period in number order, with the numbers that were skipped. Use it to account for every pre-printed receipt.

Where to find it: Teller > Reports > Official Receipt Register.

Filters: Dates, From, To, Branch and Series (Official receipts or Disbursement vouchers).

How to read it: the numbers are sorted within each series; each branch prefix, such as P, is a series of its own. Where numbers are missing, an amber line gives them, for example "P000164 to P000165" and "2 number(s) not used in this period". The columns are Number (click it to open the receipt), Date, Member, Mode, Amount (blank for a voided receipt), Status ("VOID:" and the reason), Teller and Prints (how many times it was printed). The note under the title gives the number of missing numbers, or "The series has no gap in this period."

Tip

A gap is not always an error: the number may have been used just before or after the period, or the receipt was spoiled. Account for each one and keep the spoiled receipts.

Cash Position

The cash in the teller drawers and the bank balances on a date, against the savings members can withdraw: a quick liquidity check.

Where to find it: Teller > Reports > Cash Position.

Filter: As of.

How to read it: Teller cash and cash items lists the drawer accounts (those used by shifts or set on user accounts, the Teller Cash on Hand default) and Checks and Other Cash Items, with their general ledger balance and "open shift 3 (admin)" beside a drawer in use; click an account for its general ledger. Banks lists the other bank and cash accounts. TOTAL CASH POSITION adds them. The last line, Deposits that can be withdrawn on demand (savings, net of holds), is the ordinary and special savings of active and dormant accounts less holds and uncleared checks, at their current balance.

The Balance column is shown only to users with the permission "View bank balances outside Banking".

Net Surplus Allocation
