After the fiscal year, the net surplus is divided as the cooperative's bylaws and the General Assembly decide: first the statutory funds (reserve fund, education and training fund, community development fund, optional fund), then interest on share capital (ISC) and patronage refund (PR). This page records that division for each fiscal year, has it approved with the resolution, and posts it once. The ISC and PR amounts it sets aside are then shared among the members with Interest on Share Capital and Patronage Refund batches.

Where to find it: Year-end Distribution > Net Surplus Allocation. The Net surplus allocation button on Distribution Batches also opens it, and each year on the Net Surplus Allocation Summary opens its allocation.

Fiscal years on the left lists each allocation with its net surplus and status (Draft, Approved or Posted); click one to open it. With no year chosen, the page shows the form for a new allocation. New year in the title bar starts one; Edit opens a draft.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Fiscal year The year being divided. Last year by default. One allocation per fiscal year.
From, To The fiscal period, worked out from Fiscal year ends (MM-DD) on Cooperative Profile & Policies.
Net surplus The net surplus to divide. A new allocation starts with the figure in the ledger. The line under it reads "Net surplus in the ledger for the period: ... (income less expenses). Use the audited figure when it differs."
Reserve fund %, Education fund %, Community development %, Optional fund % The share of the net surplus for each statutory fund. They start from the percentages on Cooperative Profile & Policies (10, 10, 3 and 7 when installed). The help on the policies page gives the usual limits: reserve fund at least 10 % (RA 9520 Art. 86), education fund not more than 10 %, community development fund at least 3 %, optional fund not more than 7 %. Confirm the percentages against your bylaws and the General Assembly resolution.
of which to the federation % The part of the education and training fund due to the union or federation (CETF). 50 when installed.
Interest on share capital The amount set aside for interest on share capital. "The patronage refund is what remains after the statutory funds and the interest on share capital."
Notes Any remark. A reversal adds its date and reason here.

The Allocation table shows the division: Net surplus; each fund with its percentage and amount, and the education fund split into "retained by the cooperative / due to the federation"; Statutory funds; Interest on share capital; Patronage refund. While you edit, it is worked out from the figures the page was opened with; save the draft to see new figures.

A saved allocation also shows Period, Resolution, Approving officers, Approved and Allocation voucher, and Member batches: the ISC and PR batches of the year with their amount and status, ISC used / set aside and PR used / set aside (batches approved or posted), and the buttons ISC batch and PR batch to start one for the year.

Step by step

To prepare the allocation:

  1. Open Year-end Distribution > Net Surplus Allocation.
  2. Check the Fiscal year, From and To.
  3. Type the Net surplus of the audited financial statements if it differs from the ledger.
  4. Check the percentages and type the Interest on share capital approved.
  5. Click Save the draft. The message "Allocation saved as a draft. The General Assembly approves it before it is posted." appears.

To approve it (users with the Approve right):

  1. Open the year. In Approve (General Assembly resolution), type the Resolution no., its Date and the Approving officers.
  2. Click Approve and confirm. The message "Allocation approved and locked." appears. An approved allocation can no longer be edited.

To post it (users with the Create right), after the end of the fiscal year:

  1. In Post the allocation, type the Posting date. It must be after the last day of the fiscal year.
  2. Click Post and confirm "Post the allocation? Retained earnings are debited and the funds and payables credited, once."
  3. The message "Allocation posted (voucher CJV-000003). The ISC and PR batches of the year can now be computed." appears.

To reverse a posted allocation (users with the Approve right): in Reverse, type the Date and the Reason, click Reverse the allocation and confirm. "Allowed only while no ISC or PR batch of the year is posted. The original voucher stays; an adjusting entry reverses it." The allocation goes back to approved and can be posted again.

What it records

Posting makes one Cooperative Journal Voucher, memo "Allocation of net surplus 2025 (resolution GA-2026-03)", with the resolution number as reference:

Account Debit Credit
Retained Earnings (where the year's net surplus is once the year has ended) Net surplus
Reserve Fund Reserve fund amount
Education and Training Fund Part retained
Due to Union / Federation (CETF) Part due to the federation
Community Development Fund Amount
Optional Fund Amount
Interest on Share Capital Payable ISC set aside
Patronage Refund Payable PR set aside

The accounts are those set on Module Default Accounts. A reversal is a Cooperative Journal Voucher "Reversal of ..." that turns every line around.

Example

The 2025 net surplus of ₱200,000.00 gives the reserve fund ₱20,000.00 (10 %), the education and training fund ₱20,000.00 (10 %, of which ₱10,000.00 is due to the federation), the community development fund ₱6,000.00 (3 %) and the optional fund ₱14,000.00 (7 %): ₱60,000.00 of statutory funds. With ₱60,000.00 of interest on share capital, the patronage refund is ₱80,000.00.

Good to know

  • "There is no net surplus to allocate (...)."
  • "Interest on share capital (...) cannot exceed the net surplus left after the statutory funds (...)." The Allocation table warns: "The interest on share capital is more than what is left after the statutory funds."
  • "An allocation for fiscal year 2025 already exists." Open that year instead.
  • "Enter the General Assembly / Board resolution number and date."
  • "Post the allocation after the end of the fiscal year (12/31/2025), when its net surplus is in Retained Earnings."
  • "ISC or PR batches of this year are posted. Reverse them first."
  • The allocation voucher cannot be voided: "It posted the net surplus allocation of a fiscal year. Reverse it with an adjusting entry."

Interest on Share Capital and Patronage Refund

A distribution batch shares an amount among the members. An interest on share capital (ISC) batch shares it by each member's share capital, on the average daily balance or the ending balance of the period. A patronage refund (PR) batch shares it by the income the cooperative earned from each member. The program computes the batch for your review: computing posts nothing and asks nothing. You see the list of the members with their amounts and what each one owes the cooperative, remove the members who should not receive it (for example a member with a past-due loan or another obligation to the cooperative) with the reason, or adjust an amount, and finish the review; another user approves it with the resolution; then it is posted once, crediting each member's savings or share capital, or keeping the amount as payable to be paid out later.

Both menu items open the same page, for its own kind of batch. What is specific to a patronage refund is summed up under Patronage Refund.

Where to find it: Year-end Distribution > Interest on Share Capital, or Year-end Distribution > Patronage Refund; each opens a new batch. ISC batch and PR batch on Net Surplus Allocation start a batch for that year, and New ISC batch and New PR batch on Distribution Batches start one too. Click a batch number on Distribution Batches or Net Surplus Allocation to open it.

Fields: Computation

Field What to enter / what it means
Fiscal year allocation The approved or posted net surplus allocation the batch draws on, for example "Fiscal year 2025 (ISC 60,000.00)". The line under it gives the amount set aside and what approved or posted batches already used. None (not limited by an allocation) leaves the batch without that limit.
Description For example "Interest on Share Capital 2025". It shows on the batch lists.
Balances from, to (ISC); Income earned from, to (PR) The period measured. The fiscal period of the allocation by default.
Basis (ISC only) Average daily balance or Ending balance of share capital. It starts from Interest on share capital basis on Cooperative Profile & Policies.
Method Share an amount in proportion: the amount is shared by each member's basis over the total basis. Apply a rate: each member gets the rate times their basis.
Amount to distribute For Share an amount in proportion. A new batch starts with what the allocation set aside less what other batches used. The rounding difference goes to the member with the largest basis, so the amount is distributed exactly.
Rate % For Apply a rate: the dividend (ISC) or refund (PR) rate, in percent.
Credit to Credit to a savings account; Credit to share capital; Share capital until the subscription is fully paid, the rest to savings; or Keep as payable (paid out in cash / cheque later).
Savings product For the savings and share-capital-then-savings choices: the savings product whose account is credited.
Memo A remark on the batch.

Fields: Members covered

Active and suspended members are covered. These filters narrow the list:

Field What to enter / what it means
Branch, Cluster, Segment Only the members of one branch, cluster or segment. All by default.
Share capital product (ISC only) The share capital product measured. Every share capital product by default.
Member types Choose one or more types. "None selected: every type."
Income accounts (patronage) (PR only) See Patronage Refund.
Basis from, to Only the members whose basis (balance or income) is within this range. Leave blank for all.
Leave out members with a past-due loan Ticked, a member with a past-due loan is put on the list already removed, with the reason "Past due loan" (Put back brings the member back). Unticked, such a member is on the list and stands out in red during the review.

The batch: the computation for review

The steps at the top show where the batch is: Review the list, Reviewed, Approved, Posted (and Reversed). While the batch is not approved, the note under them says "Nothing is posted yet", with the number of members and the amount, and has Finish the review. The tiles give the Members on the list (and how many were removed), the total basis (Average daily balances, Ending balances or Income earned), To distribute (with the rate, or the pool and any rounding) and how many members on the list have a past-due loan or are on hold.

The list of Members has, for each member: the name (click it to open the member) with the member no., the account credited and any reason; Basis (underlined with dashes: point at it to see where it comes from, see Where the basis comes from); Computed; Adjustment; Net ("removed" for a member removed); and Owes the cooperative:

  • Past due and the amount, in red, with the loan nos.: the amortizations of the open loans that fell due before today and are unpaid, with the penalties;
  • On hold and the reason, in red, for a member put on hold (Members);
  • Loans: the principal balance of the member's open loans;
  • Subscription unpaid: the share capital subscribed and not yet paid;
  • None when the member owes nothing of these.

A member with a past-due amount or on hold is marked with a red edge. Find a member narrows the list by name, member no. or account, and Only those with a past-due loan or on hold shows only them. The members removed stay on the list, greyed, with "Removed:" and the reason, and Put back.

The totals under the list:

  • Total, "of the 10 member(s) on the list": the Basis, Computed, Adjustment and Net of the members who receive the amount. The total Net is what is posted; with an amount shared in proportion it equals the amount to distribute.
  • Removed, "2 member(s), not given any amount": the basis of the members removed from the list (shown when there are any).
  • Shown, for example "3 of the 10 member(s) on the list": while Find a member or Only those with a past-due loan or on hold narrows the list, the totals of the members on the list that are shown.

On a phone each member is a card, and each total is a card under them.

Under the list are the box of the next step (1. Finish the review, 2. Approve or 3. Post) and the card with Batch, Description, Period, Fiscal year, Status ("In review, not posted", "Reviewed, not posted", Approved, Posted), Resolution, Approved, Voucher and Memo. The title bar has Recompute (a batch not yet approved), Print register, CSV, Delete (a batch not yet posted, see Deleting a computation) and Batches.

Where the basis comes from

Point at a member's amount in the Basis column and, after a moment, a window shows how the amount is worked out, so you can see where it comes from. Move the pointer away and it closes; move it into the window to read it or to click a document in it. Click the amount instead to keep the window open until you click elsewhere, press Esc or click its X. With the keyboard, Tab onto the amount opens it, and Enter moves into the window so that Tab reaches its documents and its X. On a phone, tap the amount: the window opens from the bottom of the screen; tap X or outside it to close it. The window is worked out from the records when it opens, with the same arithmetic as the batch.

Interest on share capital, average daily balance (the window is titled Average daily balance): the member, the share capital account and its product, and the period with its number of days; then a table of each stretch of days the balance stood the same:

  • Dates: from the first to the last day of the stretch;
  • Days: the number of days;
  • Balance: the balance on those days, with what changed it on the first day under it (a deposit in green, for example +600.00, a withdrawal in red), or "brought forward" for the balance the period started with (when money moved on the first day of the period, a first line "Before 01/01/2026" gives that balance);
  • Balance × days: the balance times the days.

The Total row adds up the days (the days of the period) and the balance-days, and the line under it divides them: "2,421,400.00 ÷ 302 days = 8,017.88", the Average daily balance. An entry counts from its value date when it has one. Voided entries do not count.

Interest on share capital, ending balance (titled Ending balance): the balance brought forward on the first day of the period, each day the balance changed with what came in or went out and the balance after it, and "the balance at the end of 10/29/2026 = 8,600.00".

Patronage refund (titled for example Patronage: income earned): what the patronage is measured by and the period, then the documents behind the amount, grouped by income account (for example 4400 - Interest Income on Loans, with its total): the date, the document no. (click it to open the document) with its kind, and the amount. For purchases or deliveries, the sales or purchase documents of the member that count, returns and debit memos negative. The line under them gives the total, the member's basis. With more than 40 documents in a group, the rest is summed on one line.

Then, for every batch, how the member's amount comes from the basis:

  • Computed: with a rate, the basis × the rate, for example "8,600.00 × 5.0000 % = 430.00"; with an amount shared in proportion, the amount to distribute × the member's basis ÷ the basis of the members on the list, for example "10,000.00 × 8,017.88 ÷ 96,971.52 = 826.83";
  • Rounding, for the member with the largest basis when the shares did not add up to the amount to the centavo: the difference given to that member;
  • Adjustment with its reason, when the amount was adjusted;
  • Net: what the member receives. For a member removed from the list: "Removed from the list" with the reason, and no amount.

If an entry dated in the period was recorded or voided after the batch was computed (a deposit entered late, for example), the figures worked out now no longer give the basis on the batch. The window then says so in yellow: "Worked out again now, the basis is 8,167.72; the batch has 8,017.88. An entry dated in the period was recorded or voided after the batch was computed. Recompute takes it." Click Recompute on a batch not yet approved to take it in; an approved or posted batch keeps its figures.

Reviewing the list: removing a member

To remove one member (a batch not yet approved, users with the Edit right):

  1. Click Remove beside the member.
  2. Choose the reason: Delinquent: past-due loan, Has another obligation to the cooperative, Not a member in good standing or Other reason, and type the details if you like (they are needed for Other reason).
  3. Click Remove. "1 member(s) removed from the list." With an amount shared in proportion, "The amount is shared again among the members left."; with a rate, "The others keep their amounts."

To remove several members together: tick them in the first column (the box in the heading ticks every member shown, so Only those with a past-due loan or on hold then ticks all of those). A bar appears above the list with the number ticked: choose the reason, type the details if needed and click Remove from the list.

To put a member back: click Put back on the member's line. "The member is back on the list."

To adjust a member's amount: click Adjust, type the amount to add or take off (for example -250.00) and the reason, and click Save. "The adjustment is saved; the list is back in review."

Every removal, put-back and adjustment is written to the audit trail, and puts a reviewed batch back in review. The printed register lists the members removed under Not included, with the reasons.

Step by step

To compute a batch:

  1. Open Year-end Distribution > Interest on Share Capital (or click ISC batch on the year's net surplus allocation).
  2. Check the Fiscal year allocation, the period and the Basis.
  3. Choose the Method and check the Amount to distribute, or type the Rate %.
  4. Choose Credit to and, where asked, the Savings product.
  5. Set the filters under Members covered.
  6. Click Compute. Nothing is posted, so nothing is asked: the batch opens for review with "Computed: 12 member(s), 10,000.00. Nothing is posted yet: review the list below."

To review it: go through the list, remove the members who should not receive it and adjust any amount (see above).

To recompute: click Recompute, change the fields and click Recompute, then confirm "Recompute the batch? Members removed by hand stay removed." with Recompute. Adjustments are not kept; enter them again.

To finish the review: when the list is right, click Finish the review (in the note at the top or in 1. Finish the review). It checks that every member left on the list has the account to credit and that the year's amount set aside is not exceeded. The message "The review is finished. An approver records the resolution next, then the batch is posted." appears, or "The batch cannot be validated yet: ..." with the problems. A user who may not approve sees in 2. Approve who approves next.

To approve (a user with the Approve right who did not prepare the batch): in 2. Approve, type the Board / General Assembly resolution, click Approve and confirm "Approve and lock the batch of 10,000.00 for 10 member(s)?" with Approve. "Approved and locked." From then on the list cannot change.

To post (users with the Create right):

  1. In 3. Post, type the Posting date.
  2. Check Charged to: "Normally the payable set up by the net surplus allocation" (Interest on Share Capital Payable, or Patronage Refund Payable).
  3. Click Post to members and confirm "Post 10,000.00 to 10 member(s)? This is done once." with Post. Only the members on the list are credited; the members removed get nothing.
  4. The message "Posted (voucher CJV-000004). Each member's account was credited." appears (with Keep as payable: "Each member's amount is payable to the member, paid out from the report Unclaimed Distributions (click the member).").

To reverse a posted batch (users with the Approve right): in Reverse, type the Date and the Reason, click Reverse the batch and confirm "Reverse the posted batch? Each member credit comes back out." The message "Reversed by voucher ..." appears and the batch shows Reversed.

Deleting a computation

A batch that was never posted (in review, reviewed or approved) can be deleted, for example a computation made on the wrong period or with the wrong amount, or one computed twice. Nothing of it was posted, so no member account, voucher or payable changes.

  1. Click Delete in the title bar of the batch (or Delete beside it on Distribution Batches).
  2. The window Delete the computation shows the batch no., its kind, members, amount and status, for example "Interest on share capital, 10 member(s), 10,000.00, in review, not posted".
  3. Type the Reason (required, recorded in the audit trail).
  4. Click Delete the batch (or Keep it to leave it). The batch and its list of members are removed and Distribution Batches opens with "Batch DIST-00001 deleted with its list of 12 member(s). Nothing had been posted; the batch as it was is kept in the audit trail."

It cannot be undone: compute a new batch to start over. The audit trail keeps the batch as it was, with each member's basis, computed amount, adjustment and net, those removed and why, and the reason of the deletion. The batch number is not used again.

A posted batch is not deleted: it is reversed (see To reverse a posted batch above), and a reversed batch stays on file with its vouchers. Deleting needs the Delete right on the page (Interest on Share Capital or Patronage Refund); an approved batch also needs the Approve right.

Example

The sample batch DIST-00001 shares ₱10,000.00 of interest on share capital by the average daily balances of 2026. Ramos, Danilo and Torres, Edgar have a past-due loan and were removed with the reason "Delinquent: past-due loan", so the 10 members left share the whole ₱10,000.00: Aquino, Rosa, with an average daily balance of ₱11,000.00, receives ₱1,134.35. The rounding difference of (0.01) was applied to the member with the largest balance, so exactly ₱10,000.00 is distributed. Nothing is posted until the batch is reviewed, approved and posted.

The register and the CSV file

Print register prints the INTEREST ON SHARE CAPITAL REGISTER (or PATRONAGE REFUND REGISTER) with the batch number, the status, the period, the method and basis, how the amounts are credited and the resolution; the members included with Member, Account, Basis, Computed, Adjustment and Net, and the totals; Not included with each reason; and the signature lines Prepared by, Checked by and Approved by. A reprint asks for the reason and is marked as a copy.

CSV downloads the lines (Member no., Member, Account, the basis, Computed, Adjustment, Net, Excluded, Reason) as a file for a spreadsheet. Both need the Print/Export right.

What it records

Posting makes one Cooperative Journal Voucher with the batch number as reference:

Account Debit Credit
The Charged to account (Interest on Share Capital Payable or Patronage Refund Payable) Total to distribute
Each member: the ledger account of the savings or share capital product credited Member's net
Each member, when kept as payable: Interest on Share Capital Payable or Patronage Refund Payable, under the member's name Member's net

Each member's account gets an entry with code ISC (Interest on Share Capital) or PR (Patronage Refund), for example "Interest on share capital 2025". With Share capital until the subscription is fully paid, the rest to savings, the share capital account takes what is still unpaid on the member's subscription and the savings account the rest. An amount kept as payable is paid on Member Disbursement and listed until then on Unclaimed Distributions.

A reversal is a Cooperative Journal Voucher "Reversal of ..." that turns every line around and takes each credit back out of the member's account with code DM.

Good to know

  • The batches of a fiscal year cannot use more than its allocation set aside: "The batches of fiscal year 2025 (...) would exceed the ISC set aside by its allocation (...)."
  • "Santos, Maria (100001) has no savings account to credit." Open the account first, or choose another Credit to.
  • "There is nothing to distribute."
  • "Enter the dividend (ISC) rate in percent.", "Enter the amount to distribute.", "Choose the savings product the amounts are credited to." and "No share capital product is set up."
  • "Enter the reason the member is removed from the list." (an Other reason without details), "Tick the members to remove from the list first." and "Enter the reason for the adjustment."
  • "The batch is locked: it is approved." Once approved, the list no longer changes; a posted batch is corrected by reversing it.
  • "The batch is approved by someone other than the user who prepared it." An administrator may approve their own.
  • "Validate the batch first." and "Only an approved batch is posted, and only once."
  • "Some members have already been paid. Reverse those payments first." A credit that the member has already withdrawn cannot come back out until the account holds it again.
  • A posted batch is never voided: "It posted a year-end distribution batch. A distribution is corrected with an adjusting entry, not voided."
  • Users who may approve see "Distribution batches waiting for approval" in the alerts.
  • Deleting: "Enter the reason for deleting the batch."; "Batch DIST-00001 is posted: it is not deleted. Reverse it instead."; "Batch DIST-00001 was posted and reversed: it stays on file with its vouchers."; "Batch DIST-00001 is approved: deleting it needs the Approve right as well."
  • Rights: Create computes a new batch and posts; Edit recomputes, removes and puts back members, adjusts amounts and finishes the review; Approve approves and reverses; Delete deletes a batch not yet posted (an approved one with Approve as well); Print/Export prints the register and downloads the CSV. Seeing where a basis comes from needs only access to the page. By default the General Manager and the Branch Manager have Delete; the Bookkeeper and the Accountant do not.

Patronage Refund

Year-end Distribution > Patronage Refund opens the batch page described under Interest on Share Capital and Patronage Refund, for a patronage refund. The computation for review (nothing posted on Compute), the list with what each member owes the cooperative and its totals, where each basis comes from (the documents behind it, see Where the basis comes from), removing members with the reason, the approval, posting, reversal, deleting a batch not yet posted, register and CSV are the same.

Where to find it: Year-end Distribution > Patronage Refund (a new batch, titled "New patronage refund batch"), PR batch on Net Surplus Allocation, or New PR batch on Distribution Batches.

What differs from an ISC batch:

  • Patronage measured by chooses what each member's share is based on:
    • Income earned from the member (the income accounts chosen): the credits less the debits, in the period, on the Income accounts (patronage) you choose, on the lines that carry the member's name or are made out to one of the member's customer records. "The member's share is the income these accounts earned from them in the period, in lines of business whose member transactions count, on the goods and services that count." The loan interest income account is chosen at first; choose all the income accounts your bylaws count as patronage. If none is chosen the program says "Choose the income accounts (income codes) the patronage refund is based on."
    • Member's purchases of goods and services that count: the invoices, sales receipts, POS sales and statement charges made out to any customer record of the member, less credit memos and POS returns.
    • Member's deliveries (produce sold to the cooperative) that count: the bills, item receipts and checks for items from any supplier record of the member, less supplier debit memos.
    For purchases and deliveries only item lines count, and only items marked Counts for patronage refund. For every basis, documents of a line of business whose member transactions do not count are left out. The member is recognized through the Party Master, so a purchase on the member's customer record counts even though the member's ledger name is not on it. See Lines of Business. Members with nothing on the chosen basis are not listed.
  • The period boxes read Patronage from and to. There is no Basis and no Share capital product.
  • The amount comes from the PR set aside by the allocation, Rate % is a refund rate on the chosen basis ("Enter the refund rate in percent."), Charged to starts at Patronage Refund Payable, and the member ledgers get code PR.

Distribution Batches

The list of the interest on share capital and patronage refund batches, latest first, with their status.

Where to find it: Year-end Distribution > Distribution Batches. Batches on a batch page and Back on a new batch also return here.

Filters: Kind (Both, Interest on share capital, Patronage refund) and Status (Any, In review (not posted), Reviewed (not posted), Approved, Posted, Reversed). The list changes as soon as you choose.

The columns: Batch (click it to open the batch; the description under it), Kind, Year (the fiscal year of its allocation), Period, Members, Amount, Credit, Prepared by and Status (a computed batch shows as In review and a finished review as Reviewed, both with "not posted"; a reversed one as Voided). Up to 300 batches are listed. On a phone each batch is a card.

Delete beside a batch not yet posted (in review, reviewed or approved) deletes the computation: the window Delete the computation names the batch, asks the Reason and has Delete the batch; "Batch DIST-00002 deleted. Nothing had been posted; the batch as it was is kept in the audit trail." It shows to users with the Delete right on Interest on Share Capital or Patronage Refund (for an approved batch, with Approve as well). A posted batch has no Delete: it is reversed on its page. See Deleting a computation.

When amounts kept as payable are not yet paid out, a note above the list gives their number and total: "... are payable and not yet paid out. They are paid from the report Unclaimed Distributions (click the member)." The title bar has Net surplus allocation, New ISC batch and New PR batch.

Year-end distribution reports

The Year-end Distribution reports are listed under Year-end Distribution > Reports in the sidebar and in the Year-end Distribution card of the Report Center. The common report tools are explained in Working with reports.

Distribution Register

Every member line of one distribution batch, included or left out, with when it was credited or paid.

Where to find it: Year-end Distribution > Reports > Distribution Register.

Filters: Batch (number, kind and status; the latest batch at first) and Search (part of the member's name, or the member no.).

The columns: Member (click it to open the member), Member no., Account, Basis, Computed, Adjustment, Net, Note ("Removed:" and the reason, or the reason for an adjustment) and Credited / paid, with totals. The members removed from the list are listed last, without a computed or net amount. The note under the title gives the batch, its period and status (for example "in review, not posted"), how the amounts are credited and the resolution.

Unclaimed Distributions

The interest on share capital and patronage refund kept as payable and not yet paid out, member by member.

Where to find it: Year-end Distribution > Reports > Unclaimed Distributions.

Filter: As of, the date the days unclaimed are counted to.

The columns: Member, Member no., Batch, Kind, Year, Posted, Days unclaimed and Amount, with the total. Click a member to pay them on Member Disbursement.

Net Surplus Allocation Summary

One line per fiscal year: how the net surplus was divided, and how much of the ISC and PR set aside has been distributed.

Where to find it: Year-end Distribution > Reports > Net Surplus Allocation Summary.

The columns: Fiscal year (click it to open the allocation), Net surplus, Reserve, Education, Community development, Optional, ISC set aside, ISC distributed, PR set aside, PR distributed (posted batches) and Status (with the resolution number). The report has no filter.

Officers, Committees & Trainings

The register of the cooperative's officers: the Board of Directors, the committees and management, with each person's position, term and the trainings attended. "CDA rules require officers to complete the mandatory trainings for their position; the list printed here goes with the annual reports." The number of active officers whose term had not ended by the end of the year is also a figure of the CAPR data.

Where to find it: CDA Compliance > Officers, Committees & Trainings.

The officers are listed by board or committee: the name (with the member no. for a member), the position, the term ("term ended" in red, "term ends soon" in amber within 60 days), the number of trainings or "no training recorded", and Edit. Only active officers are listed; Include past officers shows the others greyed, and Active only hides them again.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Member The officer, when a member. Type part of the name or the member no. and choose.
or name (not a member) The name of an officer who is not a member, for example a hired general manager.
Board / committee Board of Directors, Audit Committee, Election Committee, Credit Committee, Education and Training Committee, Ethics Committee, Mediation and Conciliation Committee, Gender and Development Committee, Management (General Manager, Treasurer, Secretary, Bookkeeper) or Other committees.
Position Required. For example Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson, Member.
Term from, to The term of office.
Trainings attended (one a line: title, date, provider) Each training on its own line.
Active Untick it when the person leaves office. Officers are not deleted.

To add an officer: fill in New officer and click Save. To change one: click Edit, change the fields and click Save, or Cancel.

Print the list prints the LIST OF OFFICERS AND COMMITTEE MEMBERS of the active officers with today's date, by board and committee (Name, Position, Term, Trainings attended), with the signature lines Certified correct (Secretary) and Chairperson. A second print on the same day asks for the reason for the reprint.

Messages: "Enter the officer (a member, or the name) and the position." and "The term ends before it starts."

Compliance Tracking

A checklist of the reports and requirements the cooperative submits for each year, with the due date, the date submitted, the receiving reference and a copy of what was filed. Overdue items stand out here and in the alerts.

Where to find it: CDA Compliance > Compliance Tracking. Compliance tracking on Regulatory Calendar and a requirement on the Compliance Status report also open it.

Type the Report year and click Show; last year is shown at first, and "On file:" links to each year recorded. The tiles count the Items of the year, those Done (submitted, accepted, submitted late or not applicable) and those Overdue. The list has Report / requirement (with the notes and a link to the attached file), Due, Submitted, Reference, Status and Update. The title bar has Regulatory calendar and CAPR data.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Report year The year the requirement is for.
Report / requirement Required. For example Social audit report.
Due The deadline.
Submitted on The date it was filed.
Reference / receiving no. The receiving or reference number given by the office.
Status From the dates: without a submitted date the item is to submit ("To submit", or "Overdue" in red once the due date has passed); with one it is "Submitted", or "Submitted late" when filed after the due date. Accepted by CDA: accepted (it needs the submitted date). Not applicable: the requirement does not apply this year.
Notes A short remark, for example "Submitted online".
Copy of the submission (PDF or picture) A PDF, JPG, PNG, GIF or WEBP file of up to 8 MB. A new file replaces the one on file.

Step by step

To start the checklist of a year:

  1. Type the Report year and click Show.
  2. When nothing is recorded for that year, click Add the standard annual requirements. The program adds Cooperative Annual Progress Report (CAPR), Audited financial statements, Social audit report, Performance audit report, List of officers and trainings attended, Certificate of Compliance, Annual tax incentives / benefits report (BIR) and General Assembly minutes and resolutions, each due 120 days after the end of the fiscal year: "8 item(s) added for 2025. Check each due date against the current CDA issuances."
  3. Correct the due dates with Update, and add any other requirement with New requirement.

To record a submission:

  1. Click Update on the item.
  2. Type Submitted on and the Reference / receiving no.
  3. Choose the file of what was filed in Copy of the submission (PDF or picture).
  4. Click Save. Later, when the CDA accepts it, set Status to Accepted by CDA.

"The standard list and the 120-day due date are a starting point; follow the current CDA memorandum circulars for your cooperative's type and category." Items are not deleted; mark one that does not apply as Not applicable. Overdue items count in the alerts as "Regulatory submissions overdue". Message: "Enter the report or requirement."

Regulatory Calendar

The cooperative's own list of recurring deadlines (CDA, BIR, local government and internal ones such as the General Assembly) with a reminder before each. "Deadlines within their reminder period appear on the dashboard and in the alert count at the top of every page."

Where to find it: CDA Compliance > Regulatory Calendar. Regulatory calendar on Compliance Tracking also opens it.

The list has Deadline (with the notes), For, Next due (the date and "in 171 day(s)", "today" or "passed"), Repeats (with the reminder) and Edit, the nearest first. A deadline within its reminder period is shown in amber, a passed one in red. Inactive deadlines are greyed at the end. The program comes with a few common deadlines to start from.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Title Required. Two deadlines cannot have the same title.
For CDA, BIR, Local government, Internal (General Assembly, Board) or Other.
Due date Required. The first (or next) due date.
Repeats Once, Every month, Every quarter or Every year (the default). A repeating deadline rolls forward by itself: Next due is the first date on or after today counted from the due date.
Remind days before How many days before the next due date the reminder starts. 30 by default, up to 365.
Notes A short remark.
Active Untick it to stop a deadline without deleting it. Untick a one-time deadline once it is done, since it keeps showing as passed.

To add a deadline: fill in New deadline and click Save. To change one: click Edit, change it and click Save. Saving refreshes the alert count.

"The dates set here are your own: confirm them against the current CDA, BIR and local government issuances." Messages: "Enter the title and the due date." and "There is already an item with that title."

CAPR Data Preparation & Export

This page gathers the figures the Cooperative Annual Progress Report (CAPR) asks for, from the general ledger and the member ledgers, for a fiscal year: membership, capital, deposits, loans, results, statutory funds and distributions. Print them or export them, then transcribe them into the current CDA form. The page gives the data for the form; it is not the official CAPR layout. Which figures appear, and how each is computed, is set on Report Field Mappings.

Where to find it: CDA Compliance > CAPR Data Preparation & Export. CAPR data on Compliance Tracking and Compute the report on Report Field Mappings also open it.

To compute the figures: type the Fiscal year (last year at first) and click Compute. The line beside the button gives the period, worked out from the fiscal year end of Cooperative Profile & Policies: "Period 01/01/2025 to 12/31/2025 (fiscal year end 12-31)." When more than one report is mapped, choose it in Report. For a year that is not over, "Fiscal year 2026 is not over: the figures are as of today."

How to read it: the lines are grouped in sections (General, Membership, Capital, Deposits, Loans and so on). Each line gives the item with its field code under it, its source (for example metric:members_total) and the value: amounts with two decimals, counts as whole numbers, text as it is. A line whose source cannot be computed shows the error in red, and a note above the list says "1 line(s) cannot be computed; correct their source in Report Field Mappings."

Print prints the COOPERATIVE ANNUAL PROGRESS REPORT DATA of the year with the period, the sections and values, the note "Figures from the books of the cooperative as of ... Transcribe them into the current CDA form; the line definitions are kept in Report Field Mappings.", and the signature lines Prepared by, Certified correct and Chairperson (the Bookkeeper / Accountant, General Manager and Chairperson of Cooperative Profile & Policies). A reprint of the same year asks for the reason. Export CSV downloads Section, Field code, Item, Value and Source for a spreadsheet, numbers without thousands separators, an error as "ERROR:" and the message. Both need the Print/Export right. Field mappings opens Report Field Mappings.

Tip

Before submitting, check the Subsidiary vs General Ledger Reconciliation and the Trial Balance for the same date. The member figures come from the member ledgers and the totals from the general ledger; they should agree.

Report Field Mappings

The definition of each line of the CAPR data (and of any other report you map): its section, code, label and where its value comes from. "When the CDA revises the CAPR form, change the lines in Report Field Mappings: each line takes a named figure, a list of account codes or a fixed text, so no program change is needed." The program comes with a CAPR mapping to start from.

Where to find it: CDA Compliance > Report Field Mappings. Field mappings on CAPR Data Preparation & Export also opens it.

The list has Order, Section, Field (the label, with the field code under it), Source, Edit and Remove. When more than one report is mapped, a tab opens each one. Compute the report opens the CAPR page for the report shown.

Fields

Field What to enter / what it means
Report The report the line belongs to: capr by default. Lower-case letters, digits and the underscore. A new name starts another report, which then gets its own tab and appears in Report on the CAPR page.
Section The heading the line is grouped under, for example Loans.
Field code Required. The code of the line in the form; letters, digits, underscore, point and hyphen. It must be unique in the report.
Order The position of the line. Lines are listed by it.
Label Required. The item as it prints.
Source Required. Where the value comes from (see below).

The Sources card lists what a source can be:

  • account: account codes separated by commas; a code ending in * takes every code that starts with it, for example account:4*. Balance-sheet accounts give the balance at the end of the year, income and expense accounts the year's activity, in their normal direction.
  • text: a fixed text.
  • setting: a cooperative setting, such as setting:cda_reg_no.
  • metric: one of the figures listed with their meaning, for example metric:members_total ("Members (active and suspended) at the end of the period"), metric:loans_outstanding, metric:par_ratio or metric:net_surplus.

To add a line: fill in New line and click Save. To change one: click Edit, change it and click Save. To remove one: click Remove and confirm "Remove this line from the report?" (the Delete/Void right).

When you save, the program computes the source for the last year to check it works. Messages: "Enter the field code, the label and the source.", "That field code is already used in this report." and "The source does not work: Unknown source "...": use metric:, account:, text: or setting:."

CDA compliance reports

The CDA Compliance reports are listed under CDA Compliance > Reports in the sidebar and in the CDA Compliance card of the Report Center. The common report tools are explained in Working with reports.

List of Officers and Committees

The officers of Officers, Committees & Trainings as a report, grouped by board and committee, with their trainings written out.

Where to find it: CDA Compliance > Reports > List of Officers and Committees.

Filter: Show: Active officers or Including past officers.

The columns: Name, Member no., Position, Term from, to, Trainings attended (separated by semicolons, or "none recorded") and a last column with "past" for an officer no longer active.

Compliance Status

The requirements of Compliance Tracking with their status, for one year or every year.

Where to find it: CDA Compliance > Reports > Compliance Status.

Filter: Year (Every year by default).

The columns: Year, Report / requirement (click it to update the item), Due, Submitted, Reference, Status (OVERDUE, To Submit, Submitted, Late, Accepted or Not Applicable) and Notes.
